195. And if he does not, during the time of this life, turn his will into the Cross of Christ (as is seen in this Figure), then he is reserved in the Mystery for the Judgment of God. There he shall be tested in the fire to see whether the spirit of his will has any divine <margin>power or virtue</margin> power in it or not, or whether he can survive in the fire. There his proud earthly works will be burned up; and if the soul remains in the dark magic fire of the Will—for the soul itself is a magic fire when the Divine Light-fire is not in it—then one magic fire receives the other, and then there is no remedy to help it out from there.

For the Image here is <margin>or beyond</margin> outside of Nature, in the Liberty <note>The "Liberty" refers to the stillness of God beyond the turmoil of natural forces.</note>, but the humanity is in Nature, as it is described here.

### The Soul’s Eternal Habitation: Number 35.

201. After the ninth number stands the Soul’s Eternal Habitation, noted with No. 35. This signifies that these escaped souls are indeed in God, in the angelic world, but they are without their works. They cannot attain the brilliance of the Divine Majesty as highly as those who, <margin>e Or here.</margin> <margin>e</margin> in this life, have clothed themselves with the power of God. The word "Habitation" enters into the Liberty <note>term: Liberty; the state of God's existence beyond the constraints of nature or manifestation</note> without nature, just as the word "Image" does above it. For the soul must stand in nature, but the habitation of the Image is outside of nature in the Divine Liberty.

Here, the Devil’s fall is to be considered.

204. He has driven his proud will upward from the line of the Cross, seeking to dominate the mystery of the Divine Wisdom through <margin>k</margin> cunning, subtlety, and wrath in the power of fire. He sought to <margin>k Or reason, or in wit, craft, and fury.</margin> <margin>l</margin> kindle <note>original: "kindle," meaning to ignite or inflame into a state of wrath</note> the mystery of the Trinity so that he might be Lord. Even to this very day, he desires to fly out above the highest thrones of angels.

### ->Spiritual Body’s Habitation: number 59.<-

232. Adam swallowed this morsel <note>A reference to the forbidden fruit and the fall of man.</note> and thereby lost his Angelic form. The four elements should have remained hidden within him, and he should have lived only in the "One Element" in Divine Power, knowing nothing of evil. As shown at the right hand in the Liberty at number 59, there stands the "Spiritual Body’s Habitation." The body of the Image (that is, the body of the soul) should have dwelt there; but it was hindered, and it had to go under the Earth and be shut up within the Earth.

### ->Earth; number 60.<-

233. Under the words "Four Elements" stands "Earth," number 60. This signifies that the Earth has wholly slipped out or fallen away from the inward world. The word "Earth" touches neither the left nor the right circle; it is, as it were, dead. However, the Cross goes through it, signifying its restoration: that the human "Earth" (the physical body) is regenerated on the Cross. It signifies that the Heavenly Divine Essence shall be severed from the essence of the dark world by the Divine Fire. Then, there shall be a new Earth in a heavenly source, form, essence, and property. That which is hidden in the Earth shall spring up again in the heavenly part. Here, the Resurrection of Man is to be considered. Furthermore, it should be noted that the Earth is placed thus in the Abyss; because it reaches no eternal Principle, it must eventually vanish.

### Wonder; number 62.

235. Under the line of the Cross stands number 62, **Wonder**. This signifies that the evil wonders, as well as the evil part of the Earth, shall <margin>Or in.</margin> at the Judgment of God (when God shall make separation) fall <margin>As to its own place.</margin> back home to the abyss of darkness. This shall be the "Earth" for all devils and <margin>Or evil.</margin> wicked people to dwell upon together; for the abyss stands beneath it, marked as number 1.

### Babel; number 63.

236. Next to the word "Wonder" stands number 63, **Babel** <note>original: "Babell"; referring to the biblical Tower of Babel, symbolizing spiritual confusion and false religious systems.</note>, signifying that Babel is only a wonder of the abyss, and she performs only wonders within that abyss.

### Self-Reason in Babel; number 64.

<margin>Or self-reason.</margin>
237. A little above, under the circle on the right hand after "Earthly Man," number 64 stands as **Self-Reason in Babel**. This reason circles around the second principle <note>The "second principle" refers to the divine world of light and love.</note> and proceeds by its own power beneath the divine world. It imagines itself to be in God and believes that it serves God, yet it remains without God within itself. It teaches and performs only its own business; it rules the outward world according to its own reason without the Spirit and Will of God, following only its own self-will. Therefore, it goes about the world of Light with flattery and gives God <margin>Or good.</margin> fair words, but it remains outside of God in the abyss and eventually enters into it.

### Wonder of the Great Folly; number 65.

238. Under "Self-Reason," number 65 stands as **Wonder of the Great Folly**, signifying Babel. Babel has discovered all <margin>Or inventions.</margin> arts, <margin>Cunning deceit, fallacies.</margin> subtleties, and <margin>Feats or tricks.</margin> devices, yet has lost itself. It seeks gold and loses God; it mistakes Earth for gold and death for life. This is the greatest folly that can be found in the <margin>Or Being of all Beings.</margin> *Essence of all Essences* <note>original: "Essence of all Essences" (Latin: *Ens Entium*), referring to the ultimate reality or God.</note>, as has been sufficiently demonstrated in other places.

### The Conclusion.

239. Thus we see where our home is: not in this world, but in the two inward worlds. Whichever of these we <margin>Or converse.</margin> trade in during this life, into that same world we enter when we die. We must leave the outward world behind; we must be born again only upon the Cross.

244. Therefore, the Devil wanted to be above God, and therefore God became man so that He might bring the soul out of the Wrath, through death, and into another life—into another world. It goes out from itself with its own will through death; it considers itself dead and sinks itself down as if dead. In doing so, it falls with its will through the principle of fire and into the eye of divine light. There, it becomes the chariot of the Holy Spirit, upon which He rides.

246. But when the soul tries to go by its own power, it remains in its own "fire-nest"—the origin where it was first awakened, just as Lucifer did. <margin>The soul.</margin>

The body has two arms, signifying two principles, and the torso in the middle makes a whole person. The heart is the first principle, and the brain is the second. The heart holds the soul <margin>That is, the soul is seated in it.</margin>, and the brain holds the spirit of the soul. It is a "new child," and yet not a new one either; the trunk of the tree is from eternity, but the branches grow out of that trunk.

248. And though it has not existed as a soul from eternity, yet it has been known from eternity in the Virgin of Divine Wisdom upon the Cross. In its root, the soul belongs to God the Father; in its essence, to God the Son; and in its will, to God the Holy Spirit.

251. But you must know that God is both the Beginning and the End; therefore, we put the Cross at the End according to Reason. For there we go through Death into Life; it is our Resurrection.

Whoever does not enter through death upon the Cross into Regeneration (that is, into the Divine Body) does not understand this. Therefore, intellectual searching is not the best way to find the Mystery; rather, to be born **s** in God is the right way to find it. The inward world is Paradise itself, for the Spirit grows up within it out of Eternity.

280. You may very well see that this is true by observing the following. Observe the summer and winter, heat and cold, and your eyes will soon be opened—provided you are born not <margin>or without the visible world</margin> outwardly, but within yourself, with a true magic <note>original: "Magick"; here meaning a creative or spiritual power of the soul</note> will to find God; for it is very plain.

281. For the water in the deep arises from the fire—not from the "Wrath" <note>The harsh, consuming aspect of fire</note> but from the Light. For the Light proceeds from the fire and has a <margin>longing, sucking, or drawing</margin> seeking of its own. It seeks a glass <note>original: "Glasse"; a mirror or reflective medium</note> to behold itself in, and it seeks a habitation. It draws that habitation <margin>or into</margin> into itself by its desire and dwells there. That which is drawn in is water, which receives the Light. Otherwise, if the Light did not dwell in the water, the "deep" of the world could not comprehend the Light. The water is the satisfying of the Light's desire.

282. And the water, in turn, seeks the glass and desires a house to dwell in, and that is flesh. As you see, the water receives the shadow of all bodily substances so that the body may be seen in the water; this is because the seeking of the water has captured it.

283. Furthermore, you see here the end of Nature. For the eye finds its life in the water, and so goes back into the seventh form <note>In the author's cosmology, the seventh form is the "Sabbath" or the completed manifestation of a thing</note> and sees its body in the water. The outward desires no more; this body desires no other body more in the outward world, but it looks back toward its mother. A mirror is a true example of this, being made of water and fire <note>referring to the process of making glass/mirrors with heat and minerals</note>, and it receives the image very clearly.

284. And thus you see that the end goes back again and seeks the beginning, and goes no further <margin>or outwardly</margin> in the outward world. For this world has a limit and is included in Time; it hastens toward that limit, and there the end finds the beginning. This world is like a model or a glass in the Beginning. By this, you may find something of the Mystery and behave yourselves well in the beginning, so that you may be found to be a wonder in the Love of God.

285. And know that the second <margin>or form</margin> kind of water is in the Spirit. It is the glass of its Father—of its Maker—who dwells in the Spirit. It is found only by its Maker; it does not find itself. For as long as a thing goes <margin>outward</margin> forward, there is no finding in the inward; but the Spirit which dwells in the inward finds itself in the outward.

Thus we know the foundation of this world: it is a figure <note>a symbolic representation or reflection</note> of the Inward world according to both "Mothers"—that is, according to both Fires: the fire of Wrath and the fire of Light. The Sun is a model or mirror of the Light of Eternity, and the outward fire is a mirror of the Wrath. The substance of them both is Water and Earth. The Earth is the substance of Wrath, the Water is the substance of the Light, and the Air is the substance of the Eternal Spirit, who is called God, the Holy Ghost.

<margin>

Resembles the Father.
The Son.

289. Yet you must know that this world is not the actual Essence of Eternity, but a figure or a mirror of it. Therefore, it is said to be a distinct "Principle" because it has its own life, yet it exists only through the "Magic seeking" <note>the spiritual attraction or longing</note> of the Inward.

<margin>

Or Maker, Ruler, or Orderer like a craftsman.

290. The Word *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let it be done"</note> is the Master of the outward world, for it keeps the outward world within its conceived mirror. The outward is not the mirror itself, but a likeness in which His Spirit expresses itself in works of wonder, so that it might see the wonders of both fires: the Wrath and the Light. Thus, it continually brings the end of all essences back into the beginning. Therefore, this world turns in a circle, for the end continually seeks the beginning. When it finds the wonders, the end gives those wonders back to the beginning; this is the very reason for the Creation of this world.

291. The life of every creature was a wonder before the beginning, for the Abyss <note>original: "Abysse," the bottomless void or "Ungrund"</note> knew nothing of it. The beginning of the Eye finds everything and sets the model within itself, so that it has an eternal number and delights itself in the number of the wonders.

### The Eighth Form of Fire

<margin>
Abyssal, bottomless.
Or contrived, conceived, or formed.
</margin>
292. Seeing then that an essence consists of two forms—one of which takes an unsearchable, bottomless beginning into itself and keeps it eternally, and the other of which is the model of the Eternal, framed and included in a limited body—we must therefore consider the *Turba* <note>Latin: "turmoil" or "disturbance"; in this context, the destructive element that breaks down forms</note>, which destroys the limited, framed life.

life again, and sets the model of the framed wonders back in the beginning, and presents such a thing to the beginning as did not exist from eternity, but only within the framework of time.

293. My beloved friend, such things as these are shown to you, and to people like you who seek the beginning. For your mind is our mystery; you should seek it in us, not in me. I (the outward man) do not possess it; but the inward man in the Virgin <note>The "Virgin" refers to the Divine Sophia or Wisdom, a central concept in Boehme's mysticism representing the pure, heavenly mirror of God.</note> (where <margin>The "I" calls itself twofold.</margin> God dwells) possesses it, which speaks of itself in the plural number.

294. My outward man is not worthy of the mystery; but God has so prepared it that he might reveal himself to you by that means, so that you should know him by some other means, and not say my own wit has done it.

295. Because you are a very learned person, you shall know that God also loves the simple, and those who are despised by the world, if they seek God as I have done. You shall also know that the true discovery <note>original: "Invention"</note> consists not in human skill <note>original: "Art"</note>, but in the Spirit and Will of God.

<margin>A secret <note>original: "Arcanum"</note> or a hidden treasure.</margin> 296. For this hand is simple and accounted foolish in the eye of the world, as you know; and yet there lies such a secret within it as is incomprehensible to human reason.

298. You shall not find this plant among the high-ranking people of this world; for you cannot reach them, as you are a mystery to them. The Spirit itself seeks the beginning: look to it, and do not play the hypocrite (for the beginning is of Paradise <note>original: "Paradificall"</note>), so that the impure does not enter into the pure, and at last the Serpent beguiles Eve again.

<margin>Soothing or flattery.</margin> 299. Let there be no hypocrisy <note>original: "dissimulation"</note> in you, but plain dealing—yes and no. Do not fear, for that which is eternal will continue, and the distemper is nothing else but the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "turmoil" or "disturbance" that destroys and brings about the wrath of God.</note>, which as a destroyer always creeps in. Beware of that (for the Old Serpent is subtle) and take care that you may be pure both in the beginning and in the end.

300. For this work endures no dissembling; it has a clear foundation. Also, it does not belong to the *Turba*, but to the beginning <margin>Or Clarity.</margin> of the glory. Therefore, beware of those who are born with a wolf-like disposition, whose spirit is a subtle serpent; we speak freely to you.

<header>The first Question Answered.</header>

<margin>g Or former state.</margin>
If there is a limit in a thing, then the beginning proceeds toward that limit and leaves the g first state, seeking further until it finds the Abyss <note>original: *Abysse*; the infinite void or the bottomless depth of God's nature</note>. Then it must remain within itself, for it can go no further; there is nothing beyond it.

302. But if the beginning leaves the first state, then it is under the power of the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "disturber" or the spirit of wrath and destruction that breaks things apart</note>, which destroys it and makes it as it was in the beginning.

303. Then, when the thing is destroyed, the *Turba* is naked without a body, yet it seeks itself and finds itself, but without <margin>h Or substance.</margin> h Essence. Then it enters into itself and seeks itself until it comes into the Abyss, and there the first Eye is found, from which it originally proceeded.

304. But seeing as it is naked and without Essence, it therefore belongs to the Fire, for it places itself into it. In the fire, there is a desire to seek its own body again, and so the i Fire of the beginning is awakened. <margin>i Or Original fire.</margin>

305. And in this, we understand the Last Judgment in the Fire and the Resurrection of the flesh. For the *Turba* desires the body which it had before—even though it was destroyed at its limit—and the desire of the soul was the life of that body.

306. But seeing as there are two fires, the *Turba* is known in a twofold k manner: in an incorruptible body and in a corruptible <margin>k Or Form.</margin> body. That is, one exists in the fire of Wrath, and the other in the fire of Light, l in which we understand the Divine Body to be. In the <margin>l Namely, in the light-fire.</margin> wrathful fire exists the earthly body, which the *Turba* destroys because the *Turba* finds its limit.

307. Now, the Eternal fire in the Eye of God is m understood to be both the fire <margin>m understood to be.</margin> of Wrath and also the light-fire of Love. You must understand that the spirit without a body must remain in the wrathful fire, for it has lost its n Essentiality; the *Turba* in the fire has swallowed <margin>n Or substantiality, or Body.</margin> it up.

308. But the Spirit which has a body that the *Turba* could not devour remains forever in Essentiality—in the Divine Body where its Spirit is. This is the body in the love of God, which is the "hidden man" within the old Adamic man <note>The "Adamic man" refers to the fallen, earthly human nature inherited from Adam</note>, which possesses Christ’s flesh within the corruptible body.

309. And thus we understand the soul to be a life awakened out of the Eye of God. Its origin is in the fire, and the fire is its life. But if it does not go out of the fire—by its will and imagination—into the Light (that is, through the wrathful Death into the second Principle, into the fire of Love), then it remains in its own original fire. It has nothing for a body but the *Turba*—namely, the * harsh wrath in the desire of the fire, a consuming <margin>* Astringent or contracting.</margin> hunger, and yet an eternal seeking, which is an eternal anguish.

<summary>The author explains that the soul originates in the "Eye of God" and must choose between the fire of wrath and the fire of light; if it fails to move toward divine love, it remains trapped in eternal hunger and anguish.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the soul's fate after death and the nature of the "Turba" or divine wrath, following the previous explanation of the soul's origin.</meta>

310. But the soul which, with its desiring will, enters into itself and sinks down into its reason (that is, into its desire) and seeks not itself, but the love of God, finds its own fire is as if it were dead. For its will, which the fire once awakened, is now dead to the "fire-life" <note>The "fire-life" refers to the soul's natural, self-centered, and potentially wrathful energy.</note> and has gone out of itself into the fire of love. That soul is fully within the fire of love; it also possesses the body of the fire of love, for it has entered into it and is a great wonder within the Divine Body. It is no longer centered in itself, for it has mortified its own will. Therefore, the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; refers to the "disturber" or the power of divine wrath and destruction that breaks things apart.</note> is also as if it were dead, and the will of love completely satisfies the original fire, and there the soul lives eternally.

311. But the souls which have awakened the *Turba* have lost the Image <note>The "Image" refers to the divine likeness of God in man.</note>, for the *Turba* has devoured it. Therefore, such souls receive bestial images in the wrath and in hell, according to how the *Turba* exists within them—just as *Lucifer* received the image of a serpent. As the will was shaped here in this life, it remains <gloss>either naked as it is, or in its true likeness</gloss>.
<margin>Or in its true likeness.</margin>

312. For the wrathful *Turba* always seeks the Image but cannot find it, and therefore it shapes the image according to the will. Because earthly desires stick to the will, that image remains among the wonders of God, within the eye of the wrathful principle.

313. And here we understand that the eighth form is the *Turba*, which seeks the Image. If it finds the limit of the image, it destroys it and enters into that limit, seeking further within itself, and finally finds the abominations of what the soul has committed in this life.

314. We also understand here the fire (which at last shall purge the <margin>threshing floor. Matthew 3:12.</margin> floor) and the severe judgment. We understand that every fire shall receive its essence from the *Turba*, and we also understand what that *Turba* is.

315. Then the fire will devour the earth and draw the elements, along with the wonders within them, back into the Beginning. There, that which was at the first will be again, and the elements will become one. Everything will represent its own wonders, each thing within that fire into which its will entered.

316. Listen to this, you children of men, for it concerns you: no beast proceeds from the eternal beginning, but only from the model of the eternal; and its spirit does not reach the eternal as the soul of man does.

317. Also, the corruptible body cannot possess the eternal; it belongs to the *Turba*. But the "new man," born of God, shall possess the eternal; for he has departed from the corruptible and has put on God in Christ. He possesses the Divine Body within the old body.

318. The *Turba* takes away the earthly source, the outward body...

<summary>Boehme explains that the soul's eternal state depends on whether its will seeks God's love or its own desire; the former finds eternal life in the Divine Body, while the latter is consumed by the "Turba" (wrath) and takes on a bestial form.</summary>

The body from the Earth remains in the Earth, but the will takes its works along with itself; for they are in the new body and follow it. Therefore, let every person consider what they do while they are here in this life.

### The Ninth Form of Fire: The Great *Earnestness.

<margin>* Severity.</margin>

309. We understand that all things proceeded from the Beginning, and that one thing always proceeds out of another. We also understand that Fire is a cause of Life, and that life divides itself into two parts yet does not corrupt—only the outward life is what corrupts. It falls into the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; Boehme's term for the "disturber" or the destructive, chaotic power of God's wrath that breaks things apart</note>, which destroys it. We must now consider what the inward Eternal life consists of and what upholds it, so that the body <margin>Does not corrupt or break.</margin> does not fade, seeing that its material existence <note>original: Essentiality</note> has a beginning; and yet we can say with good reason that it has no end. It must have a <margin>Or a sure foundation to uphold it.</margin> ground <note>original: *Grund*; a foundation or underlying reality</note>, or else the *Turba* will seize it and find its limit.

320. The Eternal Body must not have a limit but must be free in the Abyss <note>original: *Abgrund*; the bottomless void or the "un-ground" of God's being</note>, in the Eternal nothingness. Otherwise, another essence would exist within that essence, which would divide it and create a limit.

321. We have told you before that everything which shall endure forever must pass entirely through the Fire, for the *Turba* takes whatever remains in the Fire. Now, no Spirit is created <margin>In.</margin> for the fire so that it should remain in it.

322. Only the *Turba* has captured many of them, but not by the Will of God; for God’s Will is only Love, but the *Turba* is the Will of his Wrath. Through its vehement hunger, this wrath has gained a great dominion, wherein it has manifested its wonders: namely, the Devils and the wicked souls of Men.

323. But Eternal life consists in Meekness and has no Death or *Turba* within it. Therefore, we must say that the soul and Spirit are not in the *Turba*—especially the <margin>Or Image.</margin> body of the soul. If it were, the *Turba* would destroy it.

324. This is only to be understood as mentioned before: that the will in the source of anguish, in the fire (meaning the will of the soul), sinks down into itself as if into Death. It <margin>May not.</margin> cannot live in the fire, and so <margin>The will.</margin> it falls into another world: namely, into the beginning, or (as we may better say) into the free Eternity, into the Eternal Nothingness. In that place, there is no source of pain, nor anything that <margin>Or makes.</margin> gives or receives a source of suffering.

325. Now, there is no dying in the will that has sunk down in this way; for it has gone entirely out from the fiery beginning in the Eye <note>referring to the "Eye of Eternity" or the center of consciousness</note>, and so it brings its life into another Principle and dwells in the Liberty.

<summary>Boehme explains that the eternal soul must pass through the "fire" of existence to reach a state of meekness and liberty in the "Eternal Nothing," escaping the destructive power of the Turba (wrath). He emphasizes that while the physical body decays, the will and its works follow the soul into the next life.</summary>

# The first Question Answered.

The soul indeed searches into God and dives into him even to the Abyss <note>original: "Abysse"; the bottomless depth of God's being</note>, but it does not know its own <margin>f</margin> making; this alone is hidden from it, and nothing else.

<margin>
* That is, as perfectly a Man. Regarding the human race. <note>original: "Quoad genus humanum"</note>
</margin>
343. For a child knows its father and mother well, but it does not know how its father made it. The child is also as <margin>*</margin> highly ranked as its father, yet it is hidden from the child how it existed within the seed. And even if the child discovered that, it still would not know the time and place; for it was in the seed, in the Wonders, and in the Life—a spirit within the Wonders. And here we are commanded to stop diving any further and to be silent.

344. For we are a creature, and we should speak only as far as it belongs to a creature to know—concerning the inward and outward, the body and soul, God, Angels, Men, and Devils; also concerning beasts, birds, worms, plants, and grass, in Heaven and Hell. All of this we are able to explore, but not our own making.

<margin>
t Namely, our own making.
u Or flood.
</margin>
345. And yet we know and find the first *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let it be done"; God's creative command</note> in <margin>t</margin> that, though indeed we do not know what first moved God to create. We know well the making of the soul, but how that <margin>u</margin> essence was in its being from eternity, or how it became movable, we know no ground for that. For it has nothing that could awaken it, and it has an Eternal Will which is without beginning and unchangeable.

<margin>
x Or Fan.
y Or threshing-floor.
z We should not go beyond obedience, patience, humility, and chastity, nor live according to our own self-will and desire.
</margin>
346. But if we should say that the angels and souls have existed from eternity in the Spirit, the reproduction of the soul will not allow that, as we see by experience. Therefore, this is solely God's mystery, and the creature should remain in humility and obedience under God and not soar higher; for the creature is not God.

347. God is a Spirit from eternity without ground or beginning; but the spirit of the soul and of angels has a beginning, and they are in God's hand. The Trinity <note>original: "Ternarie"</note> holds the <margin>x</margin> winnowing shovel, which will purge the <margin>y</margin> threshing floor.

348. We must only have patience and humility in obedience here in this life, or else our proceeding from God avails us nothing. The Devil was indeed an angel, but his pride threw him into darkness. Let no one climb <margin>z</margin> above the Cross; if he does, he will fall into Hell to the Devil.

The body is a likeness, and the spirit is a likeness of God's Spirit, but the <margin>b</margin> true soul is a child born out of God.

All that God has and can do, and all that God is in his <margin>Or Trinity, or Threeness.</margin> Trinity, all this is in the Essences of the soul, just as the virtue of a tree is in the twig that grows out of it.

The children of Israel were likewise tested in the wilderness while Moses was forty days on the Mount; in that instance, they did not stand firm but instead made a golden calf.

When a twig grows out of a tree, its form is like the tree; indeed, it is not the trunk or the root, but yet its form is like the tree. So also, when a mother brings forth a child, it is an image of her.

This <sup>m</sup> resembles a sprouting through the fire—through the anguish as if through death—and yet it is no death, but a going forth out of itself into another source. It stands thus in the midst between the two bows, like a sprout springing out of the Cross.

4. And the arm of the Cross at the right hand signifies that the spirit of the soul enters into the majesty of the Light, and clothes the soul (that is, the Center) with Divine Essentiality.

5. The arm at the left hand of the Cross signifies that its origin is in the Fire; it contains the first principle, and so belongs to the Father. It stands in the Original Eye, in the strong and eager power, as a lord and ruler over Nature.

6. And the lower part of the Cross represents water: namely, humility or death. This signifies that the soul should not dominate in the fire and inflame itself, but should sink down within itself, and under itself before the majesty of God. It should be, as it were, dead in its own will, so that God may live in it, and the Holy Ghost lead and govern it. In this way, it may not do what the *Turba* in the fire desires, but what the Will in <sup>n</sup> Light desires.

## The Sixth Question.

->*What is the Power and Ability of the Soul?*<-

### I.

We know that whatever <margin>Or subsists from itself.</margin> comes out of the abyss and is the ground of itself can do all things within itself; for it is its own essence <note>original: "Essence"</note>, and it makes itself.

2. But though the soul is a twig from the tree, it has now <margin>Or entered into the condition of a creature.</margin> become a creature and is its <margin>Or its own master, or a thing of itself.</margin> own master. It is an image of the whole and a child of the whole. For when a child is born, the mother and the child are two; they are two persons. But as long as it is in the seed within the mother, the seed belongs to the mother, and the mother governs it.

3. But when the child is born, it then has its own life within itself.

### The second Power of the will of the Soul.

15. Even if the Spirit were an Angel, the very likeness of God, the will can still turn it into <margin>a wicked man</margin> a proud Devil. Conversely, the will can also make a Devil into <margin>a child of God. See the Book of the Three Principles, chapter 15, verse 64.</margin> an Angel. If the soul sinks itself into "Death"—that is, into humility under the Cross—and casts itself into the Spirit of God to submit to His government, it then sinks into Eternity. It moves out of the "source" <note>The state of spiritual agitation or pain</note> and into the "still Nothing," which is nevertheless All. In this way, the soul returns to the beginning where God first created it, and the Word *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let it be done"; the creative command of God</note>, which preserves the Image of God, receives it once again.

### The third Power of the Spirit, or will of the Soul.

16. Thirdly, the Spirit of the Soul also has the power to enter into another person—into their very marrow and bones, which is to say, into their "Sulphur." It can bring the *Turba* <note>Latin: "Turba"; a term for the "disturber" or the awakening of God's wrath and chaos within a person</note> into them if that person is <margin>or wicked</margin> false. This is possible as long as that person is not armed with the Spirit of God but is found "naked" in the spirit of this world, as can be seen in the case of <margin>or the bewitching whores</margin> witches.

### The fourth Power of the will of the Soul.

17. Fourthly, if the soul is a child of God, it has such power that it can lead the *Turba* captive and pour it out upon the house of the wicked. This is what Elijah did with the fire, and Moses did before Pharaoh; for such a soul can throw down mountains and break rocks.

18. You must understand that this is only possible if the target is <margin>or liable to the Turba</margin> capable of receiving the *Turba* by having its own inner wrath awakened. If it is not—if the Spirit of God is within a thing—then this power cannot prevail. The Spirit of God would pour "water" upon the *Turba* of the "fire," rendering it dead, and the attempt would end in mockery.

19. Therefore, Heaven is a middle ground between God and Hell—that is, between Love and Anger. It was created out of the midst of the waters so that the Devil cannot rule with his *Turba*. The "water" turns his purposes into a joke; just as false magic and blinding enchantments are <margin>or nullified</margin> drowned in the water.

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains the four powers of the human will, describing how it can transform a person from an angel to a devil (or vice versa) and exert influence over the physical and spiritual world through the "Turba."</summary>
<keywords>Will, Faith, Soul, Turba, Sulphur, Tincture, Magic, Spiritual Warfare, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

### The fifth Power of the Will, or Spirit of the Soul.

<margin>Joshua 10:12</margin>
20. And fifthly, the Spirit of the Soul has such power that it may and can seek all wonders that are in Nature: namely, all arts, languages, buildings, plantings, destruction, and knowledge. It can command the starry heaven, as Joshua did when he commanded the <margin>The Sun</margin> Sun, and it stood still; and as Moses commanded the sea, and it stood up; he also commanded the darkness, and it came. It can create an earthly life, as Moses made the lice and frogs, as well as serpents and other <margin>Or Wonders.</margin> wonders.

<margin>The will of the Holy Spirit.</margin>
21. It has death in its power, so that it can overpower it, if it rides in the chariot of the Bride <note>A mystical reference to the union of the soul with Christ or Divine Wisdom</note>. It can bridle and overcome the Devil if <margin>Namely, the will or Spirit of the soul.</margin> it is in God; there is nothing that can be named that it cannot subdue.

<margin>Or give, or afford.</margin>
22. Only you must understand that the soul has such power from its origin, and it would still have the ability to send forth such a Spirit out of itself if it had not let the <margin>The Great Turba <note>original: "Turba Magna"; refers to the spirit of wrath, confusion, or the "disturbance" in nature caused by the Fall.</note></margin> Great *Turba* into itself, which now holds it back.

<margin>Note: the soul must now seek this power in Jesus Christ.</margin>
23. Unless the Holy Spirit rides upon its chariot—as it did in Moses and Elijah, and in all the prophets, as well as in Christ and his disciples, and always still in the holy children of God—they all have this power. They can raise the dead and heal the sick, and also drive away all diseases. It is natural; the Spirit only reigns through this power over the *Turba*.

24. But it has this as an objection: the soul knows well whether it has made any pact with the Devil, and whether he has anything to do with it. It will not go forth unprotected unless the Spirit of God drives it, and unless it has Him for a shield, lest the subtlety of the Devil insinuate itself.

<margin>Or Miracles.</margin>
<margin>Or awaken it.</margin>
25. It performs no miracle unless the Spirit of God stirs it up. It attributes the power to God and gives Him the glory. It acts as an humble child and stands still under the Cross, letting the Devil go rushing away over it; but it sprouts forth in humility and meekness, through death into eternal life, and brings forth much fruit in patience.

<margin>Or before.</margin>
26. And so the Devil can do nothing to it; it is as if it were dead to him. He may cause a racket with his *Turba* in the earthly life, using his helpers, the wicked men; but this is seen only as a thing of scorn in the sight of God. For he is a proud spirit and would be above the <margin>Or subdue.</margin> wonders of God, but humility can bind him.

<margin>Or Magician.</margin>
27. In this manner, every person may escape the false magician and also the necromancer <note>original: "Negromancer"; one who practices black magic or communicates with the dead.</note>. For no power can touch him in whom God—

<summary>This page describes the immense latent power of the human soul to command nature and perform miracles when aligned with the Holy Spirit, while warning that such power is hindered by the "Turba" (wrath/chaos) and must be exercised through humility.</summary>

God dwells; and as Christ in his death overcame death and the Devil, so also can we in Christ. For the Word which became man dwells in us, and in the Word we can reign over the Devil and Hell; nothing can hinder us.

28. And thus we give you as an answer to this question: the soul in its origin is greatly powerful and can do much. However, its power exists only in that principle in which it resides, for the Devil cannot reign over God.

29. Its power is not given to it in the way a King grants favor and power to a man; rather, it consists in a natural right. Therefore, we are children of the <margin>Or Almightiness.</margin> omnipotence of God, and we inherit his goods within that omnipotence.

***

# The Seventh Question.
### *Is the soul corporeal <note>original: "corporeall"; meaning having a physical or material body</note>, or not corporeal?*

->I.<-

That thing which comes from no beginning also possesses nothing. But if it is something, then it seeks its beginning within itself; for every spirit dwells in the deepest abyss of its <margin>Or Being.</margin> essence. If it must create its own essence, then it can dwell in nothing that is foreign to it, but only in itself, in its own essence.

2. When God created the soul, the Holy Spirit <margin>Or encompassed.</margin> clothed it with the tincture <note>In Boehme's terminology, the "tincture" is a spiritual essence or life-force that transmits qualities from the divine to the material.</note>. Because one part of the soul consisted in the tincture, it was <margin>Or without a body.</margin> naked of itself—just as glowing fire is naked and becomes "clothed" when it possesses the tincture.

3. But you must understand that growth proceeds from warmth; warmth is what drives forth the tincture. It drives the twig out of its root—that is, out of its own fire, whether that fire be cold or hot.

4. For darkness contains the cold fire until it reaches the point of "anguish" <note>original: "Anguish"; a technical term in Boehme's theosophy referring to the tension or contraction of spiritual forces before they ignite into light or heat.</note>, and then it kindles itself into heat, as you see in a herb if it is moved into another <margin>Or Source.</margin> property <note>original: "Property"; referring to a specific quality or state of being.</note>.

5. And thus we give you to understand that the tincture is the true body of the soul. For the soul is fire, and the tincture arises from the fire. The fire draws the tincture back into itself and calms itself with it, so that the wrathful source is quenched, and then the tincture remains in a state of meekness.

6. For the soul has no essence, nor <margin>Might or fire-ability.</margin> power in itself, but the...

<summary>This page concludes the sixth question by asserting the soul's divine power through Christ and begins the seventh question by defining the soul's "body" as a spiritual "tincture" generated from its own internal fire.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, soul, tincture, omnipotence, spiritual body, fire and light, essence, alchemy of the soul</keywords>

# The Seventh Question Answered

Fire is its power, and thus water proceeds from the meekness of the Tincture <note>Tincture: In Boehme’s alchemy, the spiritual "dye" or essence that transmits life and quality from the divine to the material.</note>.

7. For the fire is full of desire, and where there is a desiring of the Original <note>Original: The source or first principle of being.</note>, there is also a finding of the Original. Thus, the fire finds water in the Tincture and turns it into Sulphur, according <margin>Or the.</margin> to the power of all the seven Spirits of Nature; and this is a water of Life.

8. For the Tincture springs up in the water like a sprout, and the fire in the Abyss <note>Abyss: The bottomless void or the "Unground" from which all existence emerges.</note> causes it. And so the water in the Sulphur of <margin>Secret.</margin> the seven Spirits is turned into the Mystery; for the Great Secret <note>original: Arcanum</note>—what God and Eternity can do—lies within it.

9. And thus the Mystery contains two forms, namely fire and water, and changes itself according to them both: according to fire, it turns into Red; and according to the Tincture, it turns into White. <margin>This white.</margin> This white is a splendor or glance of the Fire, by which the Life may see and know itself, and from which Reason and the senses arise. And the Mind is in the wheel of the anguish in the Fire, out of which the Essences arise.

<margin>Or a habitation.</margin> 10. And so we see what the blood is: a house of the soul, but the Tincture is its body.

11. The true soul has no physical body that may be called "soul"; but the body grows in the Tincture, in the Sulphur out of Sulphur. That is, each spirit of the seven Spirits of Nature desires substantiality <note>original: Essentiality</note>, and that concrete substantiality is Sulphur.

12. For *Sul* is the virtue of the Light, and *Phur* is the virtue of the four forms of the Original of Nature, as is mentioned at length in the third book.

<margin>Mixture or concretion.</margin> 13. And thus the *Phur* desires flesh—that is, a mass of fire and water—and it is conceived and born in the Tincture.

14. And the Tincture is the sprouting of the body, and the fire is an origin of the Spirit through the Tincture. For the Spirit of the soul takes its origin in the Tincture, which then shapes the true Image according to the Image of God—that is, according to all the three Principles. For in the Fire, the soul is not a likeness of God; but in the Spirit, it is the Image of God.

15. For the first soul was incorporated with the Divine Substantiality together in the Tincture, so that it had the Divine body in the Virgin of Wisdom, in which the Tincture stands, which is the Angelic Image.

16. And so we answer you: as for the soul alone, apart from the Spirit, it is a Globe of Fire, with an Eye of Fire and an Eye of Light, which turn themselves backwards into one another like the wheel in *Ezekiel* that could go on every side. Though *Babel* <note>Babel: Boehme's term for the confused, divided, and spiritually blind institutional churches.</note> has contrived another meaning about it, it is a blind one, without Spirit.

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains the alchemical and spiritual composition of the soul, describing it as a "Globe of Fire" that finds its form and "body" through the Tincture and Sulphur. He distinguishes between the soul's fiery nature and its divine image, which is only realized through the Spirit and the "Virgin of Wisdom."</summary>

# The Seventh Question Answered

<margin>f The soul.</margin>
17. But, regarding its <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tincture"; here meaning the spiritual essence or "color" of the soul's life-force</note> proceeding from the Light—which arises from both the fire and the Light—<gloss>f</gloss> it is a Spirit in which the origin of the soul and the Spirit cannot be separated in Eternity. It is an eternal bond; and when the blood <gloss>t</gloss> has run out and the body dies, that bond remains for eternity.

18. Regarding the soul alone: the body does not belong to the essential nature of the soul; they are two distinct essences. For the body is the mirror and dwelling house of the soul, as well as its <gloss>u</gloss> property. The body is also the reason that the <gloss>x</gloss> soul, on its own, alters the Spirit according to the cravings of the body or the spirit of this world.

19. By this, the image within the Spirit is completely changed according to the contents of the Will. The soul has <gloss>y</gloss> brought this Will out of the Center of Fire and out of the Wrath into the Light, all according to the power of the <term>Imagination</term> <note>original: "Imagination"; in this context, it refers to the soul's deep formative desire or "imaging" power that shapes its inner reality</note>.

<margin>z Or in this time of the body.</margin>
20. And we give you to understand that the Spirit, <gloss>z</gloss> while it remains here in the body, can change itself. This happens without its conscious knowledge through the Imagination—namely, by the desire of <gloss>a</gloss> lust <note>original: "Lust-sucht"; a driving, addictive craving or passion</note>. Thus, the act of desiring shapes such a form in the soul's will as the lust (whether for evil or good) dictates.
<margin>a Lust-passion.</margin>

21. And we further say that the soul itself is not physical. However, within its Tincture, a body grows—whether it be a heavenly or a hellish body. Yet this is not a body that can be grasped outwardly; it is a body of power—the Divine body, Christ’s heavenly body, the heavenly flesh which he gives us to eat in his Testament.

<margin>b Or substance.</margin>
22. It is a body that the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Böhme to describe the "turmoil" or "disturber"—the destructive element in nature that breaks things down</note> cannot touch or seize. It is immortal and incorruptible, contained in nothing but the Noble Tincture, which is without <gloss>b</gloss> material substance; and that body is incomprehensible to the outward, physical flesh.

23. But the outward Spirit—if the soul does not prevent it but lets it in—brings its own imagination into the soul and spoils it. Consequently, another strange image comes to exist in the Spirit and the Tincture, according to the nature of the lust. For example, the covetous person becomes a wolf, the envious a dog, the proud a horse, peacock, or other beast; or even toads, adders, serpents, and other worms and creeping things. God’s Spirit does not receive these images as long as they remain in that state.

24. And therefore Christ said, "You must be born again of water and of the Spirit if you would see the Kingdom of God." For this reason, God became Man and brought the Divine Image back into the Tincture of the soul after it was spoiled in <term>Adam</term>. So now we must be born anew in Christ if we wish to see God.

25. And that is also accomplished by the Imagination, or Faith; for Faith is an eating of the Divine Body, just as every body grows by what it eats.

<summary>The author explains that the soul's inner form is shaped by its desires (Imagination), which can transform its spiritual "body" into either a divine image or a beastly one. True salvation requires being born again in Christ to restore the divine image lost by Adam.</summary>

26. And the New Birth does not happen at all in the way that *Babel* <note>original: "Babell"; referring to the confused and false teachings of the established, divided churches</note> teaches. For *Moses’s* veil is gone <note>A reference to 2 Corinthians 3:13-16; the legalistic or obscured understanding of God is removed</note>, and from now on, we—meaning the children of God—shall see with clear, paradisiacal eyes.

## The Eighth Question.

->*In what manner does the soul enter into the body of man?*<-

### I.

<margin>Or the body of the mother.</margin>
<margin>Turn over a new leaf, or change our approach.</margin>
My beloved friend, I understand this question to refer to the soul's propagation <note>The process by which souls are produced in offspring</note>. *Moses* tells you how the soul entered into *Adam*, and we have explained that previously. But if you ask about its propagation—how it enters into a child within the mother's womb—we must adopt a different perspective.

<margin>After, or according to.</margin>
2. You know what is written in our third book, very precisely and at length, with many details concerning its propagation: how *Adam* was created as a single Image. He was both man and woman before *Eve* existed; he possessed both the "tincture" <note>term: Tincture; in Boehme’s cosmology, a spiritual essence or life-force that governs qualities of being</note> of fire and of water—that is, soul and spirit. He was intended to bring forth his own likeness out of himself—an image modeled after himself—through his imagination and his own love. He was capable of doing this without the tearing or dividing of his body.

<margin>Might or ability.</margin>
<margin>The Test, or Temptation.</margin>
3. For, as we mentioned before, the soul had the power to change the body into another form; and so it also had the power to bring forth a "twig" <note>A metaphorical offspring or branch of life</note> out of itself according to its own properties, if *Adam* had remained steadfast through the trial.

<margin>In, or as, or with.</margin>
4. But when he focused his imagination on worldly power and allowed the spirit of this world into his soul, and the Serpent into his "tincture," he began to long for earthly fruit—to eat of both evil and good. Then his tincture conceived an image that was half-earthly: namely, a monster. Into this monster, the *Turba* <note>term: Turba; the "disturber" or the wrathful principle of destruction and chaos</note> immediately inserted itself and sought to bring about its end.

<margin>Beget, or bear.</margin>
5. And so the noble Image was found trapped within the earthly one; then destruction and death began, and *Adam* could no longer bring forth life, for his divine power was lost.

6. And it would indeed have been lost forever, if the Heart of God had not instantly turned toward *Adam's* soul with the Word of Promise. This promise preserved the soul, even though its outward image had to perish, and

<summary>Boehme explains that Adam was originally an androgynous being capable of spiritual reproduction, but lost this power after the Fall. He begins to address how the soul is now propagated in the womb through the lens of this lost divine state.</summary>

# The Eighth Question Answered

...and the soul must sink down with the heavenly body through death into the new life, where its spirit will be renewed again.

<margin>k Inability or weakness.</margin>
7. And thus Adam fell asleep in <note>original: "impotence"</note> weakness; and then the second creation began. For God took the "Tincture" <note>In Boehme’s philosophy, a "Tincture" is a spiritual essence or life-force that gives qualities to matter.</note> of the water—like a twig taken from Adam’s soul—and a rib out of Adam, and half of the "Cross" <note>The "Cross" refers to the balanced intersection of the spiritual and physical forces within the original human.</note> that was in Adam, and <margin>l Or framed, or built a woman with them.</margin> made a woman from them.

8. As you know, the woman has one half of the Cross in her head and the man has the other; for the spirit of the soul dwells in the head, in the brain. Out of this spirit, God has taken a "twig" (that is, a child out of the spirit of the soul of Adam) and has given it to the woman.

9. And He has given the Tincture of the water to her, so that she should not bring forth devils; and the man has the Tincture of fire, which is the true origin of life.

10. And therefore the woman has received the "Matrix" <note>The womb or generative environment.</note>, which is the Tincture of Venus <note>Associated here with love, desire, and the "light" aspect of the soul.</note>, and the man has the Tincture of fire. Understand this: the woman has the Tincture of light, which cannot awaken life on its own; life arises only in the Tincture of fire.

11. And so it cannot be otherwise now: they must propagate as beasts do, using two seeds. The man sows the soul, and the woman sows the spirit; and being sown in an earthly field, the child is also brought forth after the manner of all animals.

<margin>Note how the soul exists before the conception.</margin>
12. Yet nevertheless, all three "Principles" <note>The three realms of existence: the dark fire-world, the light holy-world, and the external physical world.</note> are in the seed, but the inward cannot be known by the outward. For in the seed, the soul is not yet living; but when the two Tinctures come together, then it becomes a complete <margin>m Or Being, or substance.</margin> essence. For the soul is essential in the seed, and in the <margin>n Or out-breeding, or hatching.</margin> conception, it becomes substantial.

13. For as soon as the fire is <margin>o Hammered by the smith, or the *Faber* <note>Latin: "Faber" means craftsman or smith.</note> has struck.</margin> struck by *Vulcan* <note>The Roman god of fire, used here to represent the spark of life or the "inner master-worker."</note>, the soul is made perfectly whole in its essence. The spirit immediately goes out of the soul into the Tincture and attracts the outward <margin>p Rule, or government, or influence.</margin> dominion to itself—namely, the stars together with the air.

14. And then it is an eternal child, yet it also has the corruptible spirit clinging to it, along with the *Turba* <note>Latin: "Turba" refers to the "turmoil" or "disturber"—the wrathful power that seeks to destroy or dissolve complex beings.</note> which Adam took in through his imagination.

15. Then instantly the *Turba* seeks its <margin>q Or bound, or term, or goal, or end.</margin> limit in the spirit of this world and tries to reach that end. As soon as the soul has its life, the body is already old enough to die; and thus, many a soul perishes in its essence while it is still in the "Sulphur" <note>A chemical/alchemical term for the combustible, active substance of the seed.</note> of the seed.

16. But so that you may perceive that the man has the Tincture of fire and the woman has the Tincture of light in the water (the Tincture of Venus), you must observe the eager imagination of both toward one another. For the seed in its essence eagerly seeks life: the <margin>r Masculine.</margin> man's seed seeks it in the <margin>s Feminine.</margin> woman's Venus, and the woman's seed seeks it in the fire, in the origin of life. 17. And we answer here, that the soul does not come into the body at all, nor is it breathed into it <gloss>t</gloss> from the outside. Instead, each of the three principles <note>The three principles represent the dark fire-world, the light-world, and the outward physical world</note> has its own <gloss>u</gloss> artificer: one <gloss>x</gloss> works the fire in the center, another creates the tincture and water, and the third creates the earthly <gloss>y</gloss> Great Mystery.

## The Ninth Question.
### *In what way does the soul unite itself with the body?*

-> 1. <-

It was clarified before that all three principles are within one another, and they beget a child according to their likeness. They all remain within one another until the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; the "disturber" or the power of wrath and destruction that breaks down physical forms</note> destroys the body. Then the soul remains in the inward body—that is, in the divine body—or, if the soul is false, it remains in the *Turba*, which gives it a body according to its imagination, based on the wicked deeds it has committed.

2. The soul stands in the blood of the heart; there it has its seat and origin. The outward water and blood mingle together, but the soul does not fully receive the water of the blood; rather, it is captured by the imagination.

3. It does indeed naturally receive the inward water, but it only receives the Majesty with the Tincture of the Light through the imagination. Therefore, many times a child is more <gloss>a</gloss> blessed than an old person who <gloss>b</gloss> has the Devil as his guest.

<margin>
a Or in a better condition.
b Or harbors the Devil.
c Or saints.
d Or evil.
e Or body.
</margin>

4. But there are not many born <gloss>c</gloss> holy, except those from good seed. Yet many times a wrathful *Turba* winds itself in according to some powerful constellation <note>referring to astrological influences at the time of birth</note>, as is seen when honest parents often have <gloss>d</gloss> wicked children; but God knows who are His.

5. You see this in Jacob and Esau, who struggled in their mother’s <gloss>e</gloss> womb; also in Cain and Abel, in Isaac and Ishmael, and many others.

<summary>This page explains that the soul is not an external addition to the body but grows from the parents' "seed" through three creative principles. It also discusses how the soul's character is shaped by the heart's blood, the imagination, and spiritual influences, explaining why good parents may have wicked children.</summary>

# The Tenth Question

### Whether is the soul *from a branch* <note>original: "Ex Traduce"; the theological theory that souls are propagated from parents to children like a physical graft or branch</note>, and propagated after a human bodily manner, or is it newly created every time and breathed in by God?

---

->1.<-

<margin>From the ground up.</margin>
I very much wonder what kind of understanding and philosophy the world now has, that it cannot resolve this. Yet I do not blame you; for I know such questions are debated by those who account themselves learned Doctors in the schools and universities, who make great disputations about it. I cannot but wonder at the proud blindness, that there is no knowledge at all of God in reason <margin>Their reason, arts of logic, etc.</margin>.

2. Now therefore, you wise men, behold yourselves—what you are, and what you understand. You understand nothing at all of the Mystery; how then will you be teachers? It would be better for you to carry a shepherd’s crook in your hand than to put on the garment of Christ <note>i.e., to be a literal shepherd rather than a priest or minister</note>.

3. Oh! You shall give an account for your seducing of the world. And yet you boast of yourselves as if you were God, and arrogate divine power <margin>Or might, or authority; Divine Right.</margin> to yourselves. Take heed what you do; you shall see against whom you have kicked <margin>Or striven.</margin>. I fear that most of you are in *Babel* <note>The author uses "Babel" to signify a state of spiritual confusion and false, worldly religion</note>; awake, for it is day.

4. To you, my beloved friend, I give this answer: the soul is not newly created and breathed in every time. Instead, it is propagated after a human manner, as a branch <margin>Or twig springing forth.</margin> grows out of a tree. Or, to put it better, as a man plants or sows corn or seed, and so a spirit and body grow out of it.

5. And this is the only difference: the three principles <note>In Boehme’s cosmology, the three principles are the dark fire-world, the light holy-world, and the external visible world</note> are always in conflict <margin>Or wrestling, or contending.</margin> over man; each would gladly have him. Consequently, a wonderful *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe a state of disturbance, wrath, or spiritual confusion that destroys harmony</note> is often brought in while he is still in the seed.

6. But if the parents, both father and mother, have their souls clothed with Christ’s flesh—the Divine Essentiality—then this <note>the Turba</note> cannot happen. For Christ says, "A good tree cannot bring forth evil fruit." Yet the *Turba* can enter in over time through <margin>Or by.</margin> the use of reason.

7. So also, an evil tree cannot bring forth good fruit. That is, if both parents are evil and held captive by the Devil, then an evil soul is sown. However, the principles cannot yet judge it, nor can the *Turba*—

<summary>The author argues that the human soul is not created anew by God for each birth but is propagated from the parents like a branch from a tree, though it remains subject to spiritual conflict (the Turba).</summary>
<keywords>soul, propagation, Ex Traduce, Traducianism, Turba, Three Principles, Babel, original sin, spiritual biology</keywords>

<margin>
n In the Word of the Lord.
o One parent good, the other evil.
p Goods, Riches.
</margin>

The <term>Turba</term> <note>original: "Turba"; refers to the "Turba Gentium" or the spirit of confusion and wrath that can take hold of a soul</note> is neither; it is indeed an evil child, yet if it turns, it may, through the power of Imagination, enter into the **Word of the Lord**.

8. But it is rare, and seldom comes to pass, that a black raven becomes white. However, where the child is only half-and-half <note>referring to having one virtuous and one wicked parent</note>, it may be more easily accomplished. Nevertheless, it is possible and may very well happen; God casts no soul away unless it casts itself away. Every soul is its own judgment.

9. Consider this, you evil parents: you gather money for your children; instead, get them good souls, for that is more necessary for them.

## The eleventh Question.
### *How and where is the soul seated in Man?*

<margin>
q Or ungroundable/unfathomable.
r Forming, or making.
s Or resemblance/reflection.
</margin>

1. A thing which is unfathomable, and yet seeks and makes a foundation within itself, has its origin and seat in its first conception, where it conceives itself within itself. Its limit is there—namely, in the most innermost part—and it goes forth out of itself and seeks forward, where it then always creates one reflection <note>original: "Glasse"</note> according to the other, until it finds the first again: namely, the unfathomable limit.

<margin>
t Or formed.
u Or Son, or word.
x Or splendor.
y Or the hollow, pit, or furrow of the heart; the diaphragm or midriff.
Greek: *phrenes* (mind/diaphragm). *Sphage* (throat/slaughter).
z The Fire-Smith. Vulcan.
a Inward senses.
b Or outward senses.
c Threefold life.
</margin>

2. The soul is the same way; it is conceived in God, in the Heart, and the Word which conceived it was in the Heart—namely, in the Center. And so it continues in that form and in that seat, just as it was first grasped by the <term>Fiat</term> <note>Latin: "let it be done"; the creative command of God</note>; and so it remains to this day.

3. It dwells in three Principles, but the Heart is its origin. It is the inward fire in the heart, in the inward blood of the heart. Its spirit, which has a splendor from the fire, exists in the <term>Tincture</term> <note>a spiritual essence or "dye" that gives quality and life to matter</note>; for it is clothed with the Tincture and burns in the Heart.

4. And the Spirit moves upon the Heart in the bosom of the Heart, where both Principles <note>the light and dark worlds</note> separate themselves. It burns in the Tincture like a sulfurous light and diffuses itself abroad into all the members of the whole body, for the Tincture goes through every part.

5. But the true Fire-Smith <note>original: "Fire-Smith... Vulcanus"; the transformative power of the soul's will</note> in the Center sits in the Heart and governs with the Spirit in the Head, where it has its council-house—namely, the Mind and the inward senses. It also has five chief counselors—namely, the five outward senses—which arise from the five spirits of understanding, as we have declared in our third book <note>referring to the author's work "The Threefold Life of Man"</note>; and in our...

<summary>The author explains that while a soul's disposition is influenced by its parents, it ultimately determines its own judgment. He describes the soul as being seated in the heart, acting as a "fire-smith" that governs the body and mind through the senses.</summary>

<keywords>soul, heart, free will, Turba, Tincture, five senses, Fire-Smith, Fiat, Three Principles</keywords>

--- Page 93 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the soul's relationship with the physical world and begins the twelfth question regarding the nature of spiritual enlightenment.</meta>

<margin>d *The Three Principles*.</margin>
our **d** second book, and also in our **e** first.

<margin>e *Aurora*.</margin>
6. The soul is indeed seated in the inward Principle <note>The spiritual or divine realm within</note>, but it rules even in the outward—namely, in the Stars and Elements. If it is not a mere imitator <note>original: "Ape"</note> and does not allow itself to be taken captive, it has power enough to rule them; and if the soul immerses itself into God, the outward world must be obedient to it.

7. And if the soul returns again into the outward world, riding upon the "Chariot of the Bride" <note>A mystical reference to the soul's union with Divine Wisdom</note> and having the Holy Spirit as an assistant, no assault of the Devil is of any consequence. The soul destroys his nest and drives him out, and he must stand in scorn and shame.

8. And this is our *Answer* to this question: it must not be understood as if the soul is killed when a man is beheaded and his blood gushes out, causing the outward life to perish. No, the soul loses *one* Principle <note>The physical world</note> indeed, but not the **f** essence of that Principle; that follows the soul in the Tincture <note>term: Tincture; the spiritual quality or "dye" that carries the essence of one's life into the next realm</note>, in the Spirit, like a shadow.

<margin>f Namely, the third or outward works.</margin>
9. For the outward essence does not reach the inward part of the soul, except through the Imagination. There is nothing else in this world—no fire, nor sword—that can touch the soul or put it to death, except for the Imagination; that is its poison.

10. For the soul originally proceeded from the Imagination <note>The creative, formative power of the mind/spirit</note> and remains in it eternally.

## The twelfth Question.

### *How is the Soul enlightened, and what is its Illumination?*

->1.<-

<margin>h Or bitterness.</margin>
We must consider that if the Sun were taken away from this world, all things would be in darkness. Then outward Reason would say, "We are in dark death and in the **h** wrathfulness of the cold"; and it would indeed be so.

<margin>i Namely, the Sun.</margin>
2. Now observe, my beloved mind, and consider within yourself: when your body perishes and your spirit loses the **i** Sun, how can you then enjoy the Light? And with what will you see? In the *light of simplicity* <note>A state of humble, direct spiritual understanding</note> we remind you of this, so that you may consider it.

<margin>k Give, make, or cause.</margin>
3. That thing which exists in the Eternal Liberty, if it always enters again into the Eternal Liberty, it has no darkness; for it dwells in "Nothing" <note>The *Ungrund* or the absolute void/freedom of God before manifestation</note>, which does not **k** produce darkness. It is free as the...

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that the soul is immune to physical death and can only be harmed by its own "Imagination," then begins an inquiry into how the soul receives spiritual light after the physical sun is lost.</summary>
<keywords>soul, immortality, Imagination, Tincture, Illumination, Eternal Liberty, Three Principles, Aurora</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of the soul's relationship to God's light and the dangers of earthly attachment, following the discussion of the soul's origin and nature.</meta>

# The Twelfth Question Answered

<margin>Or Being, or Essence.</margin> The Eye of God, which beholds itself through a substance <note>original: "l substance"</note>.

4. When that eye imagines after something in "Lust" <note>In this context, "Lust" refers to a strong, driving desire or longing, not necessarily sexual.</note>, then the will enters into that "something" which the desiring lust itself creates. This thing receives the will into itself and overshadows it, so that the will dwells in darkness. It can have no Light unless it goes out again from that thing and returns into the Liberty <note>The "Liberty" refers to the divine state of freedom and stillness beyond nature.</note>.

5. Thus, we earnestly give you to understand that we have no <margin>Matters or business.</margin> light in all our affairs and works if we let our will enter into that which we have created. By setting our heart and will upon the <margin>Note the cause of our spiritual blindness.</margin> work of our own hands in covetousness, the soul becomes wholly blind. We then have no light in us except the outward light of the sun, which gives light only to the outward body. When that body <margin>The thing the soul was set upon.</margin> perishes, the soul remains imprisoned by that thing.

6. You must understand here the spirit and will of the soul; <margin>Cave or hole.</margin> for the prison of the soul is a dark valley <margin>Void of light.</margin> having no light. Although the soul may elevate and inflame itself, it becomes <margin>Or reach.</margin> only a wrathful flash of fire. It becomes like the Devil and cannot reach the Divine Light within itself.

7. The cause is that the soul has brought abominations into its will and spirit, which darken the spirit and hold it captive with the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "disturber" or the spirit of destruction and wrath in nature.</note>. For God’s Light does not go backward, but forward into eternity.

8. And therefore God’s Eye is <margin>Or in two halves or parts.</margin> twofold, standing back to back, as shown before in the figure; one part tends forward into the still eternity—into the Eternal Nothing, namely, into the Liberty.

9. And the other part tends backward into the Desire, and creates darkness and the "Center of Nature" within it, driving it to the greatest anguish and sharpness.

10. And then the will again sinks out of the anguish, through the darkness, into the still Liberty. It brings the wrathfulness of the "mobility" <note>The state of being active or in motion.</note> and earnest sharpness out of the anguish along with it.

11. In this sharpness, the Liberty—when the will brings the sharpness into it—becomes a highly triumphing, majestic Light. This is called God’s Light, which shines forever and cannot be <margin>Or shut up.</margin> smothered by anything; for it shines in the Eternal Liberty and desires nothing more.

12. And if (as you, earthly man, perhaps suppose) God would receive your spirit into His majestic Light while you let <margin>Note: Pride is the fiery life and the reflection of covetousness, as in a mirror.</margin> your abomination into your will—namely, covetousness, which has brought forth pride, the fiery life of covetousness—so that your will sticks wholly in earthliness; then you would darken God’s majesty. Your spirit and will would nevertheless remain stuck in covetousness and burn with the fiery source of the soul, like a reflection in a mirror (namely, in pride), and you would not be able to reach the majesty of God.

<summary>The author explains that when the human will focuses on earthly desires and covetousness, it becomes imprisoned in darkness and cannot access the Divine Light. He describes God's "Eye" as having two directions: one toward the peaceful eternity and one toward the active, sometimes painful, center of nature.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Light, Covetousness, The Will, Turba, Liberty, Center of Nature, Pride, Spiritual Blindness</keywords>

# The twelfth Question Answered.

13. No, even if you were to sit in the Cross of the Holy <margin>or Trinity</margin> Ternary <note>original: "Ternary"; a term used by Boehme to describe the Trinity or the manifested divine realm</note> and were surrounded by all the Holy Angels, you would still sit only in the Darkness, and your Spirit would shine only in the reflection of that Essence which you yourself have brought into the Spirit.

14. But if the soul and its Spirit, in its Image, desires to see God and behold the Eternal Light in God’s Majesty, then it must walk a twofold path in this world. Then it shall obtain the Eternal body—namely, the Image of God—and also sustain the outward life with the earthly body. Then it shall bring all the Wonders into the inward life (the wonders for which God created it in an outward life, and which it ought to awaken in that outward life) and eternally rejoice in them, holding them as a <margin>Looking-glass, or as a delight</margin> mirror. This is the right way, which follows below.

### ->The Exceeding Precious <margin>Or Gate of the Dayspring from on high</margin> Gate of the Aurora.<-

15. Behold, beloved Soul: if you wish to attain the light of God and see with the Eye of God, and also wish to enjoy the light of this world, sustain your body, and seek the wonders of God, then do as God himself does.

16. You have in your soul two Eyes which are set back-to-back. <margin>In Resignation <note>The act of surrendering one's self-will to God.</note></margin> One looks into Eternity; <margin>In self-hood</margin> the other looks backward into Nature and always moves outward, seeking through Desire and constantly creating one reflection after another. Let it be so; it must be so, for God wills it so.

17. But do not turn this <margin>Namely, the left Eye</margin> other eye back into worldly longing. Instead, use the Right Eye to always draw the left one back toward you. Do not let <margin>The left, or Eye of Nature</margin> this eye wander away from you with its will for wonders—that is, away from the Eye turned toward Divine Liberty—but rather draw toward you the wonders it has revealed and performed.

18. Let this eye seek food for the earthly body, but do not let it enter into the food—that is, into Covetousness. Instead, draw it close to the seeing Eye and do not let it go.

19. Let the hands labor and obtain food, and let the Eye draw the Wonders to itself, but not <margin>Covetousness, Envy, Pride, or Anger</margin> Matter <note>meaning material greed or worldly attachment</note>. Otherwise, that which is drawn in will become darkness to you.

20. Let the Devil roar at you, making a noise before your left Eye; he cannot get in unless you allow your Eye to receive in <margin>Something in which it trusts</margin> Matter.

21. Thus, when your earthly body perishes, you shall see with the right Eye all the wonders <margin>Or which are in the Eye of the Reason</margin> in the Left Eye which you have worked and discovered here. And when the earthly life is gone, then—

<summary>Boehme explains the "two eyes" of the soul—one focused on eternity and the other on nature—and instructs the reader to keep the worldly eye subordinate to the spiritual eye to avoid the darkness of greed.</summary>
