Then your left eye is free from the nature of wrath.

22. And although it possesses nature—for it is nature itself which awakens and contains the wonders—yet it exists with those wonders within the Eternal Liberty. Since it has taken in nothing of material matter, it is free.

<margin>Or solace itself.</margin>
23. And nature, with its wonders, is a fiery sharpness; it takes hold of the Eternal Liberty and thus creates Majesty within that Liberty and those wonders. From this, the right eye (which is as if it were dead here in this life) becomes enlightened. It <note>original: "rejoice"</note> rejoices with the left eye forever in that exceeding joyful Majesty, and sees God with both eyes eternally.

<margin>Or the understanding of the whole Scripture is contained in it. Sight or vision.</margin>
24. This is one Gate. He who sees and knows this rightly in the Spirit sees all that God is and can do. Through this, he also sees through Heaven, Hell, and Earth, and through the essence of all essences. Furthermore, this understanding contains the whole of Scripture—everything that has been written from the beginning of the world until now. But this is a rare and precious sight; the "Old Adam" <note>The unregenerate, sinful human nature inherited from Adam.</note> does not know it and does not see it; only the "New Man" who is born in God can perceive it.

25. But since the weak mind understands us with such difficulty, we will set it down more plainly: Behold! If you wish to see God’s light in your soul and desire to be enlightened by God, then do this:

<margin>Or employment.</margin>
26. You are in the world. Do you have an honest calling <note>original: "employment"</note> free of deceit? Continue in it; work, labor, and finish your business. As necessity requires, seek out wonders both in the earth and the other elements. Whatever the craft or art may be, it is all the work of God. Seek silver and gold in the earth and make artistic works from them; build and plant. All of this serves to manifest God’s works of wonder.

<margin>Or Lesson.</margin>
->But mark this A. B. C. <note>The basic fundamentals or primary lesson.</note><-

<margin>Or make its nest therein.</margin>
27. You must not give your spirit permission to enter into these things and fill itself with them, thereby making a "Mammon" <note>An idol of wealth or material greed.</note> of them. Do not let the spirit settle itself there as if in darkness, otherwise it is merely a fool in the sight of God and the Devil’s ape. If its will is wholly fixed on material things, your noble image is altered according to the imagination in your spirit and according to your will, which is stuck in covetousness; thus, you lose God’s image.

28. For that image is magical; it is as subtle as a spirit—indeed, much more subtle. It is much more subtle and thin than the soul itself. It is like God, who dwells in the Eternal Liberty and yet is not grasped or contained by anything, for He is thinner than anything; and so is your noble image.

29. And yet it consists <margin>Or of.</margin> in heavenly flesh and blood, and is an essentiality <note>original: "Essentiality"; here meaning a spiritual substance or essence</note> come out of the Divine Body; it is Christ’s flesh and blood, and your soul dwells within it; <margin>The soul.</margin> it is the fire of the Majesty therein.

30. And the Holy Spirit sits in the heart of the Image, and proceeds from the Image with voices, languages, wonders, sounds, and songs.

31. If you are <margin>Faithful and sincere.</margin> upright, you bring your wonders into this Image, and do it in this way: set your left-hand will <note>The "left will" refers to one's engagement with the physical, temporal world.</note> upon the work which you do, and consider that you are God’s servant in the vineyard of God, and labor faithfully.

32. And direct your right-hand will <note>The "right will" refers to the soul's primary orientation toward the eternal and divine.</note> upon God and that which is eternal; and do not think yourself secure at any time. Think that you are only at your day-labor, and must always listen for the voice when your Master shall bid you come home.

33. Give Reason no <margin>Or leave.</margin> room to say, "This is my treasure, it is mine, I have enough, I will gather much so that I may get honor in the world and leave much to my children."

34. But consider that your children are God’s children, and you are God’s servant; that your work is God’s work, and that your money, goods, mind, and blood are in God’s hand—He may do what He will with them. When He calls you home into your own country <note>Meaning the spiritual afterlife or the soul's true origin.</note>, then He may take your labor and give it to another.

35. And give your heart no <margin>Or leave.</margin> room to suffer the spirit of your will to bring haughtiness into the Image, but cast down your will continually in humility before God. In this way, your Image always enters with your will in humility into the Majesty of God; and so your Image is continually enlightened with the high, triumphing light of God.

36. Oh, how cheerful is the soul when its source of fire and anguish <margin>Or perceives.</margin> tastes God’s light! How exceedingly <margin>Or friendly.</margin> courteous is it? Oh, how it bows itself before God!

37. Thus the soul and the Image in the Spirit are all three within one another, for they are one essence according to the Holy Trinity. My beloved brother, we answer this question of yours in this way: that the soul cannot be enlightened in any other way than this; its illumination is only after this manner.

38. The soul is in this world and also in God. Here in this life, it is a servant of God’s wonders, which it should open with one eye, and with the other eye bring them into the beginning before God, and set and cast all its doings into God’s will. By no means say of anything in this world, "This is mine, I am lord of this," for the soul lies if it says so.

39. All is God’s; you are a servant and should walk in love,

and humility towards God and your brother; for your brother’s soul is a fellow-member with your soul. Your brother’s joy in Heaven with God is also your joy, and his wonders are also your wonders.

40. For in Heaven, God is all in all; He fills everything, and the Holy Spirit is the life in all. There is pure joy; there is no sorrow. There, all things belong to God, and all things also belong to the image of God. All things are held in common; everyone rejoices at the power, brightness, and beauty of one another. There is no malice or envy, for all of that remains in death and Hell.

41. Therefore, you chosen children of God, who are born again in Christ, take this into consideration: depart from covetousness and self-will. You have been led blindfolded for a long time in *Babel* <note>original: "Babell"; referring to the state of spiritual confusion and false religious systems</note>. Go out from her! You are called with a <margin>strong or loud-sounding voice</margin> shrill voice. It will shortly raise the dead; let it be a help to you, so that you may obtain eternal joy in God.

42. The Spirit shows plainly that whoever will not grow forth together with the new sprout, which grows in the <margin>namely, the Eternal Word</margin> Mother, shall and must be cast into the lake of brimstone with the Dragon’s Whore in *Babel*.

43. There is a time of earnestness at hand, and though you do not see it with earthly eyes, yet it will certainly come upon you. You will see clearly enough at your death what kind of judgment this is, and in what time and under what *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe a state of spiritual disturbance, wrath, or the "turmoil" that destroys life</note> you have lived. We speak in good earnest, as we ought.

## The thirteenth Question.

### *How does the soul feed upon the word of God?*

-> 1. <-

If the soul enters in this way (as mentioned above) into the light of the Majesty and receives the light of God, then it has a complete longing and desire. It continually attracts into itself the divine power—that is, the divine body—through its desire. The Holy Spirit is the power of God’s Spirit, and so the soul obtains the body and Spirit of God and eats at God’s table. All that the Father has belongs to the Son, and whatever the Son has belongs to his image <note>referring to the human soul as the image of God</note>.

2. It eats God’s flesh and Christ’s body, and by this eating, the divine body also grows <margin>or to, or from, or out of it, or as a chicken grows in an egg</margin> within it, so that it thus obtains the divine body.

<summary>This page describes the communal joy of Heaven and warns the "chosen children" to flee the spiritual confusion of Babel before the coming judgment. It also begins explaining how the soul is nourished by the "Divine Body" and the Holy Spirit through spiritual desire.</summary>
<keywords>Humility, Holy Spirit, Babel, Turba, Soul, Divine Power, Image of God, Spiritual Nourishment</keywords>

# The Thirteenth Question Answered.

Body, and so becomes God’s child—not only a likeness, but a child born in God out of His essences, and lives in God.

<margin>Note the food of the soul.</margin>
3. When the soul hears God’s children teach and speak God’s word (even in this world), it receives it and eats it.

<margin>The author calls Baptism and the Lord's Supper two Testaments.</margin>
4. The outward man eats earthly bread, and the soul eats the bread of God, of which Christ said that He gives us His body for food; and His testaments <note>original: "Testaments"; referring here to the sacraments of Baptism and the Lord's Supper</note> are nothing else.

5. Indeed, we do not eat Spirit without a body, for the soul is already Spirit beforehand and desires to have a body; and so it receives both body and Spirit.

6. Let this be spoken to you, O *Babel* <note>A term used by the author to describe the confused and divided state of the institutionalized church</note>, and see how you handle Christ’s testaments and what you teach. When you say Christ’s testaments are Spirit without a body, you lie against God and deny God’s substantiality—Christ’s heavenly body, which is greater than all things and is the fullness of all things, yet exists within its own principle.

7. O earthly mouth, you shall not bite it with teeth; the soul has another mouth, which receives it under the outward element. The outward part of man receives the outward element, and the inward part receives the inward substance.

8. The Supper of Christ with His disciples was like this: the outward part is a remembrance; the inward part is the substance. For the Kingdom of God consists in power; it is magical <note>original: "Magicall"; in this context, it refers to the divine creative power that brings spirit into substance, not sorcery</note>—not merely a thought, but essential and substantial.

<margin>Something.</margin>
9. The *Magia* <note>The divine creative power</note> creates substance; for in the eternal nothing there is nothing, but the *Magia* creates something where nothing was.

10. Now in God there is not only Spirit, but nature, substance, flesh and blood, tincture <note>A spiritual essence or life-force</note>, and all things. This outward world is a likeness of the inward world.

11. We tell you, we speak what we feel, see, taste, and know, and not a fable or opinion. We do this not for ourselves only, but for you, as one member of a body is bound to do for another, so that our joy may be in you, and we also may enjoy you again as brothers together in one essence. He that desires to know more of this, let him read our third book <note>original: "Threefold life. 13. Chap."</note>, and there he shall find the details concerning the soul's meal <note>original: "Seele-Messe"; the spiritual nourishment or "Soul-Mass"</note> and Christ’s testaments.

# The Fourteenth Question

## Whether such a new soul is without sin? We refer here to the soul propagated in a newly born child.

---

-> 1. <-

<margin>Sublime, sharp, or subtle.</margin> My beloved friend; this is a very deep <note>original: "sublime, or sharp, or subtile"</note> question; yet you shall be answered. For the time of manifestation is born; the day breaks, and the night is past. Therefore, let eternal praise and thanks be given to God, who has again begotten us to light and to an inheritance that never fades away, and has received us as his beloved children.

2. My beloved friend, you know well the heavy fall of *Adam*, as we have shown you extensively in all our writings: namely, that the soul has turned itself away with its "right eye" <note>In Boehme’s terminology, the "right eye" represents the spiritual vision directed toward eternity, while the "left eye" looks toward the material world.</note> from God and into the spirit of this world. It has become disobedient to God and has wholly corrupted its noble image, changing it into a monstrous image. It has let in the spirit of this world, whereas it should have powerfully ruled over it with the will, and should not have let the soul eat of evil and good at all.

<margin>Pleasure or desire.</margin> 3. But now it has plainly transgressed God’s command <note>original: "pleasure"</note> and has placed its imagination into the earthly spirit. There, the *Turba* <note>term: Turba. A Latin word meaning "turmoil" or "disturbance." In this context, it refers to the destructive, wrathful power in nature that breaks things apart and leads to death.</note>—which brought the earthly monster into the soul's noble image—instantly took it captive. Thus, the *Turba* immediately fought and found the limit <note>The "limit" refers to the boundary of mortal life or the point of death.</note> in which the image perished. If the Word <note>The Logos or Christ.</note> had not <margin>Set itself in the middle.</margin> mediated or interposed itself, the soul would have remained in that state forever.

4. And so, now that the *Turba* is once seated in the earthly abyss and has captivated both body and soul, it always drives the body toward its limit, and there destroys it and casts it away. Then the poor soul remains naked, without a body.

5. And unless the soul turns with its "right eye" again into the Word and obtains a body born out of God, it remains naked and has the *Turba* within it. This stirs up the fire in its great anguish; for <margin>That is, the Turba.</margin> it is an eager hunger, a seeker, and a finder.

6. Now it is thoroughly known to us that our soul is fast bound to the spirit of this world, for the *Turba* holds us captive in the wrath of the anger of God.

7. And although our soul may escape and become newborn in God,

<summary>The author explains that the human soul is corrupted by the Fall of Adam, becoming captive to the "Turba" (destructive wrath) and the spirit of the material world. He argues that without spiritual rebirth through the Word, the soul remains in a state of naked anguish and subject to divine anger.</summary>

# The Fourteenth Question Answered.

God, yet <margin>The *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; refers to the "turmoil" or "disturbance," the destructive principle in nature that leads to death and decay.</note>.</margin> it still possesses the outward body and consumes it, for it pierces through it even to the Abyss. There it finds that the body is only a glass <note>A mirror or reflection.</note> of the Eternal; then it goes forth from the glass into the Eternal and lets the body lie in the "Nothing."

8. Also, you know well that the soul, along with the body in the seed, is half-earthy. For it is *Sulphur*—that is, <margin>*Phur* is powder, and the *Turba* is in it, which has ability enough to destroy the seed.</margin> *Phur* and <margin>*Sul* and *Phur* together.</margin> *Sul* together—and the *Turba* is in it, which has ability enough to destroy <margin>Matter or substance.</margin> the seed.

9. How then can a soul be born pure? It cannot be; it brings <margin>*Sul* is Spirit or Light.</margin> the *Turba* with it into the world and is sinful in the mother's <margin>In one another.</margin> womb. <margin>Or body.</margin>

10. But know that God has become Man, and the Word *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let it be done"; the creative command of God.</note> has again put itself into the seed. Although the *Turba* is now in the earthly part, so that the seed is not altogether free, yet the matter stands thus with the soul:

11. The soul is not wholly forsaken by God, so long as the father and mother are <margin>Or virtuous.</margin> honest and in God; for it comes from the soul of the father and of the mother. And although a child die in the mother's womb without <margin>External Baptism.</margin> baptism, yet it is baptized with the spirit of the father and of the mother—namely, with the Holy Ghost which dwells in them—and the *Turba* is destroyed in death; for the <margin>That which belongs to Faith.</margin> faith-part passes through to God.

12. But the matter is far otherwise with wicked parents; if the child die in the mother's womb, <margin>See Question 19, verse 12.</margin> its soul falls into the *Turba* and does not reach God for eternity. It also knows nothing of Him, but it is a life according to the essence and property of the parents.

13. And yet, by this it does not reach the "inflammation" <note>The full fire of Hell.</note>, for that soul itself has not yet committed <margin>Actual sin.</margin> sin. Instead, it is a spirit in the source, quite void of self-desire and wonders; it is like burning brimstone, like the <margin>Wandering false lights that lead people astray in the night.</margin> *will-o'-the-wisps* <note>original: *Ignes Fatui*; literally "foolish fires."</note>, and cannot reach God. It remains between Heaven and Hell in the Mystery until the Judgment of God, which shall at last gather in its harvest and put everything apart in its own place.

14. Although Mr. Sophister <note>A term for a scholar who relies on clever but false reasoning.</note> may have other <margin>Polydeuces <note>A Greek name used here to represent one who is learned in the letter of the law or carnal reason.</note>. Or one that is learned in the letter, or carnal reason.</margin> philosophy regarding this, we do not care for his art; we have eyes, and he has art; we speak what we see.

15. Thus we give you to understand that no soul is born into this world without sin, however honest the parents may be; for it is <margin>Meaning or opinion.</margin> conceived in the earthly seed and brings the *Turba* of the body with it, which has also surrounded the soul.

16. Therefore God made a Covenant with children in the Old Testament through circumcision, and bound them in that Covenant <margin>Engendered or hatched.</margin> to have their blood shed, and so drown the *Turba* of the soul with it.

## The fifteenth Question.

### ->How does sin enter into the Soul, seeing that it is the work and creature of God?<-

->1.<-

<margin>e Seeking, or Longing.</margin>
As was mentioned before, so it is: the <term>Turba</term> and the earthly <sup>e</sup> desire come together into this world. Thus, the soul is strongly pulled by two forces: namely, by the <sup>d</sup> Word of the Lord <note>original: "Verbum Domini"</note>, which
<margin>d The Word of the Lord.</margin>
mediated or interposed itself, and which out of Love became Man. This draws the soul continually into the Kingdom of God and plainly shows the soul the <term>Turba</term>, so that the soul sees in Nature what falsehood and sin are; and if the soul allows itself to be drawn,

<summary>The author argues that salvation is found in spiritual power rather than outward ritual, criticizing religious exclusion of non-Christians and explaining how the soul is caught between divine love and earthly desire.</summary>

2. Secondly, the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; Latin for "turmoil" or "disturbance." In Boehme's theology, it refers to the destructive, chaotic power that breaks things apart and leads to spiritual death.</note> also mightily draws the soul with its bond, and continually brings the earthly desire into it, especially in youth. When the "Earthly Tree" is full of green, sprouting essences and poison, then the *Turba* insinuates itself so mightily that many a soul is not freed for all eternity.

3. In a thing that has its origin from two beginnings of equal weight, one part will sink down if weight is added to it—whether it is good or evil that is added.

4. Sin does not make itself, but the will makes it; it comes from the imagination into the spirit. For the spirit enters into a thing and is infected by that thing, and so the *Turba* of that thing enters the spirit and first destroys the Image of God.

5. And the *Turba* proceeds further and searches deeper, and so it finds the Abyss <note>original: *Abysse*; the bottomless depth or the "Unground" from which all existence emerges.</note>—namely, the soul. It seeks within the soul and finds the wrathful fire, by which it mingles itself with the thing that was introduced into the spirit; thus, at length, sin is fully born. Now, therefore, whatever desires to bring that which is outward into the Will, that is sin.

6. The will ought to incline to nothing but to meekness and Love, as if it were a "Nothing" or dead to the self. We should only desire to live for God, so that God may work in us; and whatever else we do, our will must be directed so that we do it for God.

<margin>

</margin>

9. If it is begotten by godly parents, then each principle <note>original: *Principle*; referring to the three realms of existence: the dark fire-world, the light holy-world, and the outer astral-world.</note> has its own part in it.

## The Sixteenth Question

### *How is the soul kept in such union, both in the Adamic <note>original: "Adamicall"; referring to the natural, fallen state of man inherited from Adam</note> and the Regenerate Body?*

-> 1. <-

We have mentioned before that there are three Principles <note>The three "Principles" in Boehme’s theology are the Dark World (fire/wrath), the Light World (love/majesty), and the Outward World (nature/matter).</note> which are all three in the soul already, and exist within one another as one thing. You must understand that the conflict in the soul begins beforehand in the "seed" <note>The biological and spiritual essence of a human being before birth.</note> while it lies hidden in both sexes, in the man and the woman. At that time, the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe a state of disturbance, wrath, or the "awakener" of judgment that disrupts harmony.</note> stirs itself up by driving the essence of the seed toward a false imagination and a false desire.

2. Although the Spirit tames the body, the body still forms imaginations. This *Turba* causes these in the seed, and no one can deny that many times this imagination is offensive to him. Where there is a righteous spirit, it wishes such thoughts were <margin>Excommunicated.</margin> accursed <note>original: "anathematised"; to be formally set apart or cursed.</note>. You must know that the spirit of the soul is stuck in a miserable strait and cannot be loosened until the *Turba* takes the body <note>Referring to the death of the physical body.</note>.

3. Now, there is never a perfect union between the outward man and the Regenerate man. The outward man would always devour the Regenerate, for they exist within one another; yet each has its own Principle, so that the outward cannot overpower the inward if the <margin>Or the Combat.</margin> Spirit continues in the struggle.

4. They may very well depend on one another, for all three set forth God’s works of wonder, provided they continue in due order, with each keeping to its own Principle.

<margin>Note that three bear rule in man:
1. The Spirit of the Soul.
2. The outward Spirit.</margin> 5. For the soul has the government of the Fire, and it is the cause of the life of all three. The Spirit of the soul has the government of the Light, in which the noble heavenly Image exists within the Divine Body. And the outward spirit has the government of the earthly life; this spirit should seek and manifest the Wonders of creation, while the inward Spirit should give it the understanding to do so. The soul should then manifest the Abyss <note>The "Abyss" here refers to the "Unground" or the deepest hidden mystery of God's nature.</note> (that is, the highest secret) to the outward spirit.

6. The Soul is the pearl, the Spirit of the soul is the finder of the pearl, and the earthly spirit is the seeker. The earthly body is the <margin>Mystery. Secret. Or hiddenness.</margin> Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium"</note> wherein the secret <note>original: "Arcanum"</note> of the greatest hiddenness is couched. For the Deity has manifested itself in the earthliness—that is, in a tangible essence—and therefore three seekers now belong to it.

7. But you must not suppose that we are an enemy to the out-

<summary>Boehme explains the internal conflict between the earthly and spiritual natures of man, describing how the soul, the inward spirit, and the outward spirit must function in harmony to reveal God's wonders. He introduces the "Turba" as a disruptive force in the human "seed" that creates a lifelong struggle between the flesh and the regenerate spirit.</summary>

# The Sixteenth Question Answered

...ward life, for it is most profitable to us regarding the wonders of God. There is nothing more profitable to the whole man than to stand still in his threefold life <note>The author refers to the three realms: the divine light, the dark fire-world, and the external physical world.</note> and not retreat at all with the outward into the inward, but rather move with the inward into the outward.

8. For the outward man is a beast <note>original: "Beast"; referring to the animal-like physical nature</note> and does not belong to the inward man. However, the wonders which the outward man has brought forth from the inward, and which it has revealed in tangible existence, belong in their form (though not in their essence) to the inward. The inward spirit must receive these—which are God’s works of wonder—for they shall be its joy forever.

9. And thus we say that the soul may be kept very well in the "New Man" <note>The spiritually reborn self</note> if the spirit of its tincture <note>term: Tincture; a spiritual essence or life-force that transmits qualities</note> does but hinder its <margin>longing, seeking, or desire</margin> and imagination. Although the outward spirit is bestial, the inward understanding spirit is able to restrain and tame the outward, for it is lord over it. But he who allows the bestial spirit to be lord is a beast, and he also possesses a bestial image in his inward form, within the tincture.

10. And he who lets the fire-spirit—namely, the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; the "disturber" or the wrathful, destructive principle in nature</note>—be lord, <margin>substantial, or a devil incarnate</margin> he is an essential devil in the inward image. Therefore, it is necessary here that the outward spirit pour <margin>namely, humility</margin> water into the fire, so that it may hold that <margin>or stern</margin> strong spirit captive. Thus, seeing as it will not be God’s image, it may <margin>at least</margin> remain a beast in the inward image.

11. Now, if we consider ourselves in the <margin>of the old and new man together</margin> union, the outward spirit is very profitable to us. Many souls would perish if the bestial spirit did not exist to hold the fire captive and set before the fire-spirit earthly, bestial labor and joy. In these things, the soul may occupy itself until it is able—by the <margin>or works</margin> wonders in the imagination—to discover something of its noble image, so that it may seek itself again.

12. My beloved children, who are born <margin>or of</margin> in God, I tell you: it was not for nothing that God breathed the outward spirit (that is, the outward life) into Adam’s nostrils, for great danger attended this image.

13. God knew what happened to Lucifer, and also what the great eternal <margin>Magia or desire</margin> magic could do. Indeed, Adam might have become a devil, but the outward glass <note>The physical body/world acting as a mirror or a cooling container</note> hindered that; for where water is, it quenches the fire.

14. Also, many a soul, by its wickedness, would become a devil in a <margin>or the twinkling of an eye</margin> moment if the outward life did not hinder it, so that the soul cannot wholly inflame itself.

15. How many people are there who are so full of poison and <margin>or malice, wickedness</margin> evil that they commit murder and villainy? But their fire has water, or else they would be beyond remedy. As you see in gall, which is a fiery poison, it is mingled with water, and so the violence of the fire is calmed.

<summary>The author argues that the physical, "bestial" life serves as a necessary restraint on the soul's inner fire, preventing humans from becoming "devils incarnate" by providing earthly distractions and a cooling influence. This physical existence allows the soul the time and means to rediscover its divine image through God's wonders.</summary>

16. It is the same with the Inward Essence: the Spirit of this world has wound itself into the abyss <note>original: "Abysse"; referring to the bottomless depth or center</note> of the soul. In its source, it contains a killing water, with which it often sprinkles the soul when the soul attempts to spit fire.

17. Furthermore, the outward Spirit could not have life without this Fire, as it possesses fire in all creatures; however, this outward fire is only the wrathful expression of the Inward fire.

<margin>Or kindled.</margin>
18. The Inward fire consumes earth and stones, as well as the body and blood—yes, even the Noble Image <note>The "Noble Image" refers to the divine likeness or the spiritual soul in its pure state</note>—if it is inflamed in the Will. But in that state, the water acts as a medicine for it, pulling down its ambitious force; for by that force, the fire labors to rise above the gentleness of God, just as Lucifer did.

## The seventeenth Question.
### From where, and for what reason, is the conflict between the Spirit and the Flesh?

->I.<-

My beloved friend; you know well that fire and water are opposites: for the fire is Life, and the water is its Death. You see plainly that when water is poured upon the fire, the source of the fire goes out, and so the fire is dead.

2. And although in Man the fire is not completely dead because of the Light—which continually generates fire—there is still an enmity; just as there is an enmity between God and Hell; and yet Hell, or the Fire of wrath, belongs to God.

3. And God’s Majesty would not be revealed if His Anger did not exist. This anger sharpens the dark hiddenness of Eternity through the wrath of Nature, so that it is transformed into Fire. From this, the high Light in the free Eternity is brought forth, which creates a Majesty within the gentle source.

<margin>Or life, or working.</margin>
4. And yet the Fire is the only cause for there being a source <note>original: "source"; here meaning a living power or active quality</note> in the Light and in the Gentleness; for the Light proceeds from the brilliance of the fire and contains the essence of the fire within itself.

5. But the Will (as mentioned before) sinks down into the Anguish, even into Death, and springs up again fresh in the Liberty. This is the Light which possesses the property <margin>Or property.</margin> of the Fire; yet it contains another Principle within it, for the Anguish has been transformed into Love.

6. It is in this same manner within the Body, where the flesh strives—

<summary>This page explains the metaphysical relationship between fire and water as symbols of wrath and gentleness, arguing that the conflict between spirit and flesh is rooted in the very nature of God's manifestation.</summary>

# The Seventeenth Question Answered

...strives against the Spirit. The life of the outward flesh is a mirror <note>original: "Glasse"</note> of the most inward fire-life—namely, of the life of the soul. The life of the Spirit of the soul, along with the light of the tincture <note>The "tincture" refers to the spiritual essence or "dye" that gives quality and color to life</note>, is the middle-most life, and yet it is born out of the soul.

7. But understand our depth correctly: the Spirit of the soul, in which the Divine Image consists, arises in the fire. It is, first of all, the will toward the fire; but when the <margin>Or wrathful desire and indignation of the fire.</margin> wrath in the fire is sharpened and inflamed, then the Will enters into a great anguish, like a dying, and sinks down into itself out of the wrath into the Eternal Liberty. And yet there is no actual dying, but <margin>Or second.</margin> another world thus comes out of the first.

8. For then the Will springs up in the other world as a sharpness out of the fire; yet it is without any such <margin>Or aching property.</margin> anguish-source in the Eternal Liberty. It is a moving, a driving, and an <margin>Or feeling.</margin> acknowledging of the anguished nature. It has all the <margin>Or springing virtues.</margin> essences which were brought forth in the anguish of the first sharp fire-world; but they are like one who goes out of fire into water, and so the anguish of the fire is left behind in the water.

9. You must understand that this life is the life of the Spirit of the soul. The soul is the center of nature, and the Spirit is the precious and noble Image which God created for His own Image. Herein stands the high, <margin>Or kingly.</margin> royal, and precious Image of God; for God is thus: He is contained in the same source of life.

10. The Spirit is not separated from the soul; no, just as you see that fire and light are not separated and yet are not one. They have a twofold <margin>Or quality.</margin> source: the fire is wrathful, while the light is mild and lovely. In the light is the life, and in the fire is the cause of the life.

11. And thus, without much seeking, you may find the cause of the contrariety <note>original: "Contrariety"; meaning conflict or opposition</note> that exists between the flesh and the Spirit. For the inward Spirit has the <margin>Or God's body.</margin> Divine Body from the meek essentiality, while the outward spirit has the body of the mirror of the wrathful fire—namely, the body of the mirror of the soul. This outward spirit would always awaken the <margin>Vehemence or fierceness.</margin> wrathfulness—namely, the great wonders which lie in the <margin>In the secret or hidden mystery of eternity.</margin> *Arcanum* <note>original: "Arcanum"; a secret or hidden mystery</note>, in the <margin>Sternness or fierceness.</margin> eagerness of the soul—if the inward Spirit of Love did not hinder it. The inward Spirit prevents it from elevating itself and inflaming the soul, for if it did, it would lose the <margin>The habitation or sweet taste.</margin> enjoyment of Love and the Image, and the wrathfulness of the soul would destroy it; and thus, conflict arises.

12. The inward Spirit would be master, for it tames the outward; and likewise, the outward would be master, for it says, "I have the great wonders and the *Arcanum*." Thus it brags of the mystery, and yet it is but a mirror of the mystery.

13. It is not the essence of the mystery, but a <margin>Seeking or longing.</margin> desire, an incomprehensible mirror in which the mystery is beheld; but it would be...

<summary>This page explains the metaphysical relationship between the soul (the fire-source) and the spirit (the light-image), describing how the conflict between flesh and spirit arises from their different origins in wrath and love.</summary>

be master, seeing it has attained a principle and is a life of itself; but it is a fool in comparison to the Mystery <note>original: "Mysterie"; here referring to the hidden divine wisdom or the "Great Work" of spiritual alchemy</note>.

14. Therefore, beloved brother, if you would seek the Mystery, do not seek it in the outward spirit; you will be deceived and attain nothing but a mere glimpse of the Mystery. Enter in even to the Cross, then seek Gold, and you will not be deceived. You must seek in another world for the pure Child that is without spot; in this world, you find only the drossy child that is altogether imperfect. But go about it in the right manner.

15. Go back from the Cross into the fourth form <note>A reference to Boehme's seven qualities of nature; the fourth form is the "Fire" or the point of transformation</note>, and there you have *Sol* <note>the Sun/Gold</note> and *Luna* <note>the Moon/Silver</note> together. Bring that in anguish into death, and bruise that composed magical body so long until it becomes again what it was before the center in the Will; and then it becomes <margin>desirous</margin> magical and hungry after Nature.

16. It is a <margin>seeking</margin> longing in the eternal longing, and would gladly have <margin>a desire</margin> a body. Therefore, give it *Sol*—that is, the Soul— <margin>for a body</margin> so that it may have a body, and then it will soon make a body according to the soul; for the Will springs up in Paradise with fair heavenly fruit without blemish.

17. There you have the Noble Child, you covetous gripers! We must indeed tell this to you, seeing it is born with it; but those only who are of our tribe <note>meaning those spiritually awakened or "initiated" into these truths</note> will understand us.

18. For we do not mean here a <margin>similitude, figure, or parable</margin> glass or heaven, but the Gold with which you boast, which for so long a time has been your idol-god. And your blind <margin>owl-eyes (or cow's eyes)</margin> owl-eyes are so completely put out that you see less than before. But the children shall see, eat, and be satisfied, so that they may praise God.

19. We speak here wonderfully <note>original: "wonderfully"; meaning in a way that inspires awe or seems strange</note>, yet we speak nothing but what we must speak. Let no one marvel that he knows the Mystery who has not learned it from any man. Does not a herb grow without your <margin>direction</margin> counsel? Neither does it inquire after your art. Truly, the Mystery has also grown without your art; it has its own school, like the Apostles on the Day of Pentecost, who spoke with many languages and tongues without premeditation and art; and so is this simplicity in like manner.

20. And this foretells your fall, O Babel <note>referring to the confused and corrupt state of organized religion</note>, <margin>that you may be warned of it</margin> so that you may know it. No wrath nor anger will help you; the star is born which leads the <margin>Magi</margin> wise men out of the East Country. But seek only where you are, and find yourself; and cast the <margin>wrangling, malice, and tyranny</margin> *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe the spirit of confusion, wrath, and destruction</note> from you, and then you shall live with the children. This we tell you in good earnest: there is no other remedy. Your anger is your fire, which will destroy yourself.

21. Or do you think that we are blind? If we saw nothing, we would still be silent. What pleasure would a lie be to God? Yes—

<summary>The author describes a spiritual-alchemical process of "bruising" the magical body to reach a state of heavenly purity, while warning the corrupt "Babel" that their anger will lead to their destruction.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy, Sol and Luna, The Mystery, Pentecost, Babel, Turba, Spiritual Rebirth, The Noble Child</keywords>

# The seventeenth Question Answered.

Otherwise, we would be found in the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; refers to the "Turba Magna," a term used by Boehme to describe the spirit of wrath, confusion, or the disturbing element in nature that seeks to destroy or scramble divine order</note>, which searches through all human essences and works. Or do we perform this service for wages? Is it our <margin>e Or trade.</margin> livelihood? Why do we not focus only on our physical bread, according to outward reason?

22. But seeing it is our day-labor, we must do what the Father wills, for we must give an account of it in the evening. We speak this seriously and in good earnest.

23. Thus you may well understand the conflict between flesh and spirit, and find very clearly that two spirits are within one another, one striving against the other; for one desires God, and the other desires bread. Both are profitable and good <note>The author suggests that while the spirit seeks God, the body's need for sustenance is also a natural, necessary part of earthly life.</note>.

24. But you, child of man, let this be spoken to you: lead your life circumspectly, and let the spirit of your soul be the master. Then you will have fought a good fight here, for this time is but short.

25. We all stand here in the field and grow; let everyone take care what fruit he bears. For at the end of the harvest, every work shall be put into its own granary.

26. It is better to labor a little while with toil and care in the vineyard and wait for the great wages and refreshment, than to be a king here for a little time and afterwards be a lion, a wolf, a dog, a cat, a toad, serpent, or worm in <margin>f Or shape.</margin> form <note>The author warns that one's inner character or "signature" in this life determines their spiritual form in the next.</note>.

27. O child of man! Think upon this; be warned. We speak very seriously, out of a wonderful eye <note>Likely referring to a state of spiritual vision or divine insight.</note>. You shall very shortly find it by experience; there is yet a little time, for the beginning has already found the end. This is a little rose out of the beginning; look now, and put covetousness out of your <margin>g Or sight.</margin> eyes, or else you shall wail and lament, and none will pity you. For what a man sows, that he must also reap. What will pomp and honor avail you when they leave you?

28. Here you are very powerful, but afterwards you shall be powerless. You are gods <note>A reference to Psalm 82:6, "I have said, Ye are gods; and all of you are children of the most High."</note>, and yet you run headlong to the Devil. Take pity on your own life and on your fair heavenly image.

29. Please, be the children of God and do not be devils. Let not the hypocrites keep you back by their <margin>h Or Example.</margin> flattery; they do it for their bellies, for their honors, and for money's sake. They are the servants of the Great *Babel* <note>original: *Babell*; used here to represent the "City of Confusion" or the corrupt, worldly religious systems.</note>.

30. Examine yourselves; ask your conscience whether it is in God. It will blame you and bid you drive the hypocrites from you, and seek the clear countenance of God, and look not through <margin>i Or Spectacles.</margin> a glass <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 13:12, "For now we see through a glass, darkly."</note>.

31. God is right before you; he is in you. Confess to him; come to him like the lost son <note>The Prodigal Son from the Gospel of Luke.</note>. There is no other who can take the *Turba* from you. You cannot enter into the other world except through death, where your hypocrites can never come; otherwise there is no forgiveness—

<summary>The author warns the reader to prioritize the spirit over the flesh, cautioning that earthly status is fleeting and that one's spiritual "fruit" determines their eternal state. He urges a rejection of religious hypocrisy in favor of a direct, internal relationship with God.</summary>
<keywords>flesh vs spirit, Turba, spiritual harvest, Babel, hypocrisy, repentance, Prodigal Son, inner conscience</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's discourse on the necessity of spiritual rebirth and the vanity of outward religious hypocrisy, transitioning into a new inquiry regarding the soul's departure from the body.</meta>

forgiveness of sin. And though you should give everything you own to your hypocrites, you would still be as much captivated in the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; refers to the "turmoil" or "disturbance" of the soul's lower, chaotic nature that leads to destruction</note> as you were before.

32. It is not a matter of someone standing ready to take <margin>God, fair words.</margin> away the *Turba* from you just because you give him fair words. No, no, it is a spiritual <note>original: "Magicall"; in this context, it refers to the inner, mystical workings of the soul rather than sorcery</note> thing; you must be born again, as Christ says, or else you cannot come to God. Do what you will, all hypocrisy is deceit.

33. If you would serve God, you must do it as the "New Man." The earthly Adam <note>The "Old Adam" or the unregenerate human nature</note> can do him no acceptable service. Let him sing, roar, call out, confess, pray, cry, and do whatever he will—it is all but fighting with a shadow. The will must be in it; the heart must wholly resign itself up into it. Otherwise, it is but a feigned babble and a fable of Antichrists, with which the whole earth is filled.

34. The will is greater and more powerful than much loud crying. It is able to destroy the *Turba* and to enter into the image of God; it has power to be the child of God. It can throw down mountains and raise the dead, if it be born in God and if the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note> gives it leave.

35. For a man must walk in obedience in great humility, and only cast his will into God’s will, so that God may be both the will and the deed in him. This is the way to salvation and to the Kingdom of Heaven, and no other. Let the Pope or the learned doctors preach otherwise—it is all but lies and mere hypocritical juggling.

***

# The Eighteenth Question

### ->How does the Soul depart from the Body at the Death of a Man?<-

->**1.**<-

<margin>A child of Grace can be found in her.</margin> **H**ere we would invite the world as a guest—especially Babel <note>Babylon; used here to represent the corrupt, worldly church</note> the Whore—to see whether she can be made a true child. For Death is a terrible host; he casts the proud rider and his horse to the ground.

2. My beloved friend, this is a very <margin>Or deep.</margin> difficult question, and it requires the eyes of all the "three principles" <note>The author's cosmological system involving the dark world, the light world, and the outer world</note> to see it well. Those who would enter in and behold this need not die; rather, they must be poison to Death and a pestilence to Hell. They must take Death captive if they wish to see it. No man’s understanding can otherwise find it out unless he enters into death himself, and then he will feel indeed what...

<summary>The author concludes that true salvation requires a total resignation of the will to God rather than outward rituals, then begins an inquiry into the nature of death and the soul's departure.</summary>
<keywords>New Man, Turba, hypocrisy, will of God, rebirth, Babel, three principles, death of the body</keywords>

--- Page 111 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of the nature of death and the soul's transition from the physical body to its eternal state.</meta>

# The eighteenth Question Answered.

Death is; he shall surely taste what it is, when one Principle (namely, the life) perishes.

3. You understood before that all <margin>Things, or substances.</margin> essences are <term>magical</term> <note>original: "Magicall"; here meaning produced by the creative power of the will or spirit rather than mere mechanical processes</note>, and that one is always the <margin>Or Resemblance.</margin> glass <note>original: "Glasse"; a mirror or reflection</note> of the other. In this glass, the desire of the first glass is opened and becomes an essence. Then also, the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "disturber" or the spirit of destruction and wrath that breaks down complex forms</note> is in every essence, which destroys everything until it comes to the first essence; that essence stands alone and has no destroyer. <margin>See the Book of the small six Points. Point 5. verse 65.</margin>

4. For there is nothing more beyond it; it cannot be broken. It is in itself and outside of itself, and goes wherever it will. Thus, it is everywhere in one single place, for it is in the <term>Abyss</term> <note>original: "Abysse"; the bottomless void or the groundless state of God's existence</note> where there is no place of rest; it must only rest in itself.

5. Now, if all essences have proceeded from one, then the beginning is also in the last essence. For the last has <margin>Or retired.</margin> gone back into the first and seeks the first, and finds it in itself. When it finds the first, it lets all the others go and dwells in the Limit, and there it can exist without <margin>Or pain.</margin> source <note>original: "source"; here meaning a cause of suffering or agitation</note>.

6. For there is nothing that makes it a <margin>Or dies, or falls away.</margin> source. It is itself the matter of the first essence, and although it may be something else, that is but the twig of itself—its own will and nothing else. For there is nothing that can give it another will.

7. Thus we give you to understand what dying is: the beginning seeks the Limit, and when it finds it, then it casts away the seeking—namely, the earthly life. That shall be cast away; it must break off from itself. <margin>Substance, or dross.</margin>

8. For the beginning (namely, the soul) continues in the Limit and lets the body perish. There is no complaining about it, nor does the soul desire the body anymore. The body must also go into its limit—namely, into the wonders of that which it has been. <margin>Substantiality, the Glory of the Majesty, the glorified body of Christ; and Adam's body before he slept: Sophia's wedding Garment.</margin>

9. For the spirit of the soul does not grieve when the body perishes, but the <term>fire-life</term> <note>the soul's essential energy or drive</note> grieves because the matter of the fire, which the fire has produced, also perishes—though only in essence.

10. The figure still continues in the will, for the will cannot be destroyed. Thus, the soul must continue in the will, and it takes the figure instead of matter and burns in the will. For the first glowing of the fire does not pass away, but it is quite deprived of the matter of the earthly life—namely, of the <term>Phur</term> <note>original: "Phur"; likely a reference to the "Sulphur" or the material substance that fuels the fire of life</note>.

11. And thus the fire becomes weak and passes into darkness, unless the spirit has heavenly <term>essentiality</term> <note>spiritual substance</note>—namely, the divine body. Then the fire (namely, the true soul) receives that mild body as a <margin>Or glorified body.</margin> <term>Sulphur</term> <note>the fuel for the spiritual fire</note>, and so the soul burns in the love-fire and is quite gone out from the first <margin>Namely, the elementary fire of the outward Nature.</margin> fire-life.

12. It is now in God's Principle. The first <margin>The fire of the wrath of the outward earthly life.</margin> wrathful fire cannot touch it in eternity, for it has received another source and is truly life.

<summary>This page explains the process of death as the soul's return to its original essence, shedding the earthly body and its "fire-life" to find rest in God's love-fire. It describes how the soul transitions from material existence to a spiritual state, protected from the "wrathful fire" of nature.</summary>

# The Eighteenth Question Answered

Truly born again, such a person knows no more of the first life, for it is <margin>Or Magic.</margin> swallowed up in the *Magia* <note>original: *Magia*; here referring to the supernatural or spiritual power that underlies all reality</note>.

13. The *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; the "turmoil" or destructive principle in nature that breaks things down</note> remains in the earthly body. It has again become what it was before the body existed—namely, a "Nothing," a *Magia*, wherein all its essences remain in form as if in a mirror. Yet they are not physical, but exist in the manner of eternity. We know that all the wonders that existed before this world stood in a mystery—namely, in the Virgin <margin>Or made one again.</margin> of Wisdom—yet they were without physical substance.

14. Therefore, we understand here that this mystery has been so manifested in its division that it cannot be extinguished in eternity. Instead, it remains eternally in distinction and partition. It is beheld in the Magic, in that state of division, exactly as it formed itself here on earth.

<margin>Or the parting of the soul from the body.</margin> 15. Thus we may perceive that the separation <note>death</note> occurs because the *Turba* has reached the limit of the physical essence. For a sickness leading to death is nothing else but the *Turba* having inflamed itself, seeking to destroy the essence. It has reached its limit and will cast away that which was introduced <margin>Or the middle state between the beginning and the end.</margin> in between.

16. And this is also the cause of the body's death: the *Turba* thrusts itself into the fire, and so the outward life is extinguished. It withdraws the fire of the soul, and so the soul passes into its own <margin>Sky, receptacle, or subtle atmosphere.</margin> Ether <note>original: *Æther*; the spiritual or celestial element</note> and reaches its limit.

17. And if the fire of the soul does not possess the Divine body in its spirit, nor in its will and desire, then it is a dark fire. It burns in anguish and great horror, for it possesses nothing but the first four forms of nature <note>The four lower qualities of existence in Boehme’s cosmology: contraction, expansion, rotation, and fire/friction</note> in a state of distress.

18. If the will is entirely void of the power of humility, then there is no sinking downward or inward through death into life. <margin>Anxious, mad, senseless, giddy wheel.</margin> Instead, it is like a tormenting, hurrying wheel which continually tries to rise upward, yet falls downward on the other side. It has the quality of fire, but not the actual burning of fire.

<margin>Or Astringency, the first Form.</margin> 19. For the *Turba* is an exceeding strong harshness and bitterness. The bitterness continually seeks the <margin>The second form.</margin> fire and would evaporate it, but the <margin>The fourth and third forms.</margin> astringency <note>The contracting, "holding" force</note> holds it captive. Thus, it is only a horrible <margin>The third and fourth forms.</margin> anguish. It continually turns within itself like a wheel and imagines, but finds nothing but itself. It draws itself into itself and makes itself pregnant; it eats itself and is its own substance.

<margin>Or what it did or wrought.</margin> 20. It has no other substance but that which the spirit of the soul continually <margin>Cruelty.</margin> created in the outward life: namely, covetousness, pride, cursing, swearing, reviling, backbiting, slandering, <margin>Rage or furnace.</margin> murder, hatred, wrath, anger, and falsehood. This is its food, sport, and <margin>Work or business.</margin> pastime. For the *Turba* in the will takes this substance with it; its works follow it.

<summary>This page describes the process of death as the "Turba" (destructive principle) reaching its limit and extinguishing the outward life. It explains that a soul without divine humility becomes trapped in its own negative passions, which become its eternal, self-consuming reality.</summary>

<keywords>Turba, Magia, death, soul's fire, four forms of nature, astringency, afterlife, spiritual anatomy, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of the soul's state after death and the nature of spiritual suffering, following the discussion of the soul's departure from the body.</meta>

21. And although it has done some good, yet that is done only in <margin>Or hypocrisy.</margin> a glittering show and appearance from an ambitious mind. Afterwards it continues in this way: in its ambition and constant endeavor to climb up, it always elevates itself. It would continually be above Meekness, and yet it neither knows it nor feels it. It is an incessant elevation above God, and yet an eternal depression; it seeks a solid ground, and there is none. This is its life.

22. Yet, if it had grasped any purity of Love in its will (as many a person does who is converted at last in his end), then it sinks into itself through the anguish. For the humble spark falls down through death into Life, and then the <margin>Or pain.</margin> source of the soul's suffering ends; yet it is but a small twig budding forth into the Kingdom of God.

23. It cannot be sufficiently described what refining the soul undergoes, and how it is hindered and plagued by the Devil before it can get this spark into itself. But this "wise" world will not believe this; it is too wise, and yet it is so stark blind it understands nothing, but hangs continually to the literal word. Oh, that none might feel this by experience! We would gladly hold our peace.

24. We speak not here of any strange <margin>Or pain.</margin> source of suffering, but only of that which is in the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; refers to the "Turba Gentium" or the "disturber," a term used by Boehme to describe the wrathful, destructive principle in nature that breaks things apart.</note> and also of no other power of the Devil over the <margin>Or wicked.</margin> poor soul, but its own horror and abominable suggestions, by which the imagination of the soul is so tormented.

25. The condition of Hell is far different than *Babel* <note>original: *Babell*; a term used to describe the confused, divided, and worldly institutional churches.</note> teaches. She says that the Devil <margin>Whips.</margin> beats and torments the soul, but this is spoken in mere blindness. The Devil is not at odds with his own children; they must all do his will. The anguish and horror of Hell plagues every one of them sufficiently in their own abominations. Everyone has his own Hell; there is nothing but his own poison that seizes him.

26. The four forms of the original of nature are the common plague which everyone feels according to his own *Turba*, but one far differently than another. The covetous person has cold; the angry person has fire; the envious has bitterness; the proud has a high ambition and yet an eternal sinking and falling into the abyss. The <margin>Or blasphemer.</margin> scorner swallows down the *Turba* of those abominations which he belched forth here. The false, slandering heart has the fourth form, namely, the great <margin>Or aching.</margin> anguish.

27. For the *Turba* stands in the circle of the fire, namely, in the <margin>Idle words, unfaithfulness, or jeering.</margin> heart of the soul; and false speaking, lying, and untruths are an abomination and a gnawing, and make the soul curse itself.

28. A potentate <note>A powerful ruler or person of great authority.</note> who has oppressed the poor and consumed <margin>Or labor.</margin> their sweat in pride, rides in the curses of the poor in the height <margin>Or miseries.</margin> of fire; for all the necessities of the poor stick in him.

<summary>The author explains that Hell is not a place of external torture by devils, but an internal state where the soul is tormented by its own vices and the "Turba" or destructive principle. Each sin—such as pride, greed, or oppression—creates its own specific form of spiritual suffering after death.</summary>

29. He has no rest; his pride always climbs upward, and he behaves himself exactly as he did here on earth. He continually seeks and yet lacks all things. Whatever he had too much of in life, he has too little of there. He continually desires to devour his own essence, but he has none, for he is magical <note>original: "Magicall"; here meaning existing only as a mental or spiritual desire without physical substance</note>.

<margin>Or right.</margin>
30. He has lost his true image. He has the image, as it were, of a proud prancing horse, or of whatever else he delighted in. Whatever he took with him in his will, that is his image; where his heart is, there is his treasure also, and that remains for eternity.

31. But listen, friend, to what the Last Judgment will bring! Then all things shall pass through the fire, and the floor shall be swept clean, and every person shall have their own place; at this, the very devils themselves tremble.

## The nineteenth Question.

### *How is the Soul Mortal, and how immortal?*

->**I.**<-

A thing which has an eternal beginning also has an eternal end, and such is the essence of the soul.

2. Regarding the image which God created, and which has a temporal beginning: that is born out of the eternal and is placed in the eternal essence without pain <gloss>or suffering, or working property, or nature</gloss>.

3. And where there is no pain <gloss>source</gloss>, there is also no death. Even though there is a source of activity (as there is in Heaven), yet it exists in only one single will, and that has its foundation in eternity. Just as nothing exists there that can oppose it, so there is nothing that can break into it.

4. But where there is only one will (as in God, who is All in All), there is nothing that can oppose that will. There is no *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a term used by Boehme to describe the "disturber" or the spirit of destruction and wrath</note> there, for the will desires nothing but itself and its branches, which all stand in one tree, in one essence. The tree is its own beginning and its own end.

5. The soul came out of the mouth of God, and when the body dies, it goes again into the mouth of God. It is the essence within the Word, and the deed within the Will.

6. Now, who will condemn that which he has in his own body? Since the soul is in the Divine body, it is hidden in God from all evil, and who can find it? None but the Spirit of God, and one soul another, as well as the communion of angels.

<summary>This page discusses the restless, illusory state of the proud soul after death and explores the soul's immortality as an essence derived from the breath and will of God.</summary>
<keywords>pride, Last Judgment, immortality, Turba, Divine body, essence of the soul, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

--- Page 115 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the soul's immortality and the nature of eternal suffering or joy based on the soul's alignment with the divine or the "Turba."</meta>

<header>The nineteenth Question Answered.</header>

<margin>f</margin> 7. But the wicked souls have lost their divine Image within the Limit <note>original: "Limit," referring to the boundary of time or the physical state</note>, for it has entered into a boundary, and that boundary is the end of the Image. The *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*, the "disturber" or the power of divine wrath that destroys flawed forms</note> destroys the first Image and attracts the <margin>g</margin> essences <margin>g Or works.</margin> of the will to form a new image. This is also immortal, for Eternal Nature does not die, because it had no beginning.

8. If Eternal Nature were to die in the fire of Anger, then God’s Majesty would also be extinguished, and the "Eternal Something" would again become an "Eternal Nothing." That cannot be; whatever is from Eternity continues Eternally.

9. The false soul cannot awaken any other source of being except that which has existed from Eternity in the Eye of Anger—that is, in the Center of Nature.

10. All things have existed from Eternity, but essentially within the Essence, not in the physical Substance of the Essence. They were not substantial Spirits but spirits <margin>h</margin> in figure <margin>h Figurales</margin> <note>original: *Figurales*, meaning existing as potential forms or ideas</note>, without corporality. They have existed from Eternity as if in a *Magia* <note>original: *Magia*, a magical or spiritual potentiality</note>, where one thing swallowed up the other in the *Spiritus Magia* <note>original: *Spiritus Magia*, the Spirit of Magic or the creative power of the will</note>.

11. And a third thing has emerged from these two, according to the form of those two. There has been a wrestling from Eternity and a figured substance. The Creation has placed all things among the Wonders, so that now in Eternity, all things stand this way in the Eternal *Magia* among the Wonders.

<margin>Note the condition of the wicked souls, and of those infants who die in their mother's womb in innocence, before they commit actual sin.</margin> 12. Now, if the wicked souls had brought no substance into their wills, then they would have no pain; there would be no feeling but *Magia*. But the substance they formed is an Image, and that Image is caught in the *Turba*; therefore, there is a source of pain that can be felt.

13. There is a dying, and yet no dying, but rather a will to die—that is, an anguish in that substance which was brought into the will.

14. This is caused by the longing that all things have for God, and yet they are not able to reach Him. This causes anguish and sorrow for the wickedness they have allowed into themselves. The soul continually thinks: "Had you not done this or that, then you might have attained the Grace of God." This <margin>i</margin> evil substance causes Eternal Despair.

15. And thus we say no soul is mortal, whether it be in God or in Hell; its substance remains forever as part of God’s Wonders.

<summary>This page explains that the soul is inherently immortal because it originates from Eternal Nature, which cannot die without God Himself ceasing to exist. It describes how wicked souls suffer not because they cease to be, but because they have formed a "substance" of will that is trapped in eternal anguish and separation from God.</summary>

# The twentieth Question.

### *How does the Soul return to God again?*

<margin>Or breathed.</margin>
This has been already sufficiently cleared: that the soul was spoken <note>original: "spoken out of the Mouth of God"; referring to the divine breath of life in Genesis.</note> out of the Mouth of God and created by the Holy Spirit in the Image of God.

2. Now, if the soul continues in that state, then when it leaves this earthly life, it is already in the Mouth of God; for it exists within the Divine Body, and no <margin>Evil, pain, or hurt can come to it.</margin> source of suffering can touch it.

---

# The one and twentieth Question.

### *Where does the Soul go when it departs from the body, whether it be saved or not saved?*

He who rightly understands the three Principles <note>The "Three Principles" is a core concept in Boehme’s philosophy, representing the dark world of fire/wrath, the light world of love/majesty, and the external world of nature.</note> need not ask this question. For the soul does not depart out of the mouth—since it did not enter through the mouth—but it only leaves the earthly life. The *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "turmoil" or the destructive, awakening power of God's wrath that dissolves the physical body.</note> snatches away the earthly life, and then the soul remains in its own Principle.

<margin>Comprehend, enclose, keep, or withhold it.</margin>
2. For the body does not retain it; no wood or stone can retain it. It is thinner than the air, and if it possesses the Divine Body, then it goes straight as a conqueror through the *Turba*—that is, through the Anger of God and quite through Death. And when it has passed through, then it is in God’s <margin>Or substance.</margin> Essence.

<margin>Deeds and</margin>
3. It remains in its wonders and essences <note>original: "Essences"; here meaning the spiritual fruits or results of one's life.</note> which it wrought here. It beholds the Majesty of God and the Angels face to face.

4. Wherever it is, it is in the Abyssal world <note>original: "Abyssal world"; the "Unground" or the infinite, bottomless spiritual realm of God.</note> where there is no end nor limit. Where else should it go? "Where the carcass is, there the eagles gather together" <note>A reference to Matthew 24:28, implying the soul is naturally drawn to its source.</note>. It is in Christ’s flesh and blood, with Christ its Shepherd.

5. Even if it should go a thousand miles away, it would still be in the same place from which it started; for in God there is no limit. Near and far off are all one.

6. It is as swift as a thought; it is magical. It dwells in its wonders, and they are its house.

<summary>This page explains that the soul does not travel to a distant physical location after death, but rather exists within the spiritual principle it inhabited during life. A saved soul passes through the "Turba" (divine wrath/destruction) into the infinite essence of God, where distance and time do not exist.</summary>

<keywords>Soul, God, Holy Spirit, Divine Body, Turba, Anger of God, Essence, Abyssal world, Christ, afterlife, omnipresence</keywords>

--- Page 117 ---
7. The essentiality <note>original: "Essentiality"; here meaning the spiritual substance or "stuff" of heaven</note> that exists outwardly is Paradise. it is a springing, blossoming, and growing of all kinds of fair, heavenly fruits. Just as we have all kinds of fruit here in this world, which we eat in an earthly manner, there are also all kinds of fruits in Paradise which the soul may eat. These fruits have colors and virtues <note>original: "vertues"; meaning inherent powers or life-forces</note> within their substance; they are not merely like a thought. Although they are as thin and subtle as a thought, they are substantial, comprehensible, and tangible to the soul—full of power and sappy with the Water of Life—and all of this comes from the heavenly substantiality.

8. For the heavenly body of the soul is derived from the pure Element (from which the four earthly elements were brought forth), and that gives it flesh, while the Tincture <note>original: "Tincture"; a term from alchemy referring to the spiritual essence or "soul-light" that gives life and color</note> gives it blood. The heavenly man has flesh and blood, and Paradise is the power of that substantiality; it is heavenly Earth, which is incomprehensible to our outward, worldly reason.

9. But we will teach you another **A. B. C.** <note>A basic lesson or fundamental truth</note>. Not everyone in this world has Christ’s flesh hidden within them inside the "Old Adam" <note>The fallen, sinful nature of humanity</note>. Indeed, among many people, not one has it except for the regenerate—those who have turned away from their own will and into God’s will. In them, the noble grain of mustard seed is sown, out of which a tree has grown.

<margin>The small thread of Faith.</margin>
10. Most souls depart from the body without possessing Christ’s body; yet they hang on as if by a thread, and have at last entered into God's will through their faith. These souls are indeed in the "Image" in the spirit, but they are not yet in the "Flesh" <note>meaning they lack the glorified, substantial heavenly body</note>.

11. Such souls as these wait for the Last Day, when the Image (that is, the Body) shall come forth out of the grave, out of the first Image. For God will raise it up by the voice of Christ—even that Image which Adam had in his state of innocence, which has been washed with Christ’s blood.

<margin>Or works.</margin>
12. But the earthly body shall not touch it. That earthly body must appear before the Judgment in the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "turmoil" or "wrath" that destroys corrupted forms</note>; but after the sentence of the Judgment, the *Turba* shall swallow the earthly body up, and only its wonders <margin>Or works.</margin> shall remain.

<margin>Or Source.</margin>
13. You must understand us correctly: these souls that must wait until the Last Day for their bodies remain with their bodies in a quiet rest until the Last Day, without feeling any pain <margin>Or source of torment.</margin>, but they exist in another Principle <note>A different realm or state of existence</note>.

14. They have neither darkness nor majesty in the earth, but are at rest without pain in the eternal, still liberty, without touching the physical body.

<margin>Or works.</margin>
15. Yet they see their wonders <margin>Or works.</margin>, but they do nothing with them; for they wait for God and are in humility. They have sunk down through death and are in another world, yet there is a great space <margin>Gulf, or distance.</margin> and distance between them and the holy souls who already dwell in Christ’s flesh.

<summary>Boehme describes the substantial nature of Paradise and the different states of souls after death, distinguishing between those who have already attained a "heavenly body" and those who must wait in a peaceful, intermediate rest until the resurrection.</summary>
<keywords>Paradise, heavenly body, resurrection, Turba, mustard seed, regeneration, intermediate state, substantiality</keywords>
