and Blood; but not a separate Principle, for they exist within one and the same Principle.

<margin>Or Power.</margin>
16. But a spirit without a body does not have that power which a spirit within a body possesses; therefore, they rest and are "under the Altar of God." <note>A reference to the souls of martyrs waiting for justice.</note>

<margin>Revelation 6:9, 10, 11.</margin>
17. When the Last Day comes, then they shall come forth and eat of the Bread of God, and put on the Divine Body, as is mentioned in the Revelation of *John*; where the souls under the altar, clothed in white, say: "Lord, when will you avenge our blood?" And it was answered to them that they should rest a little while longer, until the number of their brothers who were to be killed for the testimony of Jesus was complete.

<margin>
The innermost is the outer darkness.
Essence or works.
Essence and feeling.
Show juggling tricks <note>original: "jugling tricks"; meaning deceptive illusions or supernatural feats.</note>.
Or Spirit of the Air.
Or Rest.
Or Pomp and show.
</margin>
18. But the souls of the wicked have another place—namely, in the innermost region, which is also the outermost of all darkness. They dare not travel up and down; they remain merely with the body in their essence <note>original: "substance"</note>, yet they are not in this world, nor do they touch the Earth.

19. Such a soul indeed has power enough over the Earth; it can open the earth without physical substance or being felt. But it does not possess the outward Principle <note>The physical/material realm</note>; it does not have power over the outward Spirit. Yet, for a time, it can create apparitions within the sidereal spirit <note>term: sidereal spirit; the "star-spirit" or astral body that mediates between the soul and the physical world.</note>.

20. Many such souls appear again in the astral spirit and seek relief <note>original: "Abstinence"; here meaning a cessation of their state or a seeking of rest</note>, and they make many people afraid by causing disturbances in houses. They do all of this through the astral spirit until that spirit is eventually consumed; then their tricks cease and lie in the darkness, and they await the Last Judgment.

21. Our *Babel* <note>term: Babel; the author's term for the confused and corrupt organized church systems of his day.</note> says it is the Devil who goes up and down in the shape of the soul. Indeed, the damned soul has enough of the Devil in it, but it is not the Devil himself. He is in the Abyss and willingly torments the soul within the soul's own abyss during the life of the body.

22. Neither does the Devil entirely lack a disguise for his knavery, for he can put on an outward cloak to seduce or terrify men.

23. But this is the complaint we have against *Babel*: that she is so extremely blind and has so little knowledge of God. She has cast away Magic and Philosophy <note>note: Here, "Magic" refers to the divine creative power and "Philosophy" to the true study of nature and God.</note> and has received the Antichrist. Now she has lost her understanding; she still possesses a kind of academic art, but her true understanding has failed her completely. She has broken the looking glass and now peers through the holes of her spectacles.

24. What shall we say? The world is blindfolded; it is led by a string and taken captive, yet it does not see it. It would be at liberty if it could only see its condition once. The snare with which it is bound is malicious knavery. You shall soon be made to see: it is broad daylight; only wake up, you Keeper of *Israel*.

<summary>The author describes the differing states of righteous and wicked souls after death, explaining how the wicked can manifest as ghosts through the "astral spirit." He concludes by criticizing the institutional church (Babel) for losing its spiritual understanding and true philosophy.</summary>
<keywords>afterlife, astral spirit, sidereal spirit, Babel, apparitions, Last Judgment, spiritual blindness, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

--- Page 119 ---
25. Thus, my beloved friend, know that there is a difference in the places where souls reside, according to the state into which the soul has entered. If it is holy and **regenerate** <note>spiritually reborn</note>, then it possesses a <margin>The body of Christ.</margin> body which only awaits the <margin>Or works.</margin> wonders of the physical body at the Last Day. The soul has <margin>Conceived or formed.</margin> already grasped these wonders within its will, but at the Day of Judgment, it must stand before the tribunal.

26. All souls, both good and bad, shall each receive their sentence and reward. The holy shall be placed in the presence of the wicked, so that the wicked may see and <margin>Or taste.</margin> feel the cause of their <margin>Or source.</margin> pain.

27. If anyone should imagine a specific residence or place where souls must gather or sit together, that contradicts the rule of the **Magia** <note>original: *Magia*; referring here to the spiritual or divine magic/logic by which God's will is enacted</note>. Every soul is in its own country and is not bound to the location of its former physical body; it may be wherever it wishes. Wheresoever it is, it exists either in God or in darkness.

28. God is everywhere, and darkness is everywhere; the angels also are everywhere, each within its own **principle** <note>a fundamental realm or state of being</note> and in its own <margin>Or sour.</margin> property.

29. The fiction of outward reason, lacking the knowledge of these principles, is <margin>Or a false glass, a conceit.</margin> like fighting with a shadow. If I were to ask a thousand times and were always told something directly from God himself, yet remained only in flesh and blood, I would still view it as **Babel** <note>referring to the "City of Confusion" or the misguided, worldly church</note> does. Babel supposes that the soul flies into a heaven above the stars; I do not know that heaven they speak of, and I have no desire to go there.

30. Heaven is indeed "above," but that is where the **Angelic Principalities** and Thrones reside. This eye of the <margin>Skies, mansions, or spheres.</margin> **Ether** <note>the spiritual atmosphere or divine element</note> is our own principality and kingdom.

31. The same essence that is with those above is also with us, but our creation and essence are within our own Ether. A soul may go to the angels if it earnestly desires, and the angels of God will lovingly entertain it.

32. For the same essence of God which is with us is also with them. This is the only difference: they have among them angelic works that are wholly pure and without blemish, while we have the "great wonders" <note>likely referring to the complex struggle between good and evil in the material world</note>. Therefore, they long to be with us; furthermore, they are our servants during the life of the body and they withstand the Devil.

33. Now, if the angels are in this world within the holy principle, where then should the soul fly first, as Babel might think? Perhaps into pride, as **Lucifer** did? Oh no! They continue in humility and look <margin>What God does.</margin> upon God’s wonders; as God’s Spirit moves, so do they.

<summary>The author explains that heaven and hell are not distant geographic locations but internal spiritual states or "principles" present everywhere. He argues that holy souls remain in God's presence and interact with angels here in the "Ether" rather than flying to a distant heaven above the stars.</summary>
<keywords>soul, afterlife, heaven, hell, angels, Magia, Ether, Babel, Lucifer, Day of Judgment</keywords>

## The Twenty-Second Question
### *What does every soul do? Does it rejoice until the Day of Judgment?*

---

->1.<-

<margin>Or Triumphant.</margin>
This question contains the exceedingly joyful Gate of Glory, leading to the knowledge of the victorious garland <note>original: "garland," referring to a crown of victory or laurel wreath</note> of the soul.

2. When a beloved son travels far away into a foreign country to study art or seek honor, he often thinks of home and of the time when he shall enjoy the company of his parents and friends. He rejoices at the thought of that day and expects it with inward joy and longing. He also applies himself diligently to his business so that he may acquire the skills and knowledge that will make his parents, kindred, and friends rejoice.

<margin>The body of Christ.
Or works which it did here.</margin>
3. Think of this comparison and take it into consideration: it is exactly the same with the soul. The souls without the body <note>The "body of Christ" here refers to the glorified, spiritual body the soul will inhabit.</note> have a great inward joy. They wait for the Last Day with great inward desire, when they shall again receive their fair and holy body along with its wonders <note>original: "wonders," referring to the spiritual fruits or manifestations of their earthly lives.</note>.

4. Also, their ornament is in their will, where they behold their works according to the manner of the Eternal Abyssal Magic <note>original: "Abyssal Magick," referring to the divine creative power or the "Mysterium Magnum" from which all things are manifested.</note>. They shall first receive these works in physical form at the Last Day, appearing in the figure of the new body arising out of the old.

<margin>Or fully.</margin>
5. Also we know and deeply perceive—yet in the Spirit only, according to its knowledge—that the blessed souls do rejoice in the labor which they undertook here. They exceedingly refresh themselves in their wonders which they see magically. For those who have led many to righteousness have their reward in the *Magic* <note>original: "Magia," the spiritual mirror or creative imagination of God.</note>, present in the will before their eyes.

<margin>Or Crown of Victory.</margin>
6. Those who have suffered much persecution for the sake of the Truth see their bright triumphant garland, which they shall put on upon the new body at the Last Day.

7. Those who have done much good see that plainly shining in the will.

8. Those who have been scorned, despised, persecuted, and slain for the sake of Christ’s doctrine, honor, and Truth see the triumphant victory. They are like one who has overcome his enemy in a fight and then displays his victory triumphantly to his Prince or King. For this, he receives exceeding great glory when his King receives him with great joy and keeps him as his faithful assistant.

<summary>This page explains that the souls of the righteous experience a state of profound inward joy and anticipation after death, viewing their earthly good works as spiritual "wonders" while they await the resurrection of their glorified bodies.</summary>
<keywords>soul, Day of Judgment, afterlife, Magia, spiritual body, rewards of righteousness, persecution, victory garland</keywords>

--- Page 121 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the state of blessed souls after death as they await the final Resurrection.</meta>

9. We have no pen that can write of the exceeding joy that is in them. We only know this: that most of those souls have put on the Divine Body while still in this world, and so they possess greater perfection than others. They await the Last Day with great joy and glory, when their works shall be presented to them and set before their eyes in heavenly forms; meanwhile, the wicked—<margin>against whom they have been tyrannized</margin>—shall see those very souls against whom they once rebelled.

10. Every soul rejoices before the face of God in great hope of what it shall receive again, for it knows its reward. However, without the body, the soul cannot fully receive it; for it performed its works while in the body, and therefore its works follow it into the New Body and are returned to it.

11. For although the exceedingly precious, holy souls have put on Christ’s body in this world—so that they already stand in Heaven, namely, in the Image of God—all their works were nevertheless performed in the old body, which was God’s mirror. In the Resurrection, these works shall be represented in their true heavenly form within <margin>the old body <note>The author refers to the transformed or resurrected physical vessel.</note></margin> that body.

12. For the first Image—which Adam was before the Fall—is regenerated in Christ and shall be placed upon the soul again along with its wonders. Although the soul possessed the Divine Body before, the <margin>works <note>original: "wonders"</note></margin> wonders remain within that first Image.

13. But the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; a term used by Boehme to describe the "disturber" or the element of wrath and destruction in nature.</note>, along with the outward kingdom of the external source, is gone. For <margin>the first Image</margin> it was a mirror and has now become a "wonder." It lives without a physical spirit as a wonder and shall be placed upon the soul in great <margin>clarity, transfiguration, or brightness</margin> glory, which it shall receive from the Light of God. The holy souls rejoice in this exceedingly and await it with great longing.

14. You must know that every blessed soul trims its lamp so that it may willingly meet its Bridegroom <note>A biblical metaphor for Christ.</note> at the Last Day. The soul always renews its will and thinks of how it shall rejoice with all holy people and angels in its new body amidst these wonders. There is a continual springing up of joy in them when they think of what is to come, each according to their own virtues.

15. And just as their works were different upon Earth, so also is their hope. For a day-laborer who has worked much rejoices at his wages; so it is here. There is a friendly essence among them and within them.

16. All the scorn and disgrace placed upon those who were innocent becomes a great, triumphant glory to them, because they suffered in innocence and clothed themselves in patient hope. They still wear this patience; death cannot take it away or strip it off, but the soul takes with it whatever it has <margin>conceived <note>original: "wrought" or "labored for"</note></margin> conceived.

17. Its many heartfelt prayers, wishes, and good deeds done in love for its neighbor are the food which the soul eats and joyfully enjoys,
->until<-

...until its new body shall eat paradisiacal fruit.

18. But those who have put on the Divine Body here in this life eat at God’s table without ceasing. Yet, the paradisiacal fruit belongs to the "body of the wonders" <note>original: "body of the Wonders" — referring to the spiritualized physical body that reflects God's creative power</note> which shall rise out of the grave and which was originally created in Paradise. For that body was made out of the Beginning, and it brings the End—along with all its <margin>Or works.</margin> wonders—back into the Beginning again.

19. Do not wonder, nor think that we understand it as if there were two separate bodies for the holiest saints; for they are not two, but one. Rather, consider that God’s essentiality <note>original: "Essentiality" — God's substantial or spiritual being</note> fills all things, and that is the Divine Body which is placed upon <margin>The great holy souls.</margin> holy souls even during this life.

20. For they cast their own will into God’s will, and so they receive the Divine Body which fills all things. Their will dwells in the Divine Body and eats of God’s Word, of God’s fruit, and of God’s <margin>Or power.</margin> virtue <note>original: "vertue" — inherent power or divine energy</note> within that Divine Body; and Christ is in God, and God has become Christ.

21. And so they <margin>Or are clothed with.</margin> wear Christ’s body in God, yet they still wait for <margin>Namely: The works and deeds which were done by the elementary body during the whole life.</margin> their first Adamicall <note>original: "Adamicall" — relating to the first man, Adam, in his unfallen state</note> holy body, along with the wonders, which shall be restored to them with a paradisiacal <margin>Or quality.</margin> property.

22. For God’s purpose must stand: He created the first body <margin>Or in.</margin> for Paradise, intending it to remain there eternally, and so it must return there again. The soul must remain upon the "cross of the ternary" <note>original: "Crosse of the Ternary" — a complex term in Boehme's theology referring to the intersection of the spiritual and material worlds, or the Trinity manifested in nature</note> in the mouth of God from whence it came. Yet the whole person continues with body and soul joined together, for God fills all in all.

23. Oh, if only we had a human pen and were able to write this into the spirit of your soul according to our knowledge! Oh, how many would then return out of *Sodom* and *Gomorrah*, out of *Babel* <note>original: "Babel" — used here to represent the confusion of false religious systems and worldly chaos</note>, and out of the covetous, proud vale of misery—which, despite its appearance, is nothing but anguish, pain, fear, vexation, and horror.

24. And here we shall let you know, so that you may deeply consider it, what the lamentable and miserable condition of the damned souls is, and what they have to expect. We will speak of it only briefly here, as the following question deals with it at length.

25. Their expectation is like that of an imprisoned criminal who continually listens whenever anything stirs, wondering when the executioner will come to carry out the judgment and give him his "reward." They do exactly the same.

26. They have a false conscience which gnaws at them, and their sins are set continually before their eyes. They also see their works "magically" <note>original: "Magically" — in this context, seeing the internal essence or spiritual reality of their past actions</note>; they see all their unrighteousness and vanities, their immeasurable pride and haughtiness, their oppression of the poor, and their mocking and suppression of others.

27. Their false belief flies away from them; their hypocrisy was on—

It contrasts this heavenly peace with the psychological torment and "magical" realization of sin experienced by damned souls.</summary>

# The Twenty-Second Question Answered

27. It is merely a deceitful glass; it did not reach the heart of God. It stands visibly before them in the magic <note>original: *Magick*; here referring to the creative power of the will or imagination</note>—that is, in their own will. But when they search within it, they stir up the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; a term used by Boehme to describe the "disturber" or the spirit of wrath and destruction</note> of the fire, which will always consume the glass, and then they are filled with fear and horror.

28. For they see and know that all things must be tested on the Last Day by the eternal fire of God’s anger; and they feel very well that their works will remain trapped in that fire.

29. The devils also tremble exceedingly when they consider their fall, which rests in God’s judgment regarding what he will do; of this, the Holy Scripture tells us plainly enough—especially the Judge, Christ himself.

30. Thus, know that the totally miserable condition of the damned is this: when they should be trimming their lamps to meet their Bridegroom <note>A reference to the Parable of the Ten Virgins in Matthew 25</note> at his coming, they tremble and try to smother all their works, which the *Turba* nevertheless sets before their eyes.

31. But now, those souls which are damned in a high degree are very presumptuously bold; they reject God and curse him, and are his <gloss>most malicious</gloss> enemies. <margin>Most malicious.</margin>

32. They believe their cause to be just; they oppose God with daring impudence and think: "Is there fire? Then we are fire. Is there a <gloss>rising, boiling property</gloss>?" <margin>Rising, boiling properties.</margin> "Then we will climb up above God and Heaven in the source of the fire. What do we care for humility? We will have the strength and might of the fire; we will be above God and perform wonders by our own power."

33. "We have the root; God has only the radiance <note>original: *Glance*</note>. Let us be lords; God shall be the servant. Our <gloss>mother is the wrath of the eternal nature</gloss>, and we will overthrow his strong tower all at once." <margin>The wrath of the eternal nature.</margin>

34. They have the mindset of soldiers who scale forts and walls and think the city is theirs, though in truth they lose their lives and never capture it.

35. You must understand that Hell is against Heaven, and the inhabitants of Hell are against the inhabitants of Heaven; and even this is a great wonder in God, for all things contribute to his glory.

<summary>This page describes the psychological state of the damned, ranging from those paralyzed by the "Turba" (divine wrath) to those who defiantly and delusionally attempt to overthrow God's power using the strength of the "fire."</summary>

### The Twenty-Third Question.

### *Whether do the souls of the wicked, without distinction, feel any ease or refreshment during the long time before the Day of Judgment?*

A thing that enters into an eternal beginning is also in an eternal end. Who can put anything into the hand of someone who is far away and not present where the action is to be done? He must give it only into the hand of one who is nearby to receive it. And that thing which has gone out of itself by its own will can receive nothing within itself, because it desires nothing within itself.

2. This is how it is with the wicked person in this world: he has gone out of himself with his will into covetousness, pride, and pleasure; into vices, overeating, heavy drinking, and sexual immorality. His will is continually bent on despising the poor, and on plaguing and treading down the poor through his authority, to their scorn and disgrace.

3. He has corrupted judgment with lies and bribes, and has continually swallowed down unrighteousness as a cow drinks water. <margin>Or cruel.</margin> All that has come from him has been bitter anger, which he pursued as far as his power would reach. His will has been mere lawlessness; he has done whatever he pleased. He has danced to the Devil’s own tune and has wholly entered into covetousness. He has considered his money and property to be his treasure, and his will has continually entered into them.

4. He has never retired into himself to seek after love, much less humility. He has esteemed the needy as his footstool and has crushed them without mercy. He has considered it his "art" and "wit" when he was able to grip and overreach the simple-minded and deprive them of the fruits of their labor.

5. He has supposed that he found the finest policy when he <margin>Had obtained a public office.</margin> could manage his business so securely that he might do whatever he wished; then he thought himself very cunning and possessed of great wisdom.

6. All of this—and much more—he has conceived in his will, and the image of the spirit of the soul has been filled with it. All of this remains as his character and form; and whenever the body <margin>Or returns to the Earth.</margin> dies, then the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; in Boehme’s philosophy, this refers to the "turmoil" or "disturbance"—the destructive, wrathful principle that breaks things apart.</note> lays hold of this in the spirit.

<summary>This page discusses the spiritual state of the wicked after death, arguing that because they focused their wills entirely on worldly vices and cruelty during life, their souls remain trapped in those same destructive patterns after death.</summary>
<keywords>wicked souls, Day of Judgment, covetousness, Turba, spiritual image, greed, oppression of the poor</keywords>

--- Page 125 ---
7. And then, if the spirit would gladly enter into itself, the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; refers to the "disturber" or the spirit of wrath and destruction that breaks things apart</note> goes with it and seeks the ground—that is, the root of the soul—and so the fire is only <margin>inflamed</margin> kindled by it.

8. And you must know that the souls of the wicked have no ease. Their best ease and joy is when they climb up in their own will, in <margin>employment or office</margin> the works they did here on earth, and they continually desire to do them even more. It grieves them that they did not afflict honest people more than they did; their will remains exactly as it was here.

9. They are spirits of pride, like the Devil—a covetous, devouring spirit which devours the abominations they committed here. Their only joy is to think of how they will despise God and be their own lords; this is their recreation and refreshing, and nothing else.

10. For how should they receive any other refreshing? They dare not for shame lift up their eyes to God, nor dare they fly to the Saints whom they scorned while on earth. They are ashamed to do that, for their falsehood continually strikes them in the face, and their malice and falsehood boil up from eternity to eternity.

11. When even the slightest thought of the Last Day comes into their minds, fear and horror stir within them. They would rather leave that thought alone and instead find recreation in their own haughtiness.

12. And this is also a wonder—and the greatest wonder of all—that an <margin>an image of love</margin> angel should become such a <margin>mad, senseless image of anger and wrath</margin> furious devil. In this way, the power of God’s anger becomes manifest in God. For God has manifested himself according to both <margin>the eye of love and the eye of wrath</margin> eyes, in love and in anger; and it is left free to man—he may go into whichever of them he chooses. God throws no one into wrath; the soul casts itself into it.

13. But you must know that the wrath has set its <margin>jaws</margin> throat wide open and draws mightily, desiring to devour everything; for it is covetousness and pride insulting humility.

14. And likewise, Love and Humility have opened their mouth and draw with all their powers, wishing to draw man into love, into Heaven.

15. Now, whichever of these the soul enters, in that it remains and grows, whether in love or in anger. In that tree it stands, and there is no deliverance from there in eternity. Here in this life, the soul stands in a balance in the <term>angle</term> <note>original: "Angle"; likely referring to a corner, a turning point, or a state of transition/decision</note>, and even if it has been evil, it may be born again in love. But when the angle breaks, then it is gone; it is afterwards in its own country, in its own <term>principle</term> <note>original: "Principle"; in Böhme's theology, this refers to one of the fundamental realms of existence (Light, Dark, or the Material world)</note>.

16. Who shall break that which is eternal, where no breaker can be found? For the soul is its own maker. From where shall another *Turba* come, when a thing is in eternity where there is no limit?

17. But so that you may see, despite all this, that God does not will evil: He

But if you go on, you shall hereafter surely taste the hellish dregs.

19. We teach you the Cross, and the Devil teaches you pleasure. Now you may take which you will; you shall have it, whether it be Love or wrath.

20. We labor for you, but you despise us. What more should we do for you? We are even your very slaves; if you will not accept us, let it be at your own peril. Go away with that which is your own, and we will take that which is ours, and so we are parted forever.

21. We will nevertheless work in our day-labor and do what is commanded of us. At the Harvest <note>A biblical metaphor for the Day of Judgment</note> we shall appear before one another, and then you will know us; you will do that to yourselves there which you have done to us here. We will not hide this from you, but tell you what we see.

# The twenty-fourth Question.
## Whether do men's wishes profit them anything? Or sensibly <margin><sup>y</sup> Help, or do them good.</margin> avail them, or not?

My beloved friend, look upon the Rich man and poor *Lazarus* <note>A reference to the parable in Luke 16:19-31</note>; there you shall find that there is a great <margin><sup>z</sup> Or Gulf. Luke 16:26.</margin> space between them and us, so that those who would reach them with their prayers and their will cannot; neither can they come to us, for there is a Principle <note>A fundamental spiritual boundary or state of being</note> between us.

<margin>
Psalm 49:7-8, 14.
<sup>a</sup> Or deliverance.
</margin>
2. The prayer and wish of the righteous pierces into Heaven, and not into Hell. The Scripture also tells you that out of Hell there is no <term>redemption</term> <note>original: "Redemption"; meaning there is no release or salvation once a soul is damned</note>. They lie in Hell like dead bones; they call, and no man hears them; no praying avails them.

3. And though many men should pray for the damned souls, yet their prayers remain in their own Principle and pass into Heaven, and not into Hell. There is no calling back again out of Hell, says the Scripture.

4. You know what Christ said to his seventy disciples: "When you enter..."

# The Twenty-Fourth Question Answered

...enter into a house, greet the house, and if the "Child of Peace" <note>A person receptive to spiritual peace; see Luke 10:6.</note> is in that house, then your greeting and wish shall rest upon it; if not, your wish returns to you again. And so it is also here.

5. No good wish enters into Hell. But if the wicked person leaves behind much falsehood and deceit, so that hellish torment is wished upon them in the grave, such wishes reach the wicked soul; those wishes come to pass for them. For that soul must swallow down the abominations it committed here; that is the "food" which the living send after it.

6. But it is altogether unfit and does not become the children of God at all to do this; for by doing so, they sow into Hell, into the anger of God. They had better beware lest they also reap what they sow; most certainly, if they do not retract their words and repent, it will happen no other way.

7. Furthermore, we give you to understand according to our knowledge in the Spirit—not according to the opinion and conceit of the outward man, but according to our gift—that those souls <margin>The thread of faith, which is small and weak.</margin> which, as it were, hang by a thread and only at the last moment enter into repentance (and so grasp the Kingdom of Heaven as if by a thread, so that doubting and faith are mixed), are in such a condition that a hearty prayer and wish results in their profit. Such prayer <margin>Condition, property, or misery.</margin> pierces into the poor captive soul, into its source <note>original: *source* or *qualitas*; the internal state or "quality" of the soul's existence.</note>, if the prayer is made with all earnestness.

8. For such a soul is neither in Hell nor in Heaven, but in the Gate, in the middle source of the "Principle" <note>A term in Boehme’s cosmology referring to the fundamental realms of existence (Darkness/Fire or Light/Love).</note> where Fire and Light separate. It is held by its *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; the "disturber" or the wrathful, destructive power in nature that seeks to dissolve forms.</note> that always seeks <margin>Or to devour it.</margin> the fire. But <margin>Or grasped.</margin> then this small twig which the soul has conceived—namely, its weak faith—submerges itself deeply and earnestly reaches after the mercy of God. It yields itself patiently into the death of that sinking <margin>Or source.</margin> downward; and so it escapes from the anguish and sinks down from the pain into the meekness of Heaven.

9. And although many a soul is held for a <margin>Or a long, tedious time.</margin> sufficient while, yet the Anger cannot devour 그 small faith, but must at last let it go.

10. But I leave it to the individual to discover what this experience is like—he who willfully persists in sin until his end and only then desires to be saved, thinking <margin>Priest or Minister.</margin> the Pope, forsooth, must save him. He shall find out the truth by woeful experience.

11. We say that a person's hearty, fervent prayer assists such a soul; for a zealous, earnest, faithful prayer has power to break open the gates of the Deep. It breaks open a whole Principle and searches; and if there is anything there capable of receiving its will, that thing takes hold of it. That is to say, the poor soul in its source of sin receives the earnest, divine will of its loving brother, and so is strengthened;

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that while curses feed the torment of the wicked, earnest prayer can assist "weak" souls caught between heaven and hell, helping them sink away from the wrathful 'Turba' into divine meekness.</summary>

# The twenty-fourth Question Answered.

11. [The soul is] strengthened; and in its brother's spirit and will, it is able to sink down out of the **Anguish** <note>original: "Anguish" — referring to the state of spiritual torment or the "wrathful" nature of the soul apart from God</note>, through Death, and attain the **Kingdom of God**.

12. But in its glorification, this [external help] cannot assist it; for the soul shines forth out of its own essence and will. The soul of a neighbor goes no further than death with it—yet it is not the neighbor's soul itself, but the spirit and will of his soul that does so. At that point, the Anger parts, the soul is released from the wrath, and then the spirit enters again into its own soul.

13. In **Popery** <note>A derogatory term for the Roman Catholic Church</note>, much juggling has been invented about this, such as saying **Mass** for a soul, and doing so only for money. This has been a great cheat of the Pope of *Babel* <note>Babel: A term used by the author to represent the "confused" or corrupt institutional church</note>; for earnestness is required to wrestle with the Anger of God and overcome it.

14. Yet we confess and acknowledge readily that the Church of Christ has great power to ransom such a soul, if it does so with fervency and earnestness. This was done in the **Primitive Church** <note>The early Christian church of the Apostles</note>, when they had holy people and holy priests who performed their ministry in real earnestness.

15. They indeed accomplished something, but not in the way the Pope boasts of, saying that he has the Key and that he can let out <margin>Or Pardon.</margin> a soul with his blessing whenever he wishes, if a man will only give him money. This is a lie.

<margin>The Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"</note></margin> 16. If he is holy, then he carries the **Great Mystery**; he is a shepherd of Christ set over his lambs. But then he must, along with the congregation, urge God in great humility and earnestness to relieve the poor soul—not for money.

17. There is always greed in money, and it never reaches the earnest <margin>The first most inward Ground.</margin> **Principle** <note>The deep spiritual foundation of reality</note>. The prayer of the greedy goes into his <margin>Or Christ.</margin> chest <note>original: "Hutch" — a storage chest or box</note>. We say that all service done in the Church of Christ for money belongs to **Antichrist** in *Babel*, for <margin>Or they set their hearts upon it.</margin> their hearts depend upon it. It would be better if men gave them food, drink, and necessities, but no money; then they would not set their hearts so much upon it.

18. What can a spirit seek and find in the Mystery when it is not itself in the Mystery? Oh, there is a great deceit in that! When it is Day <note>A metaphor for spiritual enlightenment or the coming judgment</note>, you will see that it is so. You are still in darkness, in the Mystery, so egregiously has *Babel* blinded you.

19. And it is only because you have valued human skill and favor rather than the Spirit of God that notorious delusions have seized you. Thus, you believe the spirits of lies who speak delusions in <margin>Fine outside, seeming holiness.</margin> hypocrisy. You depend on these and stick to them, causing hypocrisy by your error. Observe well what the **Revelations of John** and **Daniel** tell you! It is Day; the reward will shortly follow.

## The twenty-fifth Question.
### What is the Hand of God, and the Bosom of Abraham?

->**I.**<-

It has been sufficiently cleared already that it is the Essential Omnipresence of God, but within its own Principle. Consider the rich man who was in Hell: he could not obtain his request that *Abraham* should send *Lazarus* to him with one drop of cold water to cool his tongue in those flames. *Abraham* said there was a great gulf between them; that is to say, a whole Principle <note>In Boehme's cosmology, a "Principle" refers to a distinct realm of existence (like Light or Darkness) that can occupy the same space but remain separate.</note>.

2. But the "Bosom of *Abraham*" is to be understood in this way: *Abraham* was the father of all the faithful, and God gave him the promise that in his seed all people should be blessed. This was to be understood as the Messiah, Christ, who would become man in the <term>y</term> faithful. Just as He became man in *Abraham’s* seed, so also He would be born anew in the children of the faithful and bless them.
<margin>y Or Believers.</margin>

3. Now, this is the Holy Christian Church, born in Christ; and that is the Bosom of *Abraham*, for we are all one body in Christ. Since the promise was made to *Abraham*, he is the Patriarch; we are all born in the same promise (meaning by the New Birth in Christ) and are in the same bosom which receives us.

4. And when, through earnest repentance, we enter into the promise of—

And so we come into *Abraham's* bosom and are his children in the Promise. 9. For to *Isaac* belong the goods of the Image, of the body of Christ. Circumcision belongs to *Ishmael*, for he is concerned with works; but the goods are *Isaac's*. Yet *Ishmael* shall dwell in *Isaac's* tents at last; for *Japheth* shall dwell in the tents of *Shem*, but the Kingdom belongs to *Shem*.

10. We have the goods of *Isaac* not by the merit of works, but from Grace, from the Love of God. We cannot attain them by works, but in Faith, in the Will, in the Deed, and in entering [into the promised Inheritance].

right of Nature enters into it by the favor of the <sup>c</sup> Donor. Why is a servant in the house angry that his Lord is so bountiful as to give a stranger the Dominion?

12. We were strangers and had to work in his house; yet the Lord promised us in Paradise that he would again freely, out of Grace, give us his Kingdom. He rejected *Cain's* offering, but he gave the Kingdom of Grace to *Abel*; for *Abel* sought it in the Spirit, and *Cain* in the work.

13. Thus you understand that God's Kingdom is <term>Magical</term> <note>original: "Magicall"; in this context, it refers to the power of the will and spirit to act upon substance, rather than physical labor or ritual.</note>; for

14. In this way, you understand (if you have your spiritual sight) all of the Old Testament; this is the only foundation, though summarized here briefly. If we were to write a commentary on *Moses*, you would find it entirely there. 16. Oh, you dear Christendom, only open your eyes! Otherwise, the light will no longer shine so clearly upon you. Go now to *Lazarus* in the bosom of Abraham.

## The twenty-sixth Question.

### Whether do the souls of the dead care about men, children, friends, and possessions? And do they know, see, approve, or disapprove of their purposes and endeavors?

->1.<-

My beloved friend; this question is beyond the reach of all human reason and knowledge according to outward logic. But since we are *Abraham’s* children, we also have *Abraham’s* Spirit in Christ. Just as *Abraham* looked back upon the Promise in Paradise and also forward to the fulfillment of the Promise—so that he saw in the whole Body of Christ what was yet to be accomplished <margin>Or the time between both the beginning and the end.</margin> in the middle, and saw Christ from a distance—so do we also see.

2. Now, since you long so vehemently for the great Mysteries and seek them with such earnest desire—yet still giving God the glory and accounting yourself unworthy despite your high learning <note>original: "high Art"</note>, humbling yourself before God—therefore God gives these mysteries to you through such a mean and poor instrument <note>The author, Jacob Boehme, frequently refers to himself as a simple, unlearned instrument of God.</note> who considers himself much more unworthy of them. Yet, I would not willingly strive against God's will; and so you are the reason that this hand finds and attains these truths.

3. For this hand knew nothing of the Mystery; it sought only for

<meta>The text continues on the next page with the author's personal spiritual search.</meta>

<keywords>Bosom of Abraham, Babel, Ishmael, Lazarus, afterlife, spiritual discernment, humility, divine revelation</keywords>

for the faith of *Abraham*, but the understanding of *Abraham* was also given to it, which you have gained through your seeking.

4. Now take care that you also obtain the Spirit of *Abraham*, which has written in the knowledge of this hand; we will impart it to you as a brother. For we are not your Lord in this hidden matter, but your servant.

5. Know us correctly: we are *Lazarus* <note>A reference to the poor man in Jesus's parable (Luke 16), symbolizing humility</note>, and you may be considered *Abraham* in comparison to us. You have labored much more than we have, but we have entered into your harvest—not by merit, but by the grace of the Giver—lest any tongue should boast in the sight of God and say, "My own understanding has done this."

<margin>That is, in his outward reason, or in the "Old Adam" <note>The unregenerate, earthly nature of man</note>.</margin>
6. You propose a deep question, which I do not understand by my own power. For if I were to understand it through human reason, I would have to dwell within the departed soul and possess that soul's exact spirit and knowledge.

7. But since we are now one body in Christ, we all possess Christ’s Spirit. Therefore, in Christ, we all see through one Spirit and possess one knowledge; for He has become man within us, and all holy souls are our fellow-members, all born from the One. We all have one will in Christ, in the true "bosom of Abraham" <note>A traditional term for the place of comfort for the dead</note>.

8. And now we have obtained the strength to reveal this hidden thing to you in Christ; for our souls see within their souls. It is not as if they come to us, but rather we go to them; for they are in perfection, while we are only <margin>In part, or in imperfection.</margin> in part.

9. And now we are able to answer you, not from the reasoning of the outward world, but from the Image in Christ, and from His and our Spirit.

10. You ask whether the departed souls <term>separated souls</term> <note>Souls that have been separated from the physical body by death</note> take care of human matters, and whether they approve or disapprove of them. You must understand that this happens in three different ways, concerning three distinct types of souls.

11. First, those souls which have not yet reached Heaven, but remain stuck in the source, in the <term>Principle</term> <note>The fundamental spiritual state or "birth" environment of the soul</note>, or in the process of birth. These still have the human essence and their earthly works within them; they diligently search for the cause of their <margin>Or stay in that condition.</margin> retention.

12. Therefore, many of them return with the <term>Astral Spirit</term> <note>In the author's cosmology, the "starry" or sidereal spirit that lingers between the physical and the purely divine</note> and wander up and down in their former houses and places of residence. They appear in human form and desire various things, often worrying about their wills or testaments. They also seek to obtain the blessing of the saints so that they may find rest. If their earthly affairs still cling to them, they often continue to care for their children and friends.

13. This condition of theirs continues until they enter into their rest and until their Astral Spirit is consumed. Then all such activities, cares, and anxieties come to an end, and they also have

<summary>The author explains that departed souls exist in different states; those not yet fully at rest may linger near the earthly plane in their "Astral Spirit" to settle worldly affairs.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the state of souls after death and their interaction with the living, specifically focusing on those who have entered into Grace.</meta>

They have no more knowledge of earthly things, except what they see purely through the Wonders, in the *Magic* <note>original: *Magick*; here referring to the divine creative power or the spiritual mirror of reality</note>.

14. But such a soul does not stir up the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; the "turmoil" or "disturber," referring to the wrathful, chaotic element in nature that leads to destruction</note>, nor does it seek what is in this world. For, having once passed through Death and away from the *Turba*, it desires such things no longer. It also takes no further care; for care stirs up the *Turba*, and then the soul's will would be forced to enter with its Spirit into earthly things. It would rather leave such things alone, because it was difficult to get rid of them before; it will no longer entertain the earthly will.

15. This is an answer concerning this first sort of souls. We tell you plainly and in truth: after they are once received into Grace, they take no more purposeful care about human earthly <margin>Or, affairs.</margin> <insert>matters</insert>. Instead, the soul beholds the heavenly matters brought to it by the Spirit of man and rejoices in them. But there is still something more to explain, which is this:

16. A living man has such power that he is able, with his Spirit, to go into Heaven to the departed souls and stir them up regarding a question through a hearty desire. But it must be earnest; it must be Faith that can break open a Principle <note>A "Principle" refers to one of the fundamental realms of existence—the dark world, the light world, or the outer world</note>.

17. We see this in Samuel the Prophet, whom the <margin>Saul.</margin> <insert>King</insert> of <margin>Israel</margin> <insert>raised up</insert>, so that Samuel might make his will known to him. This may seem otherwise to some people, of whom we may well say they are blind and void of knowledge. They speak only their own academic fables and frame opinions about things they have no knowledge of in the Spirit; these people are *Babel* <note>Referring to the "City of Confusion," or those who argue over religious dogma without spiritual understanding</note>.

18. Now secondly, the other sort of souls—those who sink into Death without a <margin>Or the body of Christ.</margin> <insert>body</insert> <note>Referring to the "spiritual body" or "heavenly garment" mentioned in previous sections</note>—are in the exact same place of the Principle as the first sort who later sank down into themselves. None of these take any evil affairs upon themselves in which the *Turba* remains.

19. But when honest souls who are still alive send these departed souls their works through their Spirit and will, the departed rejoice in them. They are so friendly that they appear to men magically in sleep and show them good paths. Many times they reveal arts which lie in <margin>In the secret, in the most inward Mystery.</margin> <insert>secret</insert>, namely, in the Abyss of the soul.

20. For since the earthly Spirit thrusts its Mystery <note>The "Mystery" here refers to the hidden essence or "Mysterium Magnum"</note> before the soul and keeps the soul captive in that mystery, the Spirit of the soul cannot always reach the deepest secrets while alive. But after the departure from the body, the soul is naked—especially if it is without a New Body. Then it beholds itself and its own Wonders. It can very easily show a living person something (if that person is honest and has not stirred up the *Turba*) in the sleeping *Magic* <note>original: *Magia*</note>. For dreams are entirely magical, and the soul without a body exists in the *Magic* of God.

<summary>Boehme explains that departed souls in Grace avoid earthly cares to shun the Turba, yet they can still interact with the living through earnest faith or appear in dreams to reveal hidden wisdom.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Turba, Magia, Samuel the Prophet, Saul, state of the soul, dreams, spiritual body, Babel, Mystery</keywords>

--- Page 134 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the state of souls after death and their interaction with the living, specifically addressing the influence of damned souls and the rest of the blessed.</meta>

21. Thus, know that no soul separated from the body enters into any wicked matter, unless it is a damned soul. Such a soul indeed enters in magically and finds its joy there; it teaches notorious and vile pranks in dreams, for it is a servant of the Devil.

<margin>n Creates horror in the magic.
o Or raises up.
p Raising of the pain, or property.</margin>
22. And whatever a wicked man desires, the Devil readily helps him to achieve; for the Devil can do it better through the soul of a man than by himself. This is because the Devil is too crude and <note>original: "terrifieth the Magia"</note> terrifies the magical field, so that the elementary spirit <note>The spirit governing the physical body and its senses.</note> is astonished and awakens the body.

23. Also, you must know this: everything is done magically in the will, without awakening the source of pain. No soul rises with its essences of its own accord to please man, unless man raises and disturbs it himself.

24. There are many villainies in <term>Necromancy</term> <note>original: "Nigromancy"; the practice of communicating with the dead.</note> which can often vex and torment the spirits of men; but it can do nothing to any soul that is clothed with Christ’s <term>Essentiality</term> <note>The divine substance or heavenly body of Christ.</note>; for that soul is free.

25. Regarding the third sort of separated souls—those who are in **Abraham’s bosom**, in Christ, possessing the heavenly essentiality—none can stir them unless they wish it themselves, such as when they feel a favor toward a soul that is like their own. Also, they take no earthly thing upon themselves unless it serves the glory of God; in such cases, they are restless until they reveal something in a magical manner.

26. But they allow no <term>Turba</term> <note>A Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "turmoil" or "disturbance" of the divine wrath that destroys things.</note> into themselves; neither do they intercede with God for us. Instead, whatever comes to them, they rejoice in it with the Angels; for if the Angels rejoice over a sinner who repents, the souls do so much more.

<margin>q Their praying, or intercession for us.</margin>
27. Why should they pray to God for us? Our help does not lie in their prayer, but in man’s own entry into God. When a person strongly turns their will toward God, then God’s Spirit helps them without the need for their intercessory prayers.

28. For His arms are stretched forth day and night to help man; what need is there then for their prayers? It is the will of God that man should come to Him.

<margin>r A separated soul of a Saint.</margin>
29. Shall a soul then be so presumptuous as to make God out to be so severe a judge that He is unwilling to receive a returning sinner? Surely there would be no true knowledge of God in such a belief. But when they see a soul pierce through with its spirit to God, it is a great joy to them that God’s Kingdom is enlarged.

30. The heavenly soul possesses God’s will: what God wills, that soul wills also. But it is God’s Spirit itself that will help the converted sinner.

<margin>s Or blessed soul.</margin>
31. The souls see clearly how God’s Spirit pierces into the human soul, if only the will of that soul gives way to it. There is no need for the prayers of any Angel; they all simply wish that God’s Kingdom may come.

<summary>This page explains that while damned souls may haunt the living through "magic" and dreams, blessed souls remain at rest in Christ, rejoicing in human repentance but not acting as necessary intercessors, as God is always ready to receive the repentant.</summary>
<keywords>necromancy, intercession of saints, Abraham's bosom, Turba, Magia, repentance, spiritual will</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the state of departed souls and their relationship with the living, specifically addressing the power of faith and the validity of miracles.</meta>

come, and that God's will be done; but the honor of government they give to God.

32. We acknowledge that in the Roman Catholic Church <note>original: "Popery"</note> people have called upon great saints who are dead, and that these saints have also appeared to men and performed wonders <margin>or Miracles.</margin>; this is true. Although this is now taught against, it is by those who are indeed quite ignorant of the matter. It has another **A. B. C.** <note>The basic principles or hidden spiritual logic.</note> which all of them on both sides do not understand at all.

33. The faith of one person receives the faith of another; the faith of the living took hold of the faith of the departed saints, and that faith <margin>namely, the will that is strong.</margin> has performed wonders.

34. Indeed, faith is so powerful that it can throw down mountains! Shall then the pure faith of the saints, joined with the faith of the living, be able to do nothing? Truly, it could even destroy <margin>or break in pieces.</margin> the whole world, if God would only permit it.

35. For He has permitted it to work so far at times that the Heathen have been converted by such means, when they have seen such wonders performed at the death <margin>or departure.</margin> of saints.

36. Should not a soul in heaven be willing to exercise its faith for the glory of God and the performing of wonders for Him? This is even done by the Holy Spirit, who has performed the wonders by the faith of both parties; and they are only the wonders of God and of His children.

37. But this is now completely cast to the ground, and there is now such a "learned" school of thought that it treats all of God's wonders <margin>or Miracles.</margin> with contempt. But this school is *Babel* <note>A reference to the Tower of Babel, symbolizing spiritual confusion and pride.</note> and not the Spirit of God; it is envious pride. They stand on high and cry, "Come all of you to me, here is Christ, here is the Gospel!" In truth, there is pride, greed, ambition, and self-important boldness—a lifting up of proud *Babel*.

38. It is even the old Antichrist, and they are young twigs sprouted out of the old tree; they have stirred up the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe a state of spiritual disturbance, wrath, or the "awakened" anger of nature.</note> with their strong, wrathful sap. This *Turba* shall root up the whole tree, for God has cursed it; it is entirely evil and worm-eaten, and it shall fall.

39. For there is a young tree grown out of the root—out of the old root—which shall reveal what the old tree has been in its "wonders."

40. Yet we would not insult anyone, but only speak of our wonders, and say that the servant shall enter into the house and be free; for the time is at hand that he should eat with the Son, and be merry and rejoice with him.

41. Thus we answer your question briefly: that indeed, the holy souls do certainly know of our holy works and approve of

<summary>The author argues that the faith of the living can connect with the faith of departed saints to perform miracles, while criticizing modern religious institutions as a proud "Babel" that has lost touch with spiritual power.</summary>

of them; but they do not at all regard false works. For they dwell in another principle <note>original: "Principle"; referring to a spiritual realm or state of being</note> into which no evil work can come. Indeed, they do not do so, nor do they inquire after that which belongs to the Devil; also, they know nothing but that which reaches into their own principle.

42. Children, parents, friends, and strangers are all alike to them; for in Heaven we are all brothers and sisters. They take no greater care of parents or children than they do of others, unless those people serve God, and then their service of God is acceptable and joyful to them. But they do not enter into their *Turba*. <note>original: "Turba"; a term used by Boehme to describe the source of destruction, wrath, or chaos</note>
<margin>The evil which they do or suffer justly.</margin>

43. For after the Last Day, honest parents shall know nothing of their children who are in Hell. Therefore, it is sufficiently and plainly known to us that they now also take no care regarding wicked matters.

## The Twenty-Seventh Question.

<margin>Or in death.</margin>
### Whether the departed souls know and understand this or that business and art <note>original: "Art"; meaning a craft, science, or specialized skill</note> of which they had sufficient skill while they were in the body.

->I.<-

This question is like the former: all their works appear to them in their will in a magical manner. They see them, but the physical form of them shall only first be given to them at the Day of Restoration <note>The Resurrection or the end of the world</note>, so that they shall be able to rightly behold their works. For they must first be tried by the fire; and whatever is false, the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"</note> of that must remain in the fire, according to the words of Christ.
<margin>Uncleanness or dross.</margin>
<margin>The words of Christ in Paul. <note>Referring to 1 Corinthians 3:13-15 regarding works being tested by fire</note></margin>

2. But it is strange that men should ask whether they know arts or not; without question, they know all arts, however deeply they are grounded. But they dare not awaken them <note>original: "awaken them"; to bring them into active use or manifestation</margin> so that they appear in spirit. For arts are born in the center of nature, out of those essences wherein the wonders lie, which they sought in this world so far as they had the mystery made known to them.
<margin>Or bring them into act.</margin>

3. A soul that is without the divine body does not willingly enter into the mystery for the sake of art; it stands still in its rest and it fears the *Turba*. It gives God the glory.

4. But those souls that are highly enlightened, who have heaven—

<summary>This page explains that departed souls in Heaven are indifferent to earthly relationships and evil works, focusing only on the divine. It also asserts that while souls retain knowledge of earthly arts and skills, they refrain from practicing them to avoid disturbing their spiritual rest or encountering the "Turba."</summary>
<keywords>afterlife, Turba, Day of Restoration, spiritual knowledge, arts and sciences, divine body, salvation</keywords>

heavenly essentiality in their spirit. They have all knowledge of heavenly things and of whatever lies hidden in the Mystery <note>original: "Mysterie"; referring to the *Mysterium Magnum*, or the hidden core of God's creation</note>: especially those who had acquaintance with the Mystery in this life. Others do not dive as deeply into the Mystery.

<margin>Or employment.</margin>
5. For everyone continues in their calling—in that which they delighted in here. Although there is no such physical labor there, they still have their joy in it; for in Heaven, there is a humble, simple life like that of children.

6. Why then should they search or care for academic art or skill, when the whole Mystery of God stands open? God fills all in all. There is only pure wonder; they all live in wonders and are all themselves the "Art of God." They have great knowledge, but it is held within a paradisiacal, simple child-like life.

## The twenty-eighth Question.

### Does the soul have any more knowledge of divine, angelic, earthly, and devilish things? And can it gain more certain experience and knowledge of them than it had while in the body?

#### I.

Concerning divine and angelic knowledge, the soul certainly has much more of that; for it exists within the Principle of God <note>original: "Principle of God"; the divine realm or state of being</note>. The son sees very well what the father does in his house, and likewise, the soul sees what is in Heaven.

2. Their knowledge varies; for the highest knowledge is found in the Majesty <note>the direct presence and glory of God</note>. Therefore, most souls must wait until the Last Day, when they shall receive their new bodies.

3. But the highly enlightened souls, which exist within the divine body and power, have superabundant understanding and knowledge of God and also of the angels. For they continue in the wonders of God until they shall bring their own wonders <note>the fruits of their earthly life and works</note> there.

4. The souls that are without a body are in Heaven, in God, as it were magically <note>original: "Magically"; acting through will and spirit rather than physical means</note>. They awaken no new wonders themselves but are under God’s altar and expect the wonders to be revealed at the Day of the Appearance <note>the Second Coming or Resurrection</note>.

<margin>No holy soul.</margin>
5. They take no care regarding devilish matters; it belongs to the angels to strive with the devils and to defend humanity. No holy soul directs its imagination toward Hell, for Hell is enmity to it.

<summary>This page explains that souls in heaven retain their earthly interests but experience them through a state of child-like wonder and divine knowledge. While all souls in God's presence gain greater understanding, highly enlightened souls possess deeper insight into the divine mystery while awaiting the final resurrection.</summary>
<keywords>afterlife, divine knowledge, Mystery, calling, angels, Day of the Appearance, Principle of God, heavenly state</keywords>

# The Twenty-Ninth Question

### What is the Soul’s Rest, <note>original: "g" refers to Resurrection</note> Awakening, and <note>original: "h" refers to Clarification or transfiguration</note> Glorification?

<margin>
Or Resurrection.
Clarification, or transfiguration.
<note>original: "i"</note> Pain, or Turba <note>The "Turba" refers to the "turmoil" or "disturbance" of the wrath of God.</note>.
</margin>

1. This has already been sufficiently cleared. Their rest is without physical essence in the stillness, where they are in God’s hand, and no <margin>pain or turmoil</margin> touches them. They have no feeling of any source of suffering, but they are like one who lies in a sweet sleep and rests very pleasantly.

<margin>
Namely, the Spirit of the soul.
Glorious illumination.
Cheerfully.
Stirring, or working.
</margin>

2. Their glorification, during this middle time <note>The state between death and the final judgment.</note>, is when they contemplate the joy to come. Then the <margin>Spirit of the soul</margin> enters into the Majesty of God and receives joy and <margin>glorious illumination</margin>. And so, all this time they trim their lamps so that they may more <margin>cheerfully</margin> receive their bridegroom in their new bodies.

3. There is a very sweet, magical, and paradisiacal joy in them; but Paradise is not yet fully <margin>manifested or active</margin> in them with total perfection. That belongs to the new body which shall rise out of the Earth.

4. The first body which God created, and which Christ redeemed with his blood, will bring its wonders with it and enter again into Paradise. It will be clothed with the Majesty of God, and then the Tabernacle of God will be with men.

---

# The Thirtieth Question

### What is the difference between the Resurrection of the flesh and of the Soul, both of the Living and of the Dead?

-> 1. <-

Christ says concerning this that there shall be a great difference. Therefore, we direct you to the Scripture, for it shall come to pass exactly according to the Holy Scripture.

2. Seeing that human reason cannot search for or find this out, how should I answer you more than the Scripture speaks of? Yet, seeing you so earnestly desire and long to know these things, you actually become the "finder" through your seeking, and I am merely the instrument.

<summary>This page describes the soul's intermediate state as a peaceful sleep in God's hand and explains that full glorification awaits the physical resurrection of the body. The author emphasizes that while the soul experiences joy now, the total perfection of Paradise requires the redeemed body.</summary>

<keywords>soul's rest, resurrection, glorification, Turba, intermediate state, Paradise, Tabernacle of God, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

--- Page 139 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of divine revelation and the responsibility of those who receive spiritual knowledge.</meta>

3. And although this knowledge is revealed and given to me, it is not something that exists merely in my own understanding or intellect. Instead, this knowing exists in the Spirit of Christ. It is according to this Spirit that our hand speaks; for it speaks from two Persons, and these two Persons say "we," not "I." It speaks of two, just as a Lord speaks of both his person and his <margin>Office, Authority, Power, or Jurisdiction.</margin> dominion.

4. In this same way, the children and servants of God ought not to say, "the knowledge is mine" or "the understanding is mine," but should give God the glory. In their manifestation of the wonders of God, they should speak of two: namely, the Giver and the Receiver.

5. Neither should anyone interpret our manner of writing as if this hand were glorying or boasting of its own human authority and worthiness. Though indeed we are worthy in Christ, as far as the outward man is concerned, we desire no honor or renown, for all renown belongs to God.

6. We are children of the Father and must do as he wills. We must not bury the talent (which he gives us) in the earth <note>A reference to the Parable of the Talents in Matthew 25, where a servant is punished for hiding his master's money rather than investing it.</note>, for the Father will require it back with increase. If there is no increase, he takes away what he has given and gives it to the one who has gained much. It would be a very lamentable thing for me to have it taken away—to know and enjoy God and then to lose him again. It would be much better for me to lose the whole world and my outward life than to lose God and the Kingdom of Heaven.

7. Neither is it a light matter to be disobedient to God. See what was brought upon Korah, Dathan, and Abiram by Moses <note>Biblical figures who led a rebellion against Moses and were swallowed by the earth as punishment (Numbers 16).</note>. We say the same fate shall come upon the disobedient and those who mock.

8. Indeed, the mocker does not see his punishment instantly, but his <margin>That is, his disturbance, malice, and wickedness create a form or "figure" of his punishment.</margin> *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*. In Boehmian theology, this refers to the "disturber" or the source of destruction and wrath that arises when elements are out of balance.</note> takes it in. If he has been a jeering mocker and a reviler and now wishes to be delivered from his <margin>Or sin.</margin> *Turba*, he must bewail it with bitter lamentation and sorrow in the sight of God. Otherwise, he will carry his mockery with him into the fire of anger, and it will gnaw at him forever. We speak this as a warning.

9. For we shall here describe a very serious matter. Do not be deceived; God is not mocked. Wrathful anger is in his power; he <margin>Severe.</margin> <margin>Or pass away.</margin> has Heaven and Hell in his power. The Last Judgment is a serious work.

10. And because we are to describe the Resurrection of the Dead, we must write about the manner of it: what it is, and by what power this world shall <margin>Disturbance or confusion.</margin> <margin>Or when the measure of your sin is full.</margin> perish and the dead arise. It will be a serious reality, no jesting matter; we shall speak of its very foundation.

11. Do not think this is a fable. It proceeds from the *Turba* upon the "Crown" <note>Likely referring to the "Crown of the Time," a period of spiritual fulfillment or judgment.</note>. The <margin>The spirit of awakened wickedness.</margin> <margin>Essences or inner qualities.</margin> spirit of your own *Turba* declares this to you, for the end has found the beginning. Thus the awakened...

# The Thirtieth Question Answered.

...in Love and Anger: and then all things together at once shall be plainly laid open, and visibly set forth in the sight of all creatures, in the following manner.

22. The beginning of the Creation, in the Word *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let it be done"; the creative command of God</note>, has enclosed this world in itself as a model. It <margin>Or appointed.</margin> founded the limit wherein the wonders are now contained. These wonders were also revealed in the middle of time and brought into *Essence* <note>original: "Essence"; here meaning actual, physical, or spiritual reality</note>, having been foreseen from eternity in the wisdom and the *Magia* <note>The "Magic" or creative power of God's will</note> of God. They will all exist together in the Essence then, and then the <margin>End, finishing, or consummation of all things.</margin> limit is near at hand. There will be no more time for seeking, for then all is finished: whatever God had in His eternal counsel, He has conceived and manifested in time.

23. Now here is the end of time, for then the beginning has found the end, and the end is then the beginning, and passes again into that which it was from eternity.

24. But the <margin>Namely, the world and every creature.</margin> middle, along with the <margin>Namely, all that has been done in the world from the beginning to the end.</margin> wonders which were manifested in time, continues forever in the beginning and in the end as an eternal middle. It remains with its wonders—namely, with the angels and men and their essences—as well as the figures of all creatures and whatever has been essential at any time. The Earth with its metals, stones, and all material substances, such as trees and herbs—all these stand in their figure, in the middle, and in the wonders, but are quite void of their former earthly essences and life.

25. For no beast returns to life, but its figure continues in the *Magia*; for it arose out of the Eternal Glass <note>The divine mirror or imagination of God</note>. Thus, now (when the outward earthly glass breaks), it must remain in the eternal as a wonder, to God’s honor and glory forevermore.

26. And these essences all belong to Paradise: for they shall be the Holy Paradise wherein the heavenly essences shall bear essential, palpable fruit.

27. In this life, we account the fruits of the <margin>Inanimate, senseless things.</margin> Earth (proceeding from its essence) as dead things without understanding. Similarly, the bestial and earthly image of this world shall <margin>Life or sense.</margin> appear as a dead essence, and so shall the essence of all creatures; they shall remain as a shadow.

28. But Paradise has and bears fruit from the power of eternal life—that is, from God’s <margin>Or wonders.</margin> essences. Now, all that which is for the most part hidden from us here is enclosed in the word *Fiat*, in the beginning and the end, and it lies therein as a great mystery.

29. But now the Spirit of the first creation will move all the three principles <note>The three realms: the dark world (fire/wrath), the light world (love/majesty), and the outward world (nature)</note>. Yet before that is brought to pass, the Word of God <margin>Forms itself by the Spirit.</margin> conceives itself with this Spirit, like an elevation or manifestation of the Deity.

30. For the Spirit stirs the *Turba* <note>Latin: "turmoil" or "disturbance"; the awakening of the wrathful principle that dissolves forms</note> of all essences in all the...

# The thirtieth Question Answered.

three Principles; and then whatever is in Heaven, Hell, or this world, will be manifested in one hour.

31. For the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; refers to the "disturber" or the spirit of destruction and awakening that breaks the physical world apart</note> stirs up all Essences, and all creatures; and all whatever is in Heaven and Hell will be made visible, and every one shall see the works of his own Heart, be they good or evil.

32. In this hour also the Judge Christ will appear, upon the Bow of the Ternary <note>original: *Ternary*; here referring to the divine manifestation of the Trinity or the holy structure of the spiritual world</note> as upon a Rainbow. According to the Principle of this world, it is a natural Rainbow, but according to the Principle of God it is the Ternary—the Cross with a twofold Rainbow. One part is turned toward the Internal Principle, that is, in the Abyss of the Anger, and there he sits upon the Anger of God: this the Devils and all wicked men shall see.

33. For this Bow is included in all the three Principles, and this Judge Christ sits upon and in the Omnipotence of Eternity, above all that <margin>Or ever had a Being.</margin> is called Essence.

34. Then the miserable horror of all Devils and wicked men will arise; and they will howl, lament, yell, and cry, and say unto the wise Virgins, "Give us some of your Oil. Oh! Comfort us, we entreat you; we beseech you, teach us! What shall we do? Give us some of your holiness so that we may be able to stand before the Angry Countenance of God; for the Eye of Hell stands wide open; where shall we flee from this Anger?"

35. And the wise Virgins, namely the Children of God, will say: <margin>Those that fell away.</margin> "Go away to your merchants and buy oil for yourselves, lest there be not enough for us and you. We have only enough for ourselves. Go away to your hypocrites and deceivers, who have tickled your ears <margin>Seeming holiness and purity.</margin> with flattering, fine dissimulation <note>original: dissimulation; meaning hypocrisy or false pretenses</note> for your money; buy for yourselves there. What? Do you have need of us now? Have we not been your fools? Away now with the flourishing show of your deceit and hypocrisy; we will not make ourselves partakers with you, lest we suffer for it."

36. They shall then stand in great horror and trembling, yelling and crying to the Judge Christ; but his wrathful Eye <margin>Or by.</margin> with their *Turba* enters into the very Heart, piercing through Spirit and flesh, through marrow and bones; for the soul in the *Turba*, by the moving of God, is stirred up already beforehand in the wrathfulness.

37. And then they will fall to the ground from very anguish, and some of them shall bite or gnaw their blasphemous tongues, and the proud will say, "O you mountains, fall on us, and you hills, cover us from the Eye of this wrathfulness!" They will creep into the caves and clefts of the rocks and endeavor to bury themselves in the mountains. They would willingly kill themselves, but there is no death anymore; they will endeavor to deprive themselves of life with

<summary>This page describes the apocalyptic moment of the Last Judgment, where Christ appears on a symbolic rainbow and the wicked realize their spiritual poverty too late. It depicts the terror of the damned as they seek to hide from divine wrath and find no escape even in death.</summary>

<keywords>Last Judgment, Christ the Judge, Turba, Ternary, Wise Virgins, divine wrath, apocalypse, three principles</keywords>
