# The Thirtieth Question Answered

Even though weapons are present, there is no more dying; only Wrath and Anger remain.

38. In this horror, all the buildings in the world will fall down; for the Earth will tremble as if it were shaken with Thunder, and the horror will be in all living things—every thing according to its <margin>Property or condition.</margin> source. A beast does not have the same source as the soul has; it is only afraid of the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; the "turmoil" or "disturbance," referring to the destructive wrath of God that breaks things apart.</note>.

39. And in this upheaval and commotion, all waters will rise above the height of all mountains, so that there will be no <margin>Or respiration.</margin> breathing upon the Earth. They will rise so high that they will be, as it were, consumed. All things will be so gripped by the Anger, by the *Turba*, that there will be nothing but pure anguish in the Elements.

40. All high mountains and rocks will cleave asunder and tumble down. The <margin>That is, those properties which are in the Firmament: or a Magic desire, as in verse 41.</margin> stars likewise fall to the Earth with their strong influence and power. All this will be brought to pass over several days; for, just as the world was created, so it shall have its end. For the <margin>Seeking, or earnest desire.</margin> longing of the Earth in its anguish will draw the stars to it, as it has always done <margin>Or all this while.</margin> throughout time; so that the earthly body has drawn the <margin>Desire or longing.</margin> seeking of the stars to itself.

41. For the stars are a magical <margin>Desire or longing.</margin> seeking which has awakened Life. Therefore, now when the Earth is awakened in the great *Turba*, it will then become so thirsty and hungry that it will draw down the stars to it; there will be such an anguish upon the Earth.

42. But the Children of God shall lift up their eyes and hands to Christ, for joy that the Day of their Deliverance is at hand; for the anguish does not touch them.

43. And in those days (though how many are appointed for it is known only to God—for in six days the world and all its hosts were created, but it is now hidden from us) <margin>The water.</margin> the water will return again to its own place and fill all the depths in a more abundant manner than they were before.

44. For now Death comes by this means; and in that hour, all creatures except man shall die. Also, all men who have crawled into the rocks and mountains shall come forth, but with anguish of their conscience (although as yet the *Turba* hindered them so that the horror was in Death); for the falling of the water captivates the *Turba*.

45. And then the voice of the Holy Trinity <note>original: *Holy Ternarie*; the three-fold nature of God.</note> will put itself forth according to all the three Principles <note>The three "Principles" in Böhme's philosophy: the dark world of fire/wrath, the light world of love/majesty, and the visible world of nature.</note>, and say by the mouth of Christ the Judge: "Arise, you dead, and come to Judgment."

46. This voice is the original, Eternal Spirit which upholds every life, and which also has always governed in all the three Principles; for it is that Spirit from which the life of every thing has sprung.

# The thirtieth Question Answered.

[The Spirit] has sprung forth, and in it all things consist for eternity. It has been the life <gloss>a</gloss> and motion of all things, in which the beginning and also the end of every life has stood—yes, even Eternity itself. For it is from Eternity and is the Creator of all things.

<margin>f Wherein the Eternal has been beheld.</margin>
47. It has two eternal beginnings: namely, one in the Fire and one in the Light. The third beginning has become the <gloss>f</gloss> Glass <note>original: "Glasse"; likely meaning a mirror or reflection</note> of the Eternal: namely, the Spirit of this world. Also, it has been as a Wonder in this world, and the Wonders have been made manifest by it. It is he to whom the Last Judgment belongs; his moving is the last.

48. For in the Creation, he moved the Father; and in the Incarnation of the Word, he moved the Son; and now the last moving and the Judgment is his. He will reduce every thing to its eternal abode; and this is done by the voice of the Word proceeding from the mouth of Christ.

49. For the Spirit goes forth in two principles in God: that is, in the Anger or Fire, he goes forth as the earnest wrath of the Fire-life; in the light of the Love, he goes forth as a flame of the Divine Majesty; and in the Spirit of this world, he goes forth as a Wonder of Life; and all this is undeniable.

50. And if perhaps some person would claim such exceeding high learning for himself as to contradict it, we offer to demonstrate it to him in everything—we will except nothing in this world. Everything will afford an evident testimony of it. Let him come to us when he will; he ought not to put it off and say we are mad. For if these few words will not satisfy him, we will so evidence it to him that he himself shall find and see who he himself is. Yes, though the Devil himself should burst for very anger, yet we would set it down plainly before his eyes.

51. Now, seeing this Spirit has the word *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let it be done"; the creative command of God</note> (namely, God’s word) and the Center of Nature, from which it has its eternal origin; and as the Spirit of the Center has a twofold outflowing: the first being in the Fire, in the essences of the origin of life, in the ground of the origin of the soul; and the second in the light of the Fire, <margin>g Or Property.</margin> which is the second <gloss>g</gloss> source, which buds afresh through Death and is called the Kingdom of God; where also in the Light it is a flame of Love, and in the Fire it is a flame of Anger.

52. And therefore it will break open the gates of Death, for it shall raise the dead. It has the word *Fiat* in it, and this *Fiat* is both in the soul and in the body also. And although the body has been long corrupted, yet the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a term used by Boehme to describe the "disturber" or the destructive element in nature that breaks things down</note> remains still in the *Fiat* with the Wonders of the body.

<margin>h The Word of the Lord.</margin>
53. And now the four elements must restore to the *Fiat* that essence which they have swallowed up: for <gloss>h</gloss> the Word of the Lord <note>original: "Verbum Domini"</note> is...

56. 58. Now, if any man has sown much in the Fire, he shall suffer loss: as the Scripture tells us, that the works of the wicked shall remain in the fire, and he shall suffer loss.

59. But you must understand us rightly; the body which has been here upon Earth; that evil corruptible body which has devoured the Noble and excellent Image of Paradise: shall come, and stand forth with its precious Image in it: It must give an account of the Image of God.

60. Now blessed are they that have Christ's Spirit, they have their first Image in the word *Fiat*; which must restore it again to the soul, and that in the Adamical Body <note>The physical body descended from Adam, now transformed</note>.

61. But they that have not Christ's Spirit shall stand forth in the evil body: but their soul shall have lost their true Image, and they shall have such an Image in the Spirit of the Soul, as their wills have been here: as their daily lust has been, so shall their Image be.

62. And in that hour also the wrathful *Fiat* of the Darkness shall bring forth the Devils; who shall then receive their wages and lodging: at the hearing of which they tremble.

<margin>
r The transitory, and Eternal body.

For now, all things shall appear in their true form, both within them and without them, so that there shall be <margin>Or lying</margin> no denial; for the Spirit <margin>Of the Judge.</margin> of the Judge, by the *Turba*, proves the soul, spirit, and flesh. 75. 77. Therefore, you rulers and potentates of the world, look to it, and see whether you are the ordinance of God and are placed in the right, divine order! Have a care how you deal with the distressed inferior: for now he stands before your eyes and blames you, saying that you have been the cause of all his sins and wickedness.

78. For there, one will cry out and accuse the other, saying that he caused me to commit such lewdness, and will curse him: the inferior his superiors, and the superiors their superiors. Also, the prince will accuse his false counselors, and his counselors the priests, because they did not reprove their courses of action but instead soothed and flattered them for the sake of ambition and honor.

79. You have provoked and urged on the princes of your land (who are the ordinance of God) to war and bloodshed for the sake of a few <margin>Terms of Art.</margin> words which you yourselves have <margin>Or Contrived.</margin> forged.

80. Where is Christ's teaching and Spirit of Love, who said, "Love one another, for by this shall they know that you are my disciples"?

82. Oh, how is the name of Christ <margin>Reproached or scandalized for the sake of your disputations.</margin> blasphemed because of your contentions! For the principle of Light is here called the right hand, and the principle of Fire <note>The author uses "Fire" to represent God's wrath or the harsh quality of nature.</note> is the left.

84. And thus the tribunal or seat of judgment will be set. All the great shepherds whom God has sent forth as a light to the world—who have reproved and taught, such as the Patriarchs who taught of the promise of Christ—shall be placed with the Prophets and Apostles at the right hand of the judgment. And Moses and all <margin>And law-givers.</margin> teachers of the Law shall be at the left hand of the judgment.

85. For Moses and Elias <note>Elijah</note>, and all those exceedingly precious teachers of the Law, bear the fiery sword and demand God’s justice; while those at the right hand demand God’s mercy.

86. And in this hour is the very Last Day, when the Judge shall say: "Come, you blessed of my Father, inherit the kingdom prepared for you from the beginning; for I was hungry, thirsty, naked, sick, and in prison, and you ministered to me."

87. And to the wicked company: "Go, you cursed, into everlasting fire; I do not know you. For I have been hungry, thirsty, naked, sick, and in prison, and you did not minister to me."

88. And then they will excuse themselves before the presence of the Judge and say, "Lord, we did not know you." But he shall say, "Since you have not done this to my poor children, you have not done it to me."

89. And here the Spirit of God will manifest himself immediately...

98. Thus, my beloved friend, you have a brief description and declaration of the last Judgment Day: for whatsoever is of this world shall pass away.

99. The Earth, and all stony rocks and elements, will melt away, and only that will remain which God intended, and for the sake of which he created this world.

<margin>God has foreseen the...</margin>
100. ## The thirty-first Question.
### *What kind of new glorified bodies shall the holy souls have?*

->**I.**<-

**T**his has been already sufficiently declared; for, as every one shall be clothed with the power of love, righteousness, and purity, and as his excellent works of faith have been, so shall he gloriously shine.

2. Yet there will be very much difference: for the works of many will all remain in the fire <note>original: "remaine in the Fire"; referring to the biblical concept of works being tested by fire, where only the pure survive</note>, and he himself will hardly escape; <margin>namely, the great saints.</margin> he will not shine as the saints.

<margin>Or envying.</margin> 3. For (as the Scripture says) they shall excel one another as the stars of heaven: but there will be no grudging, but every one will rejoice at the excellence of the other; for there is no other light there than God, filling all in all.

<margin>Or virtue.</margin> 4. And so every one, as his power <note>original: "Power" or "Vertue"; referring to the soul's capacity or spiritual strength</note> is capable of the light, shall receive the brightness of the majesty of God: for after this life there will be no bettering, but every thing remains as it returns home.

5. For there the Judge, Christ, will deliver up the kingdom to his Father; and then we shall no more need any teachers and guides. But he is our King and Brother; there is no more commanding; but we are with him as a child with the Father. Whatsoever we do, it is good; for all falsehood is done away.

---

## The thirty-second Question.
### *What shall the form, condition, joy, and glory of the soul be in the life to come?*

->**I.**<-

**H**ere we must consider Paradise; for this outward world with its fruits and colors has been a figure <note>original: "Figure"; a symbolic representation or shadow of a higher reality</note> of Paradise: for Paradise

<summary>This page describes the nature of the glorified body in the afterlife, explaining that while all souls will shine with God's light, their individual brilliance depends on their earthly works and spiritual capacity.</summary>
<keywords>glorified bodies, afterlife, celestial hierarchy, majesty of God, Christ as King, Paradise, spiritual capacity</keywords>

--- Page 151 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the soul's return to Paradise and the nature of the resurrected body.</meta>

<margin>f Or within us.</margin>
was in us; and the outward Spirit deprived us of it and drew us into itself. For when Adam lusted after it, his own lust took him captive.

2. But we shall now enter into it again and eternally solace ourselves in the excellent, beautiful flourishing of all manner of flowers and forms—both of trees and plants, and all kinds of fruits—but they will not be so earthly, gross, and palpable <note>original: "palpable"; meaning tangible or heavy in a physical sense</note>.

<margin>g Clear, subtle, transparent, bright, and shining.</margin>
3. For then our bodies shall not be so; how then can that essence be so? All things there will be angelic. The fruits are more g pure and fine than those now in the outward elements, for they create no impurity when we have eaten them.

4. We shall have no stomach or entrails which we shall need to fill, as we do here with this devouring stomach. Instead, everything there exists in power; we shall eat in the mouth and not receive it into the body. We shall need no teeth to chew with, for there is mere power; and yet it exists in a true natural form and manner with shining colors.

5. And so the Kingdom of Heaven consists not in eating and drinking, but in peace and joy in the Holy Spirit, singing and playing among the works of God’s wonders and the loveliness of Paradise.

<margin>h Play, sport, or exercise.</margin>
6. We shall lead a life like children, who rejoice and are very merry in their games; for there will be no sadness in our hearts, or fear of anything, but a delightful h recreation with the angels.

7. This world will be no more thought upon or regarded, for all earthly knowledge and thoughts shall remain in the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: "Turba"; a term used by Boehme to describe the "turmoil" or "disturbance" of the fallen material world and the fire of God's wrath</note> of the earthly life, in the fire.

8. We shall have no more knowledge of our parents, children, or friends who are in Hell.

9. We shall all know one another among ourselves by name, though the earthly name shall remain in the <term>Turba</term>. We shall have a name according to our first name in the language of angels, which here in this life we do not understand. In the "Language of Nature" <note>The idea that there is a primal language where words perfectly express the essence of things</note> we understand something of it, but here we have no tongue to speak it with.

10. None shall say to another, "You are my husband," "You are my wife," "You are my daughter," "You are my son," "my servant," or "my handmaid." All are alike there; we are all children. There is neither husband nor wife, neither child, servant, nor maid, but all are free; every one is all. There is but one sex, namely, heavenly virgins full of modesty, chastity, and purity.

11. All of us together are God’s spouse, and he is our husband; he sows his power into us, and we bring him forth praise and glory.

12. There is such a kind of dancing and singing as children use...

# The Thirty-Second Question Answered

...when they hold hands, and sing and dance in a circle.

<margin>Enjoyed the great hidden wisdom, understanding, and Art.</margin>
13. All worldly arts will be of no account. But you must know that those who have possessed the Mystery <note>original: *Myſterie*; referring to the hidden spiritual knowledge of God's workings</note>, and to whom that has been revealed, shall have far greater wisdom and knowledge than others, and will excel others greatly.

<margin>Teaching, and ordering, or compulsion.</margin>
14. Indeed, this excellence is not for the purpose of doctrine or discipline <note>original: *coaction*; meaning forced obedience or legalistic rule</note>, but their wisdom will initiate all kinds of exercise in the heavenly Mystery to stir up joy. For just as children flock together when one begins a game, so it shall be here.

15. Little children are our schoolmasters until evil stirs within them and they embrace the *Great Turmoil* <note>original: *Turba Magna*; the chaotic, destructive element in nature and the soul</note>. They bring their playfulness from the mother’s womb, which is a remnant of Paradise; but all the rest is lost until we shall receive it again.

16. A King is of no more value there than a beggar. If he has governed well, then his virtue follows him, and he shall have the praise for it in the Divine Majesty; for he will obtain an excellent glorification, like a shepherd over his flock.

<margin>The thread of Faith at the last gasp.</margin>

He will be accounted of less value in the Kingdom of Heaven than a beggar who has been honest; indeed, he will not be as glorious as that beggar.

<margin>In the Kingdom of Heaven.</margin>
18. Everyone will be known by their works and what they have been, when they shall set forth their "merchandise" <note>their life's spiritual fruits</note> in the heavenly *Magic* <note>original: *Magia*; the creative, spiritual power of God</note>, just as children do in their play.

19. And yet you must know that it shall not be a kingdom of mere sport; rather, we shall speak of the wonders and wisdom of God, and of the great mysteries of the heavenly *Magic*. The song of the great Hunter <note>A reference to the triumph over the "Hunter" or oppressor</note> will continue there to the disgrace of the Devil and to the praise of God.

<margin>The song of Miriam, Moses' sister.
Persecutor, plaguer, or oppressor.</margin>
20. We shall have some knowledge of Hell, but see nothing of it except in the *Magic*, in the Mystery; for the devils must dwell in the darkness. The wrathful fire which is in them is their only light; they have eyes of fire to see with. All other fire is gone, for the Divine Majesty has swallowed it up <note>original: *allayed*; tempered or transformed</note> so that it may burn in Love.

21. Though indeed there is fire in the Center from which the Majesty arises, this will not be granted to the devils. They shall be thrust out into darkness, where there is howling and gnashing of teeth—indeed, more cold <note>original: *frost*</note> than heat.

## The Thirty-Third Question
### What kind of matter shall our bodies have in the life to come?

**I.**

<margin>Or strong.</margin>
My beloved friend, this is a very difficult <note>original: "mighty hard"</note> question; the outward man <note>The "outward man" refers to our physical, earthly nature and senses.</note> must let it alone and not meddle with it at all, for he is not worthy of it.

2. You know that God has become Man and has taken our flesh, blood, and soul upon Himself. Now Christ said, <margin>John 8:23; John 3:13</margin> *I am from above: No one goes into Heaven but the Son of Man who has come from Heaven, and is in Heaven.*

3. Do you understand what He meant when He said He is in Heaven? He spoke not only of His Deity—that is, of the Word—but of the Son of Man, even of that Word which became flesh. This is what we must now consider: for in that flesh and blood we must live eternally, and we must have Christ’s body if we are to exist in God.

4. Yet we know of no other body that we shall have but our <margin>Job 19:26, 27</margin> own body, growing out of the old body as a sprout grows from a kernel. <note>The author uses the metaphor of a seed to explain how the spiritual body emerges from the physical one.</note> Such a body Adam had at the Creation; but he was <margin>Or by the elements.</margin> captured by the kingdom of this world and so became earthly. This was his Fall, and this caused God to take a part of Adam and make a woman from it, as we have written at length in our third book. <note>Referring to the author's previous work, *The Threefold Life of Man*.</note>

5. Now we know well that Adam was a chaste virgin <note>In this context, "virgin" refers to a state of spiritual perfection and wholeness before the division of the sexes.</note> before his <margin>That is, a bestial, animal, mortal man.</margin> sleep and before Eve was made; but afterward he became a man, having a deformity like a beast, of which we are still ashamed to this very day in the sight of God, because we have earthly, bestial members for reproduction.

6. Now Adam had the Virgin of Divine Wisdom within him, but <margin>Or forsook it.</margin> when he fell, she remained immovable in her own principle, and Adam departed from her.

7. But know that Christ became Man in that Virgin [who was] within the earthly Mary; for the Word of the Lord brought that Virgin with it into the body of Mary.

8. And here you must understand that Christ became flesh in the water of eternal life (which flesh the whole Deity fills) and also in the <margin>Substance or properties.</margin> essences of the earthly Mary.

9. But Mary was blessed with the Heavenly Virgin, and so Christ became...

<summary>This page explores the nature of the resurrected body, arguing that it is a spiritual form growing out of the physical body, modeled after Christ's dual nature and Adam's pre-Fall state.</summary>
<keywords>Resurrection, Christology, Adam, Virgin of Divine Wisdom, Fall of Man, spiritual body, Mary</keywords>

--- Page 154 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the nature of Christ’s incarnation and the spiritual body that believers will receive in the resurrection.</meta>

He became man in a pure vessel, and the earthly man clung to him.

<margin>From the soul of Mary.</margin>
10. Because of the soul which he was to receive from Mary, he therefore had to receive Mary's flesh; yet this occurred within the blessing, specifically in the Heavenly Virgin <note>The "Heavenly Virgin" refers to the divine Sophia or the spiritual principle of humanity, distinct from Mary's physical, earthly nature</note> alone.

<margin>The earthly tincture, or substantial virtue.</margin>
11. The tincture <note>original: "Tincture"; here meaning the essential spiritual quality or life-force</note> of the blood in the heavenly Virgin was heavenly. The earthly tincture would not have been able to pass through the wrath of God and through death; much less would it have had the power to rise out of the grave.

12. That Word which became flesh possessed the water of eternal life, which proceeded from the Divine Majesty, and yet it was present in Mary's blood. For further information on this, we direct you to our third book, where it is described at length.

<margin>Or born.</margin>
13. And so we tell you: we shall have a body consisting of flesh and blood, a body just like the one Christ had; for Christ, through his incarnation, has become man within us.

14. When we are born anew of water and of the Spirit, then—within Christ's Spirit—we are born anew of Christ's flesh and blood; we "put on Christ."

15. Christ becomes born in the converted sinner, and that person becomes the child of God in Christ. This is the body we shall have in Heaven.

16. It will not be gross, beast-like flesh, such as we have in the "Old Adam" <note>The fallen, corrupt state of humanity inherited from Adam</note>, but subtle flesh and blood. It is the kind of flesh that can pass through wood and stone while they remain intact—just as Christ came to his disciples while the doors were shut. It is a body that has no *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "disturbance" or "turmoil" of the wrathful properties of nature that lead to destruction</note> or fragility. Hell cannot hold it; it is like eternity. And yet, it is real flesh and blood, which our heavenly hands shall touch, feel, and grasp; it is also a visible body, just like the one we have here in this world.

<margin>Comprehend, or receive.</margin>
17. Now, please consider: how is it possible for a body like the one we carry around here to be capable of containing the Divine Majesty? Surely it must be a body that is like that Majesty, so that the Majesty can shine forth from it, out of the tincture and water of eternal life.

<margin>Or not intelligible. Those who love God and are born of him.</margin>
18. Here, indeed, we are as if silent to the understanding of human reason; yet we are well enough understood by our brothers and sisters. This knowledge belongs to the children of God. A wolf desires to fill his mouth with the kind of flesh that fills his belly; we are not speaking of that kind of flesh, but of the kind Christ has given us in his Testament <note>Referring to the Covenant or the Sacrament of the Lord's Supper</note> and left as a remembrance—an earnest <note>A pledge or down payment</note> that he will remain with us forever: we in him, and he in us.

19. Therefore we say that we shall have the Divine Body and Christ’s body, which fills Heaven. We shall not remain merely as his creatures, but be joined to one another as members, brothers, and children.

<summary>This page explains that the resurrected body of a believer will be a "subtle" spiritual flesh, modeled after Christ's glorified body, capable of passing through matter and reflecting the Divine Majesty. It distinguishes this heavenly substance from the "gross" flesh of the fallen human state.</summary>
<keywords>Incarnation, Resurrection Body, Tincture, Turba, Old Adam, Heavenly Virgin, Divine Majesty, New Birth</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author's exploration of the nature of the soul and the afterlife, specifically transitioning from the divine origin of life to the state of those who are lost.</meta>

20. There is but one Life in us all; nothing is truly mortal. All things proceed from the Eternal One. Nothing has had a beginning except for the "Wonders" <note>original: "Wonders" — likely referring to the manifested works or phenomena of creation as opposed to the eternal essence</note> alone. One Essentiality has come out of the Eternal: we are as Gods; we are true Children of God, proceeding from His very essence in both body and soul.

## The thirty-fourth Question.
### *What is the miserable and horrible state of the damned?*

->I.<-

<margin>As if in the furthest or outer darkness.</margin>
1. It has been sufficiently declared already: for God’s wrath in the Darkness is their dwelling place. Their light is that which shines from their fiery eyes, like the glimmering of a flash of fire; they have no light at all but that. For they dwell in that which is most outer; and so they ascend in haughtiness above the thrones like strong champions; and yet they have different characteristics among them, according to how their spirits differ.

2. For a dog acts like a dog, a wolf like a wolf; and so a horse, a bird, a toad, or a serpent—every one according to its kind. Yet they are all more speedy and swift than thoughts.

3. They take their joy in their abominations, and their chiefest joy is to scorn God, because they are fiery spirits and God is a Spirit of Light.

4. Their boast is always of their strong, fiery might; they are like a dragon that spits fire. They seek destruction and find abominations.

5. They also have fruit growing out of their own Principle, matching the abominations of their wills.

<margin>Jesting, jeering, scoffing, and deriding with strange, ape-like gestures of the face and body.</margin>
6. They have a sport like those who play with fireworks, such as rockets and balls of fire; also spitting fire out of their mouths. Fooling, tumbling, and juggling is their pastime—though indeed there is no "time," nor any fear of any other torment after the last Judgment Day! But their whole life is a continual fear, horror, terror, and lamentation. Everyone has his work (which he did here while he lived) present in the Figure <note>original: "in the Figure" — the spiritual imprint or mental image of one's earthly deeds</note>, which awakens the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba" — a Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "disturber" or the spirit of wrath and destruction that throws elements into confusion</note> there, and so he rides in the fire.

7. The soul has no physical feeling of it because the soul itself is fire; but the *Turba* plagues it with those abominations which it introduced. There is an eternal despairing in them, and therefore they are God’s enemies.

<meta>Continuity: The text continues on the next page with point 8.</meta>

<summary>The author describes the state of the damned as a self-inflicted existence within the "Darkness" and "Wrath" of God, where spirits act out their earthly vices in a state of eternal despair and mockery.</summary>
<keywords>Damned, Darkness, Turba, Wrath of God, Fiery Spirits, Judgment Day, Abominations, Afterlife</keywords>

# The thirty-fourth Question Answered.

<margin>Strong abominations, malice, or wickedness.</margin>
8. To blaspheme God is their greatest power. They devour hellish brimstone and abominations, for their fruits are a kind of substance that is outwardly fair, but inwardly mere wrathfulness. Such hypocrites as they have been upon Earth, such bread does their "Heaven" <note>The author uses "Heaven" ironically here to describe the environment the wicked have created for themselves.</note> afford them to eat.

9. They are at liberty and not shut up at all; they may descend as deep as they will, for the abyss and darkness is everywhere. And yet, they remain only in their original place. The deeper they desire to throw themselves, the deeper they fall, and yet they find no end or bottom.

<margin>Their time is not the time of Man. Refreshment.</margin>
10. Their number is not the number of any human time. Their breath <note>original: "refocillation," meaning a restorative breath or refreshment.</note> is a mere stink of fire and brimstone. When they consider themselves in their abominations—that they were once Angels and are now Devils—then the gnawing worm immediately arises, which bites and torments them.

11. To what end should their wickednesses be described? They are evil, unclean beasts. That which they practiced on Earth follows them, and they desire to do the same there also; they drink down abomination and wickedness without measure.

<margin>Or Dominion. Or Beast. Shadow, or Resemblance.</margin>
12. Their government is no better understood than by looking at the Antichristian beast <note>original: "horse," likely referring to the pale horse or the mounts of the apocalypse.</note> and scornful men who rave with cursing and blaspheming. Yet this is but a mirror <note>original: "glasse."</note> of the hellish abominations. We will not mention them any further, for they are not worth speaking of.

## The thirty-fifth Question.
### *What is the Enochian Life; and how long is it to continue?*

-> I. <-

<margin>The Enochian life is brought forth. Bounds, or appointed time. Time, or Age. Or wonders.</margin>
This is also above human reason; no outward reason can comprehend it. But seeing it is born <note>meaning it has been revealed or brought to light.</note>, it must be made manifest. For there are such mysteries couched in it as the world is not able to conceive, nor should we mention them all, for they have their limit as to how far they shall be discussed. For in this age, miracles <note>original: "wonders."</note> are yet to be done upon the Earth; for which cause, our speech is taken away, and we must remain in silence.

2. Indeed, we may declare what kind of life it is, or where Enoch has gone, as also Elijah <note>original: "Elias."</note> and Moses. It is no fiction; we declare only what is given to us here. We must not mention anything further,

# The thirty-fifth Question Answered.

We must not believe *Reason* <note>original: "Reason" - here referring to human logic unaided by divine revelation</note>, for it is a fool in this matter.

<margin>Born, or come.</margin>
<margin>Or prophesy.</margin>
<margin>Or the Sword.</margin>
3. But we may well speak something of it, for the time is born for *Enoch* to speak and *Elias* <note>Elijah</note> to work again; which *Babel* <note>The "City of Confusion," representing the corrupt, worldly church or society</note> shall find by experience. For *Moses* has horns <note>original: "hornes" - a traditional reference to the rays of light or power emanating from Moses' face</note>, and yet he is a patient Lamb.

<margin>Or Beams, or Rays which with their light shall contend with darkness.</margin>
4. Oh, how you will rejoice if you get into *Moses’s* flock, for he has a good message: Rejoice, O heaven, and skip for joy, O Earth! For *Enoch* is in the field and keeps his flock.

5. What does *Elias* desire? For he is clothed with a white garment! He was with Christ on the Mount <note>The Mount of Transfiguration</note> and spoke of the completion of man’s redemption; he spoke also of the entering into Paradise, and of the final deliverance from the Hunter <note>original: "the Hunter" - likely referring to the devil or the "driver" of worldly oppression</note>.

<margin>Or driver.</margin>
6. He that is born blind sees nothing. How can a lame man win the prize? Or a deaf man distinguish languages? Does not the sun shine daily, and yet the mole remains blind? Shall *Babel* come to see? We know she is a scorner, and therefore she must be blind even though the sun shines clearly upon her.

7. How can he behold two worlds, who always lives only in one? Or is it not Art and wit <note>Human cleverness and intellectual skill</note> that has understanding able to search out the deep gates? Yet they pass away like a wind which brings forth nothing, though it makes a boasting noise; and so does *Babel*.

<margin>Art and wit.</margin>

8. When we wish to speak of the Enochian Life, we must look into the Scripture and see who *Enoch* was and what life he led; and then we may soon find where he is, and what his "taking up" or translation <note>The act of being taken to heaven without dying</note> was.

<margin>Genesis 5:18.</margin>
<margin>See the *Mysterium Magnum*, chapter 30, verses 19, 20.</margin>
9. You know that the Scripture says his father’s name was Jared; if you understood the Language of Nature <note>A mystical concept of a universal language that reveals the essence of things</note>, you would have the whole foundation already.

10. And *Enoch* fathered *Methuselah*, who attained the highest age of man; and after he had fathered him, he continued in a divine life until the Lord took him away into His own Principle <note>The divine realm or state of being</note>.

11. But we must not understand it as if he were wholly perfect in the light of the Divine Majesty and should not appear at the Day of Judgment. Indeed, he is in God without death or want of anything; yes, he is in God’s love, yet only in the birth of the Divine Principle, for he also had *Adam’s* flesh.

12. And you know well that the outward kingdom with the earthly flesh belongs to the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba" - the spirit of wrath, confusion, or destruction in nature</note>; and although it is clear that he had the "body of the wonders of God" within his outward body—in which divine body of wonders he was taken away into the Mystery—the outward body was, as it were, swallowed up by the Mystery.

13. Yet, now the Mystery must give up all whatever it has devoured:

<summary>The author discusses the spiritual significance of Enoch and Elijah, explaining that Enoch was "translated" into a divine state while still possessing human nature, serving as a precursor to the final redemption of humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Enoch, Elias, Babel, Moses, Language of Nature, Turba, Mystery, Translation, Redemption</keywords>

# The thirty-fifth Question Answered.

13. ...devoured: as you know, that at the end, the outward body must appear with all its <margin>f Substance, or Essences.</margin> works before the Judgment. Thus the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; the "turmoil" or "disturbance," referring to the destructive, wrathful element in nature that leads to decay and judgment</note> is in the outward body along with the Wonders, which shall be made manifest and tested in the Fire.

14. Now then, if Enoch was taken up in this way—both body and soul (with both the inner and outer bodies)—then the outward body is in the <margin>g In the outward secret hiddenness.</margin> *Mysterium* <note>original: *Mysterium*; the outward hidden mystery of the physical world</note>, and the inward body is in the <margin>h In the inward secret hiddenness.</margin> *Arcanum* <note>original: *Arcanum*; the inward, sacred secret or heavenly mystery</note>. He lives in two mysteries, being invisible and incomprehensible to the outward world. Thus we give you to understand that Paradise is still present and has not perished; though it seems to be devoured by the curse of God, it still remains as a Mystery within that curse, uncorrupted.

15. For we are able to say with a firm foundation in Truth that Paradise is still upon the Earth. Yet we are not in it, but Enoch is in it. He still has the body of the *Turba* within the Mystery, and in the <margin>i Or Arcanum.</margin> Heavenly Mystery he has the Divine body—a Paradisiacal Body which is capable of inhabiting Paradise. Thus he exists as a Wonder and is a prophet in the Crown at the <margin>k Of the End.</margin> Limit of the Wonders.

16. For you know that the Scripture says: after he had fathered Methuselah (the man of the greatest age), he continued afterward in a Divine Life; and this has a deep meaning.

17. Methuselah signifies the end of the wonders of this world. Enoch remaining in his Divine Life for three hundred years after the birth of Methuselah signifies the manifestation of the Wonders and a clear ministry—namely, a preaching of Righteousness. Through this, the *Turba* of every person shall be shown to them, and the End of the Wonders of this world shall be declared: specifically, the vengeance of God and His reward to the good.

18. The time after Enoch (during which Methuselah lived to the <margin>l That is, the end of an Age, or Century.</margin> Number of the Crown) when Enoch and his preaching were taken up, signifies that the Enochian Light which shone in his time will enter again into its Principle. It will seek out the earthly body which Enoch had and will find that the *Turba* is still in it. Then there will be no further seeking, for the *Turba* is found at the Limit, and it works toward the Fire and Judgment.

19. And thus the end of the world is like the dregs, and it works in the *Turba* to blow up the Fire and the Judgment. For the outward world was produced out of the *Turba* and took its beginning in the *Turba*, and the *Turba* is its own property; thus the beginning seeks the end again in the wrath.

20. And just as this world was physical within the wrath, so the Spirit will have the beginning meet the end again in the wrath, for the Beginning and the End are one. You also plainly perceive that in the Beginning, the *Turba* devoured Adam, brought him into the Anger, and murdered Abel.

<summary>The author explains that Enoch exists in a dual state within Paradise, which remains hidden on Earth, and that his life serves as a prophetic timeline for the world's eventual judgment by the Turba.</summary>

Then Babylon must drink her last <margin>draught, or the very dregs.</margin>

41. Yet it will remain hidden to the wicked until the *Turba* devours them; for they look upon this just as the Jews looked upon Christ, and the first world looked upon Noah. What does the mystery profit a scorner? He looks after nothing but eating and drinking, and takes care only how to satisfy his haughty mind so that he may ride with pomp in Babylon.

42. As a wise man, consider further of it, for we dare not speak otherwise of it. Our understanding and will are driven into such a phrase, and we do not have permission in this place at this time to write more extensively or more fundamentally in plain words.

43. But if it shall please God to grant that we may write something upon the first and also upon the second book of Moses, something more should be revealed. For the names of the fathers before the flood which are set down there all belong to the Mystery, and they contain great wonders in them. When it is day <note>meaning a time of spiritual enlightenment</note>, you shall clearly know the whole course of the world in it.

# The thirty-sixth Question.
## *What is the soul of the Messiah, or Christ?*

->I.<-

1. We have sufficiently cleared this in our third book, *Of the Threefold Life of Man*. However, because everyone who reads this may not have that book, and in regard to the question itself, we must answer somewhat more here. Therefore, I set this down: for you ask in the following question about Christ’s spirit, which was <margin>readily obedient or submissive</margin> and which he commended to his Father.

->2. Here<-

<summary>The author describes the spiritual cycles of history, the eventual destruction of corrupt religious systems (Babylon), and begins an inquiry into the specific nature of Christ's soul and spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Babylon, Turba, Enochian life, end times, Moses, Elijah, Noah, Messiah, Christ, Threefold Life of Man</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the restoration of humanity through Christ, following the previous exploration of the soul's origin and the fall of Adam.</meta>

# The Thirty-Sixth Question Answered

<margin>Or Mankind.</margin>
2. Here the <note>original: "Old and sick Adam"</note> old and sick humanity shall be comfortably refreshed; he shall have a <margin>Or Cure for Death.</margin> medicine against death and be <margin>Or made alive.</margin> quickened again. For his mother shall bring forth a young son to live in her bosom, and he shall exceedingly rejoice in him.

3. If we would consider the soul of Christ, we need only seek and find ourselves; for Christ’s soul is a human soul, conceived in Mary, the <margin>Namely: the Eternal wisdom of God and the outward humanity; that is, God and Man.</margin> twofold Virgin.

4. Yet we do not acknowledge the outward, mortal life in *Mary* as a pure Virgin. For that which is mortal contains the Anger and the <term>Turba</term> <note>Latin: "turba" meaning disturbance, turmoil, or the destructive element in nature that leads to decay.</note> which corrupts all purity. Therefore, no pure virgin is born of *Eve*, but all women are her daughters.

5. And *Eve* herself was but half a virgin, for *Adam* was the other half, according to the two <term>Tinctures</term> <note>In Boehme’s alchemy-influenced theology, "tinctures" refer to the essential spiritual qualities or "colors" of the soul and spirit—the masculine/fiery and feminine/light principles.</note>. In these, man saw himself to be wholly a virgin in pure love, and so saw God through himself; that is, through the creature, he saw the Original Source which produced those two principles out of itself.

6. And thus also in one <margin>The whole and not divided person, as Adam was before he slept.</margin> whole person, there is one pure love and chastity. For it seeks no other union; it is itself the union of both Tinctures—namely, the Tincture of the soul and the Tincture of the spirit. Its power was such that it could bring forth a spirit out of the fiery Tincture, which is called a soul and spirit.

7. Which *Adam* <margin>Extinguished, or put out.</margin> lost when he allowed the earthly life to take him captive. Therefore, he had to be divided, and a woman had to be made out of him. She must set her love, <margin>Longing delight, or lust.</margin> desire, and imagination upon the Adamic fiery Tincture if she would become pregnant with a soul.

8. Thus, none can say that *Eve* was a pure and chaste virgin before her contact with *Adam*. For as soon as *Adam* awoke from sleep, he saw her standing by him and immediately set his <margin>Fancy, or Desire; or lusted after her.</margin> imagination upon her. He took her to himself and said, "This is flesh of my flesh, and bone of my bone; she shall be called Woman, because she is taken from Man."

9. And she (*Eve*) instantly set her imagination upon *Adam*, and so both were mutually kindled with the desire for each other.

10. Where is the pure chastity and modesty now? Is it not bestial? Has the outward image not become a beast? This is plain enough to see in his will and <margin>Or doings.</margin> essence: he acts like a beast—indeed, more foolishly. For he has reason, and yet he runs on against reason as if he were void of sense.

11. But so that he might be restored and the image reduced back into unity, that Word which spoke the soul out of the mouth of God—and breathed it from the Holy Spirit into the human image—has become Man. He has entered into the earthly image, which is to say, into the *Turba* of destruction.

<summary>This page explains the fall of Adam and Eve as a loss of spiritual unity and "virginity," resulting in a bestial, divided nature. It concludes that Christ, the Word, entered this corrupted state to restore humanity to its original divine unity.</summary>

<keywords>Adam, Eve, Christ, Mary, Turba, Tinctures, Fall of Man, Restoration, Virginity, Imagination</keywords>

--- Page 163 ---
12. And you know very well that the Word has the water of Eternal Life and the Fire—which is the Deity—and out of the Fire it has the Tincture <note>original: "Tincture"; in Boehme’s thought, this refers to the spiritual essence or "dye" that gives quality and life to a thing</note> of the Deity. And in that Tincture is the Spirit of God, which proceeds from the mouth of God; and in that proceeding forth, the <margin>Or Luster.</margin> glance of the Majesty is made manifest through the operation of the Spirit.

13. This Word—which exists in the Virgin of the Wisdom of God and is encompassed by the wonders of eternity—has now, in the humility of great love toward our image which was lost in Adam, come into us again. In Mary (understand the earthly Mary, yet within the Benediction <note>The divine blessing or state of grace</note>), the Word has become Man.

14. The Benediction was this: that the soul of Mary was adorned with the heavenly Virgin of the Wisdom of God, which Adam had lost. Therefore, the Angel called her "blessed among all women."

15. No woman from Adam until that time was ever clothed with the heavenly Virgin except for this Mary. Therefore, by the blessing, she became chaste and full of purity. For the Holy Spirit does not enter into that which is earthly; he does not mix himself with the <margin>Similitude, or Resemblance.</margin> glass <note>original: "Glasse"; meaning a mirror or a mere reflection of the true reality</note>, for it is impossible for the reflection to be the same as the Life itself.

16. Understand our high and precious depth in this way: The soul of man proceeds from God and is from the Eternal, while the body of man is but a reflection of the Eternal. Thus, God clothed the soul of Mary with the Divine Virgin within the "Principle" <note>The fundamental spiritual level</note> of the soul, not in the earthly flesh as if she herself had been turned into a god. No! She had to die just like all other people.

17. And in this Virgin, God’s Word—coming out of the Heart of God the Father—assumed the seed of the woman: namely, the seed of the soul and the seed of the first Image, which for such a long time had remained hidden in the Mystery.

18. But now at last, the Life of God entered into it and made it a whole Image again. For the water of Eternal Life, proceeding from the Heart of God, mingled with the water of the spirit of the soul; for the spirit takes its origin from the water, and the soul is Fire.

19. Thus the Word received the Tincture of the soul, and the Holy Spirit received the Tincture of the spirit (that is, the Tincture of the water), and both became one soul. Yet the creaturely part was still distinct from God’s Spirit, even though God’s Spirit dwelt within it. From God’s water and Tincture, and from the seed of Mary—from her own tincture and water in that high blessing—flesh and blood were produced, so that a Heavenly Man was incarnated within the earthly man all at once.

20. So it may be said: this is the Son of the woman; namely, the very bodily and natural Son of Mary, with both soul and body.

<summary>This page explains the mystical process of the Incarnation, arguing that Christ assumed human nature through Mary by uniting the divine "Virgin Wisdom" with the human soul. It distinguishes between Mary's earthly physical nature and the divine blessing that allowed her to bear the "Heavenly Man" within the earthly form.</summary>

# The thirty-sixth Question Answered.

with flesh and blood, and all that belongs to Man; and also the very Son of God, who was born from Eternity out of God’s Eternal Essence before the foundation of the world was laid. He stood both in the Majesty of the Holy Trinity <note>original: "Ternary"; referring to the three-fold nature of God</note> and also in the <margin>Or womb.</margin> body of *Mary* at the same time.

21. And the soul of Christ belongs half to the Principle of this world, and half to the Holy Ghost. For the soul of Christ used the Spirit of the Air and Stars, with the power of the Elements, and also the Word of God and the Divine food; for such a man *Adam* was in his state of Innocence.

22. Thus God has regenerated us in Christ; and so we are regenerated in Christ out of God’s word and Spirit, by the water of Eternal Life, and so we are God’s Children in Christ. If we resign ourselves up to Christ, departing from our own human Reason and will, then we put on Christ’s body, and our will and Spirit live from Christ, who is in us and we in him.

23. From this you may understand what the Temptation of Christ was: namely, the Regenerate man had to endure *Adam’s* Temptation <gloss>to test</gloss> whether his soul could stand steadfast in God. Therefore he was tested in the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a term used by Boehme to describe the "turmoil" or "disturbance" of the wrath of God in nature</note> <gloss>to test</gloss> whether he could stand steadfast in the three Principles <note>The three realms: the dark fire-world, the light holy-world, and the outer physical world</note> and rule over the outward Life. Therefore food was withdrawn from the outward Life, and the Inward had to overpower the outward, eating of the word of the Lord and upholding the outward life by its own Power and full Omnipotence. He also had to keep Death captive, so that it might not be able to destroy the outward Life; this must have been a hard Combat!

24. And the other two Temptations were these: namely, he was <margin>Or complete.</margin> tempted <gloss>to test</gloss> whether man would live in full obedience and suffer God to work in him, or whether he would exalt himself again and be free from God, as *Lucifer* did. Therefore the Devil had to tempt him, because this man was to possess his <note>Lucifer's</note> Royal Throne.

25. The Devil complained that he could not stand <note>remain in heaven</note> because the Mother of wrathfulness <note>The source of divine anger or the dark principle in nature</note> pulled him too hard. Therefore he was permitted to test this man, and to set before him that which was set before himself. If this man stood firm, then he should judge the Devil, who was found to be a liar.

26. For he fully tested him in the second and third temptation: whether he would ascend on high by his own power, as he himself had done, and so stir up the Anger; or whether he would put his trust and confidence in God only, and live to him both in will and deed, as a child in obedience to the Father. And the Devil urged this upon him for exactly as long as *Adam* stood in his Temptation before he fell asleep <note>Referring to the mystical "sleep" of Adam before the creation of Eve, which Boehme views as the beginning of the fall</note>.

27. And now we also must always be so tempted and tested.

<summary>This page explains Christ's dual nature as both eternal God and elemental man, detailing how his victory over the Devil's temptations reversed Adam's fall and secured the path for human regeneration.</summary>

And we are able to achieve victory in Christ, who has overcome; for his soul is our soul, and his flesh is our flesh, if we trust in him and give ourselves up wholly to him, just as Christ gave himself up to his Father.

28. And thus, my beloved friend, you understand what Christ’s soul and body is: namely, that it is our soul and body if we cleave to God. But if we do not, then we are parted; in our outward life, we belong to the spirit of this world—that is, to the lost and perished *Adam*—and in our soul, we belong to the Devil in the anger of God. But look for these things more at large in our other writings, where you shall find the whole foundation of Heaven and of this world.

## The thirty-seventh Question.

### What is the Spirit of Christ which was *willing, and which he commended into his Father’s hands?

<margin>* Or obedient</margin>

->1.<-

<margin>
l Treasure, Gem, or Pearl.
m Or the philosopher's stone. <note>original: "Philosophers stone" — a legendary substance in alchemy capable of turning base metals into gold; here used metaphorically for spiritual perfection.</note>
n Great Mystery. <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"</note>
</margin>
This is that great and excellent ^l Jewel; and we exceedingly rejoice that we know it, so that we are able to know ourselves and what we are. It is worth more to us than all the world, for it is that Pearl of which Christ said: a man sold all that he had and bought that Pearl.

<margin>
o Or trusting in God; and enduring whatever he lays upon us.
p Or spoiler.
q That is, in the cabinet where the Pearl lies.
</margin>
2. For it is more profitable to man than the whole world; it is more precious than the Sun, for the ^m noble stone of the wise men lies therein. It holds the heavenly and earthly ^n *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"</note>; and there is nothing in this world to be compared to it except ^o sincere simplicity, which is quiet and brings forth or stirs up no *Turba* <note>original: "Turba" — a term used by Boehme to describe a state of disturbance, wrath, or confusion</note>; and that simplicity has the Jewel hidden within it.

3. As gold lies couched in the stone and is safe—unless a ^p robber comes with the earthly *Turba* and destroys it, yet he himself gains nothing by it—so "self-reason" <note>human logic independent of divine revelation</note> is a robber in the ^q Mystery.

<margin>
r Layman, or unlearned person.
s Or Father.
t Or disputes.
u Or respect of persons.
</margin>
4. Therefore, we may say with good reason that a simple, ^r plain man—who simply, without a multiplicity of science, depends on ^s God—possesses the *Great Mystery* better and more securely, and indeed less decayed, than a highly learned ^f Doctor <note>a scholar or theologian</note> who soars aloft in his own reason, ^t wrangles, ^f spoils the Jewel, and ^u places it in *Babel* <note>a state of spiritual confusion or false religious systems</note>. This saying will not be well-received, yet that is nothing to us; we must speak the truth without ^x partiality.

<summary>The author explains that Christ's spirit and the "Great Mystery" are found through humble simplicity rather than intellectual pride. He warns that human reason often acts as a "robber" that obscures spiritual truth, which is more easily grasped by a simple person trusting in God.</summary>
<keywords>Christ's Spirit, Mysterium Magnum, Pearl of Great Price, Turba, Babel, spiritual simplicity vs. human reason, alchemy metaphor</keywords>

--- Page 166 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the nature of Christ's Spirit and its relationship to the soul and the divine image.</meta>

# The thirty-seventh Question Answered.

5. Now when we speak of Christ’s Spirit, human Reason thinks it is the soul, or perhaps the Spirit of the outward Life (which consists of the power and operation of the Stars and Elements). But it is not so. It is something else entirely, in which the Image of God consists. The outward Spirit does not belong to the Deity, but to the Wonders <note>The "Wonders" refers to the created, temporal world and its various manifestations.</note>.

6. We have spoken somewhat of this already; but because this Question reminds us of it again—mentioning that when he died, he commended it to his Father—we must say a little more about how that was done.

<margin>The Center of Nature.</margin>
7. You sufficiently perceive in what manner the soul is the Center of Nature, the Origin of Life and movement—namely, God’s Fire. This fire should be continually turned toward the Eternal will of God, in which it is originally born from the Magical Desire. It is a great Secret come out of the Eternal Nothing, in which all things are contained: even the Deity with all the three Principles <note>The three Principles are the dark world (fire/will), the light world (love/spirit), and the outward world (nature).</note>, and every being <note>original: "y Being"; meaning thing, essence, or substance.</note> that can be named.

8. And you perceive that the Light and the Spirit of the Air proceed from the fire, and also that the fire draws the Spirit of the Air back into itself, and so always fans its own flame. Thus, with the Light, Air, and source <note>original: "z source"; meaning property or quality.</note> of the Fire, it is its own Life.

9. And further, we have said something of the Noble Tincture <note>term: Tincture; in Boehme's alchemy, this is the spiritual essence or "dye" that transforms and gives life/quality to matter.</note> which arises in the Light, in which the gentleness of the Light consists. It comes forth from the Anguish (which is like a dying away) and springs forth fresh through that mortifying Anguish as a life having another property <note>original: "a property"; meaning source or quality.</note>. Here, the property of the fire is a kind of Tincture, like the driving forth of a Spirit; and yet it is desiring, and thereby it attracts the power of the Light into itself and makes it an Essence—namely, water.

10. And in that are two Forms: One according to the source of Fire, which is red, containing the power—namely, *Sulphur* <note>In this context, Sulphur represents the active, combustible principle of life.</note>. The other (which is like a thin gentleness, yet having substance) is water. The desiring Tincture contracts these together into one and changes them into Blood.

11. Now the Origin in the Blood—namely, Fire (which is a warmth, that is, a Tincture)—is a Life. In the power of the Tincture, the thin water of the Life proceeds, one power <note>The outward power.</note> proceeding forth from another <note>The inward power.</note>. The power always re-absorbs that which goes forth; and that which has gone forth is free from the Fire and also from the power, for it has gone forth, and yet it arose from that power.

12. And this is the true Spirit which is born out of the soul, in which the Image of God and the Divine Virgin of God’s Wisdom <note>The "Divine Virgin" is a recurring figure in Boehme's work representing Sophia, or the mirror of God's wisdom.</note> consist; for all understanding and knowledge lie in this Spirit.

<summary>The author explains that Christ's Spirit is distinct from the soul and the physical life, describing it as a divine "Tincture" born from the soul's fire that contains the true Image of God and divine wisdom.</summary>
<keywords>Christ's Spirit, soul, Center of Nature, Tincture, three Principles, Divine Wisdom, Sulphur, spiritual anatomy</keywords>

--- Page 167 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the soul's relationship with God and the specific victory of Christ over death and the devil during the crucifixion.</meta>

It has the senses and the noble life which unites itself with God; this Spirit is so subtle that it can and may enter into God.

<margin>Reason, subtlety, or wit.</margin>
13. If this Spirit resigns itself to God and casts away the ostentation and <insert>reasoning</insert> cunning of the fire of its own soul, then it attains the Image of God, the Divine body. For it puts its will into God and dwells in God with Power; thus it is clothed with the Divine Essentiality <note>original: "Essentiality"; refers to the spiritual substance or "heavenly corporeality" of God</note> and is outside of this world, living in the Life of God.

14. But seeing as this Spirit arises first out of the Center of Nature—that is, out of the Life of Fire (although it is not the life of the Fire itself, but the Spirit of it, and the Life of the Fire stands originally in the Abyss, in the source of God’s Anger)—therefore Christ did not commend this Spirit of his to the fire’s own life, but into the hands of his Father.

15. His hands are the Love-Desire with which he embraces our Spirit when we enter into him and commend ourselves to him.

16. For when his body was to die on the Cross and his soul was to pass through Hell—through the Anger of God—the Devils waited there and thought to themselves, "We will surely keep the soul in our *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "turmoil" or "disturbance" that leads to destruction and divine wrath</note> in the Fire." And then Christ commended the Spirit into the Love of God.

17. And so the soul of Christ, with the Spirit, came into God’s hand while being encompassed by the Fire of Anger and Death. Death would have held it there, but Death was destroyed and confounded.

18. For Death smothered the outward Spirit—namely, the outward Life—and then thought, "Now surely the soul must remain in the *Turba*." But there was one stronger in the soul, namely the Word of God, which took death captive, destroyed the Anger, and quenched the wrath with the Love in the Spirit of Christ.

19. It was a poison to Hell for the Love of God to come into it and smother it in the soul; it was also a Plague, Death, and Destruction to Death itself. Death must now suffer an Eternal Life to grow within it.

20. Thus the Spirit of Christ took the Devil captive, drove him out of the Fire of the soul, and cast him into Darkness. He shut him up under darkness, away from the Fire of the soul and away from God’s Fire, into the wrathful harshness and bitterness of the cold; there let him warm himself, lest he freeze with cold.

21. Consider the first four Forms of Nature <note>In Boehme's cosmology, the first four forms are the "dark" or "harsh" qualities of nature (contraction, friction, etc.) before they are transformed by light</note>, and you will understand what the Devil’s Mansion is. For before Christ came, the Devil kept the soul captive in the *Turba* with the Fire; and although he did not possess the Spirit of the soul, he held the root of it in the *Turba*. But then he was commanded to cease; he was thrown out and driven into Darkness, and thus his malice was destroyed by

<summary>This page explains how Christ's Spirit overcame the "Turba" (destruction) and the Devil during the crucifixion by surrendering to God's love rather than the fire of nature. It describes the transformation of death into eternal life and the casting of the Devil into the "cold" darkness of the lower forms of nature.</summary>

# The thirty-seventh Question Answered.

### Christ’s descending into Hell, and Christ became his Judge.

22. Thus we have briefly described what the Spirit of Christ and our spirit is; namely, not the outward spirit <note>The "outward spirit" refers to the physical breath or the worldly psyche.</note>, but the spirit of the soul; not the soul itself, but the spirit of its life.

23. As in God, the Holy Trinity <note>original: "Ternary"</note> is distinct, being three Persons in one Essence, and yet but one only God; where the Son has the Spirit—that is, the Life—issuing forth from the Heart and mouth; <margin>The Fountain, or Property.</margin> and the Heart is the flame of Love; and the Father the source of Anger, which is made meek by the Son in Love, so that in God there is but one only will and Essence.

24. Thus it is also in Man, and no otherwise, not even in the smallest detail. Whatever God in Christ is, that we also are in Christ, in God; his children. Therefore, let us also commend our spirit into his hands, and so we may be able to pass through Death into Life, into God, with Christ.

<margin>f Rattles, fooleries, baubles, shells, or outside shows, imitation, or mimic tricks.
g Or prattled.
h Or perfection.
i Or wit.
k Or simple layman.
l Sum, or Total.
m Or layperson.
n Disciple.</margin>
25. Therefore, do not be led about and gulled with <insert>f</insert> toys and trifles as you have been until now in *Babel* <note>Babel refers to the "City of Confusion," representing the divided and argumentative state of the institutional church.</note>; where this and that has been <insert>g</insert> disputed about the soul and its spirit, one this way and another that way. There is no solid ground among them, but mere fiction and opinion.

26. Understanding is born in God, not in the schools from academic art. Yet we do not despise academic art; for if art is born in God, it is a tenfold mystery, for it always attains the <insert>h</insert> tenth number <note>The "tenth number" symbolizes completion or divine perfection in mystical numerology.</note> in <insert>i</insert> reason much better than a <insert>k</insert> plain man; for it can make <insert>l</insert> one total out of many numbers.

27. But it is not in man's own power; no, one must enter in by the Cross as well as the other, whether he be a Doctor <note>A highly educated scholar or theologian.</note> or a <insert>m</insert> mean man <note>A person of low social or academic standing.</note>. God's secrets will admit no "Doctors" but only <insert>n</insert> scholars <note>original: "Schollers" (disciples/learners)</note> into them; yet a learned scholar may reach very far.

28. Had this hand but the "High Art" and also those high gifts, then you should see more from it. But God will have it as it is. It is indeed his pleasure to make the wisdom of this world foolishness and to give his power to the weak, so that all <insert>o</insert> may bow down before him and acknowledge that he alone is the Lord who does whatever he wills.
<margin>o All life, or creatures.</margin>

<summary>The author explains the relationship between the human spirit and the Trinity, arguing that true spiritual understanding comes from God rather than academic study or the "Babel" of religious disputes. He emphasizes that both the learned and the simple must enter divine mysteries through the humility of the Cross.</summary>

# The thirty-eighth Question.

### *What are the things that shall come to pass at the End of the world?*

---

->1.<-

Beloved friend, it is not fitting for me to answer this question of yours, nor is it within my own power. Furthermore, it is not appropriate for anyone to ask it, for it is the secret counsel of God. Let no one endeavor to be equal with God or to foreknow all things.

2. Our knowledge consists in the Spirit and will of God. When that Spirit moves, then knowledge goes forward in the Heavenly *Magia* <note>original: "Magia"; refers to the divine creative power or wisdom</note> and enters into the wonders of the earthly. Then the prophet is born, for he stands upon the <term>Crown</term> <note>The "Crown" refers to the highest point of spiritual attainment or the limit of time and eternity</note> and speaks magically of the beginning of the wonders and their *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; refers to the "turmoil" or "disturbance" that leads to destruction and judgment</note>. He shows how they shall come to an end, be destroyed, and be brought back again into the first state.

<margin>Or at the end of a time, or Age.</margin>

3. For all prophets speak from the *Turba*; they discover that which is false and declare what is better, which enters into the will of God.

4. Therefore, please do not urge us with this question, for we shall be captivated in the *Turba* by it. You may understand enough from all the other questions regarding what is to be done hereafter; we have hinted it to you clearly enough.

<margin>Or by way of similitude, or in parables.</margin>

5. We dare not speak any otherwise than in a magical manner <note>meaning through symbolic or spiritual insight rather than literal prediction</note> of things to come. This is because the wonders to come are all seen in the *Turba*; now, when the Spirit sees them, it declares clearly and plainly how the *Turba* is loaded with evil or good.

6. But behold, all things are mixed. For God has become Man and sets His mercy everywhere within the Anger, hindering perdition. Therefore, the prophet must speak magically and not in express and plain terms. For it often comes to pass that though a thing be evil in its essence <note>original: "Essence" or "work"</note>, yet there soon grows a sprout out from the wickedness which destroys the *Turba* <note>or keeps back vengeance</note> and causes a conversion.

<margin>
As at Nineveh in Jonah's time.
</margin>

7. Therefore, God admonishes you to subdue the firmamental heaven <note>the astrological or planetary influences on the human soul</note> and to oppose it; and so the evil which the firmamental heaven pours forth is many times turned into that which is better.

8. Otherwise, if all things should of necessity come to pass as the firmamental heaven holds forth, then we would need no teaching; for that

<summary>The author declines to give a literal timeline for the end of the world, explaining that prophecy serves to warn of the 'Turba' (destruction) and encourage repentance rather than dictate an unchangeable fate.</summary>
<keywords>End of the world, prophecy, Turba, Magia, free will, Nineveh, firmamental heaven, divine mercy</keywords>

--- Page 170 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's response to the thirty-eighth question, focusing on the nature of prophecy and the protection of divine mysteries.</meta>

that would be a sure, constant, and perpetual calendar.

9. You know well what Daniel, Ezekiel, and David say in their prophecies, and especially what is said in the Revelation of Jesus Christ. In them <margin>In mystical terms, which outward reason could not comprehend.</margin> lies everything that shall hereafter come to pass; and they also spoke <gloss>in mystical terms</gloss> magically of things to come.

10. But in our writings, you have these things more clearly, for the time is now nearer to the end; and therefore it appears more plainly what shall be done at the end.

11. And please refer to our other writings, and there you shall find enough of this. For the wicked world is not worthy of an open, plain, and manifest discovery; because by such a discovery, the great Secret—which belongs only to the Children of God—would <margin>Or stirred.</margin> be <gloss>defiled</gloss>. For God will not have us cast pearls before swine, but give them to the Children for their delight; and you should do the same with this information.

12. There is no need for the Mystery to lie under worldly protection. That is a folly, and God is dishonored by it, as if he were not able to protect it himself.

13. You should not look for the Mysteries among the mighty, <margin>Or pin your faith upon their sleeves, and look for a religion from them.</margin> and <gloss>confide in them more than in others</gloss>. For the <gloss>Turba <note>original: "Turba" — a Latin term used by Boehme to describe a state of confusion, disturbance, or the "wrath-fire" that breaks forth in judgment.</note></gloss> immediately enters in with a law, and so the Spirit of God is, as it were, bound, and so an antichrist is brought forth.

14. Look at Israel: when they rejected Samuel and their judges, they thought that if their doctrine were only under a worldly arm, and if they only had a king, then they would keep their Law. But it is known how they acted: did not their kings bring in the <gloss>Turba <note>The spirit of confusion and judgment.</note></gloss> and make calves to be worshipped, and compel the people to adore their <gloss>idolatry</gloss>? This is mentioned out of my affection and goodwill toward you.

15. But we give you no specific resolution to this question; you shall find enough of it in the other questions, and we dare write no plainer.

<summary>The author explains that divine mysteries are hidden in prophecy and must remain protected from the "wicked world" and worldly authorities to avoid corruption. He warns that relying on political power to protect religion leads to spiritual bondage and idolatry, citing the history of Israel's kings as a warning.</summary>

## The thirty-ninth Question.

### *What, and where is Paradise, along with its inhabitants?*

-> *1* <-

We have also hinted in the "Enochian Life" <note>A reference to the spiritual state of Enoch, who "walked with God"; often used by Boehme to describe a life of divine contemplation.</note> that Paradise is in this world, yet it is, as it were, swallowed up in the Mystery. It is not altered in itself; it is only withdrawn from our sight and our <margin>b Property, or sensory perception.</margin> **b** source. For if our eyes were opened, we would see it.

2. Yes, God in his Trinity <note>original: "Ternary"</note> is with us; how then could Paradise be lost? We have lost its **c** source and fruit in the outward life. Just as <margin>c Property or operation.</margin> the Devil lost God when he willfully exalted himself as a haughty spirit and desired to be Lord, so it is with us.

3. When *Adam* ate of the earthly fruit of good and evil, he then also acquired an earthly life of good and evil. He was driven out of the fair Garden of Paradise (where heavenly fruit grew) and into this earthly life.

4. Many have written wonderfully strange things about Paradise, but now the image of their blindness is manifested as clear as day. Yet we do not despise them, for they were seekers. Every **d** age <margin>d Or *Seculum* (Latin: an age or era).</margin> has had its seekers who have sought the **e** Mystery <margin>e Or hidden Mystery.</margin>; but for a long time, it has been very dark in *Babel* <note>A symbolic term for the confused and divided state of the institutional church and worldly systems.</note>.

5. Now, for these last two hundred years, it has begun to be revealed. In this time, the fall of Antichrist has discovered itself, and men have begun to storm *Babel* on one side; but her strong **f** tower <margin>f Or bulwark.</margin> still stands fast. Indeed, the "Whore" <note>A biblical reference to the corrupt religious system described in Revelation.</note> has been somewhat exposed, yet her **g** beast <margin>g Worldly authority.</margin> has only grown more vigorous.

6. Therefore, there is yet a wonderful time near at hand wherein all things shall be altered. Many great mountains and hills shall be made plain, and a fountain shall flow out of *Zion*, from which the afflicted and distressed shall drink and be refreshed.

7. And they shall be guided to the fresh pasture by only one shepherd's crook, and the Shepherd shall rejoice with the sheep that God is so gracious.

8. At that time, silver and gold shall be as common as in *Solomon’s* time, and his wisdom shall govern the whole earth. This is a wonder.

<summary>The author explains that Paradise is a hidden spiritual dimension present within the world but inaccessible to fallen human senses, and predicts a coming era of spiritual enlightenment and peace.</summary>
<keywords>Paradise, Adam's Fall, Babel, Antichrist, Zion, Solomon's Wisdom, Spiritual Sight, New Era</keywords>
