# The Fortieth Question.
### Whether is Paradise mutable, and what shall it be afterwards?

1.

<margin>Mutable <note>original: "h Mutable"</note></margin>
Just as God is unchangeable, so too is Paradise unchangeable: for it is a part of the Deity. When this outward dominion <note>the physical world</note> shall pass away, in the very place where the world now stands, there will be pure Paradise. For the Earth will be of a heavenly essence, so that we shall be able to dwell anywhere and be able to pass through and through it.

2. At the Last Day, we shall not ascend above the place of this world <note>original: "i Locus universi," meaning the physical location of the universe</note>, but make our home here in our own native country. We shall enter into our home in another world, in another principle, of another property <note>original: "k Or source"</note>.

3. For there will be no cold or heat anymore, nor any night. We shall be able to pass everywhere quite through the heavenly Earth without interruption, and then it will be Paradise and the Tabernacle of God with Man. For it is written: *Behold, I make all things new—a new Heaven and a new Earth—and the old shall be no more remembered.*

4. This Earth will be like a crystalline sea, and all the wonders of the world will be seen entirely transparently <note>original: "l Or through and through perspicuously"</note>. The brightness <note>original: "m Or Glance, or Luster"</note> of God shall be its light. The Holy *Jerusalem*, the Great City of God, shall be therein, where they shall offer up the "calves of their lips" <note>a biblical metaphor for sacrifices of praise and prayer</note>. There shall the bright City of God, with its wonders and wisdom, be established; and the Temple of God, the New *Jerusalem*, shall be prepared upon the New Earth, which is adorned by the power and wonders of God.

5. Everything the Prophets have written shall be fulfilled there; for God’s word and wonders shall flourish afresh upon the New Earth, like grass.

6. There is no death anymore, nor any fear, sorrow, or sickness. There will be no superior except for Christ alone, who will dwell with us. We shall have one communion with the Angels, and we shall have fruit grow according to our desire and wish.

7. There will be no old age; instead, one who is a hundred years old will be as a newborn child, and we shall live in pure, delightful love.

8. Everything that is joyful will be sought after, and the will of everyone will be directed toward making one another rejoice.

9. We shall lead a holy, priestly life, and we shall all speak of God’s...

<summary>This page describes the transformation of the physical world into a permanent, heavenly Paradise after the Last Judgment, where the Earth becomes transparent and divine.</summary>
<keywords>Paradise, New Jerusalem, New Earth, immortality, transformation of nature, divine light, communion with angels</keywords>

--- Page 173 ---
<meta>This page concludes the answers to the forty questions regarding the soul and begins a summary appendix on the nature of the soul and its destruction.</meta>

God’s wisdom and eternal wonders: for the divine *Magic* <note>original: "Magia"; referring to the creative, supernatural power of God</note> has infinite and innumerable wonders. The more that is sought, the more there is to be found within it; and this is the increasing of the will of God.

10. To this end, God has made himself manifest in his images—namely, in angels and men—so that he might have joy in himself and eternally rejoice with the essences of his life.

->**Hallelujah.**<-

11. Thus, my beloved friend, we have set down for you, according to our gifts, a complete answer to your questions. We exhort you as a brother not to contemptuously despise us because of our simple speech and <gloss>lack of learning</gloss>.

<margin>Or unlearnedness.</margin>

12. For we are not born of academic art, but of simplicity, and we speak of great things in simple words. Take this as a singular gift from God; you shall find more in it than in the best <gloss>eloquence of the most skilled orators</gloss>, unless they also have their birth from this same school of the Spirit. In that case, we will prescribe nothing to such people, but acknowledge them as our loving brethren in Christ, with whom we have the assured hope to rejoice eternally in the heavenly school—of which we have attained a little foretaste here.

<margin>Artificially eloquent orators.</margin>

13. Yet our knowledge here is only in part; when we shall attain <gloss>perfection</gloss>, then we will say what God is and what he can do. *AMEN.*

<margin>Or the total.</margin>

->### *A Short Summary Appendix*<-
# OF THE SOUL,
## The image of the soul, and of the **TURBA**, <note>original: "TURBA"; a term used by Boehme to describe the "disturbance" or "turmoil" that destroys the divine image</note> which is the destroyer of the image.

---

->Written by the same author, *J. B.* <note>Jacob Boehme</note><-

---

*In a short summary appendix, concerning which the author has written fundamentally and at length in his other writings.*

->*I*<-

The soul is an eye in the eternal abyss, a likeness of eternity, a perfect figure and image of the first principle. It resembles God the Father in his person, in regards to the eternal nature.

<summary>Jacob Boehme concludes his answers to the forty questions by emphasizing that his simple language contains divine wisdom and begins an appendix defining the soul as an eternal image of God.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Divine Magia, Hallelujah, simplicity vs. art, Turba, Eternal Abyss, First Principle, image of God</keywords>

--- Page 174 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the soul's origin and its relationship to the divine nature, specifically focusing on its structural composition and its emergence from the "Eternal Nature."</meta>

# A short summary Appendix of the Soul.

2. The essence and substance of the soul (merely and purely as it is in itself) is, first, the "wheel of nature," consisting of the first four <margin>1. Astringent (contracting). 2. Bitter. 3. Fire. 4. Anguish.</margin> forms. <note>The "wheel of nature" refers to the author's complex system of seven properties that govern all existence; the first four represent the harsh, foundational qualities of nature before they are transformed by light.</note>

3. For the Word of the Lord <margin>Or formed, or fashioned, or created.</margin> comprised the soul by the eternal *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let it be done"—the creative command of God.</note> in the eternal will of the Father, in the center of the eternal nature. He opened it with the Holy Spirit, or blew it up like a fire (which lay hidden in eternity, and wherein all forms <margin>The soul, or the forms.</margin> of the eternal nature stood from eternity). This soul is alone known in the divine wisdom, in the divine *Magia* <note>original: *Magia*; here meaning the supernatural power or "magic" by which God manifests his will into being.</note> as a figure or image without substance.

4. Yet that <margin>Or being.</margin> thing has not been substantial, but essential, and has been known in the "principle," in the flash where the fire arises. But the shadow of it has (from eternity in a figurative image) figured itself in the desiring will of God; and has stood <margin>Or in the presence of the Trinity.</margin> before the Trinity <note>original: *Ternary*; a term for the Holy Trinity.</note> of God in the *Magia*, in the wisdom of God, as a likeness of the Holy Trinity, in which God has manifested himself as if in a glass <note>A mirror.</note>.

5. The substance and image of the soul may be compared to the earth having a beautiful flower growing out of it, and also to fire and light. We see that the earth is a <margin>Ground, foundation, or soil, or the mother of that which grows upon it.</margin> center, but has no life; yet it is essential. A beautiful flower grows out of it, which is not like the earth, nor does it have the smell or taste of the earth, much less its shape; and yet the earth is the mother of the flower.

6. And so the soul also <margin>shone.</margin> appeared out of the eternal center of nature, out of the eternal essence, by the word *Fiat* in the will of God. It was held in the *Fiat* so that it <margin>shone.</margin> appeared as a fiery eye and a likeness of the "first principle" in a creaturely form and substance.

7. And from this eye went the glance of its fire, just as light comes from fire. In this glance of its own fire, the eternal image (which is in the wisdom of God) was seen and conceived by the will of the heart of God in the "second principle." That is, it was conceived by the word *Fiat* of the second principle, in the love and power of the Holy Trinity, from which the Holy Spirit proceeds.

8. And thus the soul was a complete likeness and image of the Holy Trinity. Here we must understand the soul as the center of nature, and its fiery life as the first principle. But the sprout, or the image of the soul—which is a likeness of God—buds forth from the soul like a flower from the earth, and is embraced by the Holy Spirit; for the soul is his mansion.

9. Now if the soul directs its imagination outward from itself (we mean out from its <margin>Or property.</margin> source of fire) into the light of God, then it receives the light, just as the moon receives the reflection of the sun.

<summary>This page describes the soul as a "fiery eye" emerging from the eternal center of nature, acting as a mirror or likeness of the Holy Trinity. It uses the metaphor of a flower growing from the earth to explain how the soul's divine image buds forth from its foundational "fire."</summary>
<keywords>soul, wheel of nature, Fiat, Magia, Trinity, Holy Spirit, first principle, second principle, divine image, imagination</keywords>

--- Page 175 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the soul's nature and its position between the divine light and the fiery center of its own essence.</meta>

# A short summary Appendix of the Soul.

And so its image is in the Majesty of God, and the soul is in the Light of God; its fiery property is changed into meekness and fervent love, and then it is known to be the child of God.

10. But seeing the soul is essential, and its own substance is a desire, it is plain that it consists of two *Fiats* <note>original: "Fiats" — referring to the creative "let there be" commands of God</note>. One of them is its bodily property, and the other is the second principle proceeding from God’s will which is in the soul, in which God desires to <margin>The soul.</margin> have it as his image and likeness.

11. To which end, God’s desiring is like a *Fiat* in the center of the soul, and continually draws the will of the soul towards the Heart of God. For the <margin>Lust, pleasure, will, or desire.</margin> longing of God would have the soul; and on the contrary, the center in the power of the fire would have it.

12. For the life of the soul has its origin in the fire, and that creates the struggle for the image of the soul. Whichever of these two forms overcomes the other—whether it be the fire or the meekness of love—that will be the quality of the soul. As the quality of the soul's will is, such an image will the soul have.

13. And we must know that if the will of the soul changes itself, then its form will also be changed. For if the <margin>Quality or property.</margin> source <note>original: "source" or "Sourse" — used by Boehme to mean the active quality or "boiling" essence of a thing</note> of the soul is fiery, then it also has a fiery image.

14. But if the soul turns its imagination into the center—into the strong <margin>Or harshness.</margin> astringency <note>term: astringency — a harsh, contracting, or bitter quality</note> and bitterness—then its fair image is also captivated in the dark astringency and infected with the astringent wrath.

15. And then this wrath is a *Turba* <note>term: Turba — a Latin word meaning "turmoil" or "disturbance"; in this context, a destructive force that breaks apart divine order</note>, which possesses the image and destroys the likeness of God. For in God there is love, light, and meekness; but in this image there is darkness, astringency, and bitterness. The essential <margin>Quality or property.</margin> source is fire, proceeding from the essences of wrath. Then this image does not belong to the Kingdom of God as long as it continues in this <margin>Quality, condition, or property.</margin> source and form in the darkness.

16. Fire is a further likeness of the soul: the soul is an essential fire, and the flash of the fire is its life. The soul resembles a globe or an eye of fire.

17. The burning fire in the source signifies the first principle and the life, yet the fire is not the life itself. Rather, the spirit of the source—which arises from the fire and goes forth like a fire-like air—is the true spirit of the source of the life of the fire, which continually blows the fire up again and makes it burn.

18. Now the fire shines and gives light out of the source, and dwells in the source where it shines; yet the source does not comprehend the light. This signifies the second principle, in which God dwells.

<summary>This page describes the soul as a field of conflict between two "Fiats" or creative desires: the divine longing for love and the fiery, astringent center of self-will. The author explains that the soul's ultimate form and "image" depend entirely on which of these two principles the soul's will chooses to align with.</summary>

<keywords>soul, Fiat, image of God, fire, light, Turba, astringency, first principle, second principle, Jacob Boehme, spiritual transformation</keywords>

--- Page 176 ---

# A short summary Appendix of the Soul.

<margin>For Virtue.</margin>
19. For we know that the power is in the Light, and not in the Fire; the fire only gives essences <note>original: "Essences" — the fundamental qualities or "being" of a thing</note> to the Light. The Life, or the Light, produces meekness and substantiality—namely, water.

<margin>Or amiable, Loving.
Or pain.</margin>
20. Now we understand that there is a gentle and loving life in the Light, without pain; and yet it is itself an imperceptible source of life—it is nothing but a longing or desire of Love.

21. This source we consider a Tincture <note>original: "Tincture" — in alchemy and Boehme’s philosophy, a spiritual essence or "dye" that transforms and gives quality to matter</note>, in which budding and blossoming have their origin. Yet the Fire is the cause of it, and the meekness is the cause of its physical substance. For the Desire of Love in the Light attracts and keeps this substance so that it becomes a reality, whereas the Desire of Fire consumes substance.

22. We must also understand that (as the soul exists purely and alone in the Center) it is an Essential Fire in the Eye of Eternity; and yet that Eye desires a figure and Image of the wisdom of God.

<margin>The Word "Let there be." <note>original: "Verbum fiat"</note></margin>
23. And the Image is in its desire, in its imagination; for the word *Fiat* <note>original: "Fiat" — Latin for "let it be done," referring to the creative command of God</note> has grasped it, so that it might be a likeness of the Eternal wisdom of God. In this Image He dwells, and through it He may manifest Himself by His Spirit and whatever has been in His Eternal Counsel.

<margin>Void, or empty.</margin>
24. Thus the Majesty of God flames in the Image, within the Essential Fire, if the Essential Fire places its desire into that Majesty. But if it does not, then the Image is empty and naked without God, and the Tincture is false.

25. For the Image exists in the Tincture and has its origin in the Tincture—in the Light, not in the painful source of the Fire. Just as the Heart or Word of God has its origin in the Light of the Majesty, in the Eternal Tincture of the Father's fire, so does the Image of the soul.

26. Indeed, the Image dwells in the fire of the soul just as Light dwells in Fire, but it belongs to a different Principle, just as Light is a source of life that is different from Fire.

27. And so the true Image of God dwells in the Light of the soul's Fire. The fiery soul must create this Light in the fountain of God's Love—in His Majesty—by placing and yielding its imagination into it.

<margin>Sternness, Sharpness, or eagerness.</margin>
28. And if the soul does not do so, but instead places its imagination into itself—into its own wrathful form of the fire's source, and not into the fountain of Love or the Light of God—then its own source of bitterness, harshness, and resentment rises up. The Image of God then becomes a *Turba* <note>original: "Turba" — Latin for "turmoil" or "disturbance"; in this context, a destructive, chaotic force</note> and swallows up the likeness of God in its wrath.

29. And then the harsh *Fiat* in the fiery essence of the soul shapes an image for the soul based on the imagination that is in its will. Whatever the Essential fire of the soul desires, that [will...]

<summary>This page explains the metaphysical relationship between the soul's "fire" (its raw energy) and "light" (God's love), arguing that the soul must direct its will toward God to maintain its divine image. If the soul focuses on itself rather than God's love, it falls into a state of wrath and chaos known as the Turba.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of the soul and its formation of images based on the direction of the human will.</meta>

# A short summary Appendix of the soul.

will be figured in the soul; **namely**, earthly figures. Whatever the will of the heart casts itself into, that image the *Fiat* <note>Latin: "let it be done"; here referring to the creative or formative power within the soul</note> of the soul will make. This occurs as far as the third principle <note>The physical, material world governed by the stars and elements</note> and the spirit of the stars and elements have power.

30. So, if the will of the soul casts itself into the kingdom of this world, then the outward kingdom has the power to bring its imagination into the inward principle. If the inward *Fiat* perceives that in the fire of the soul, then the soul becomes pregnant with it and keeps it.

31. And then the soul has the image of a beast in the third principle, and that cannot be destroyed forever, unless the will of the soul returns again out of earthly lust and pierces into the love of God again. Then it receives the image of God again. This may be done only in this life, while the soul is essentially in <margin>its ground, soil, or bed of earth.</margin> its ether, during the growing of its tree; but after this life, it cannot be done.

32. Thus you may understand what the soul, spirit, image, and *Turba* <note>Latin: "turmoil" or "disturbance"; in Boehme's theology, the destructive wrath or "breaker" that destroys flawed forms</note> are. The soul dwells in itself and is an essential fire. Its image stands in itself—in the imagination within the light of the soul—if it cleaves to God. If not, then it is in anxiety in the <margin>vizard, or monster.</margin> wrath of darkness, and is an abominable image, or an image of the Devil.

33. The *Turba* of the soul, which destroys the divine image, is the essential wrathfulness. It is caused by the imagination, or <margin>or imaging.</margin> false love and representation. Therefore, everything lies in the imagination; the image consists of whatever we allow to enter into our desire.

34. It is very necessary for us to strive continually against the earthly reason of flesh and blood, and to yield the spirit of our wills into the mercy and love of God. We must always cast ourselves into the will of God and not count earthly <margin>or profit.</margin> goods and pleasure as our treasure, setting our desire upon them. To do so will destroy the image, for it is a *Turba* of the image of God and brings earthly properties into the image.

35. <margin>or to conclude.</margin> To sum up all: Christ said, "Where your treasure is, there will your heart be also." According to this, God will judge the secrets of mankind and sever the clean from the unclean. He will give that which is false to the *Turba* of the fire to be devoured, and bring the holy thing (which has entered into God) into His kingdom. AMEN.

# THE PREFACE
## TO THE
# READER
## Of these writings.

->I.<-

<margin>
a Understands, or receives not.
</margin>
It is written: *the natural man* <note>original: "Naturall man"; referring to a person living only by human reason and instinct without spiritual inspiration.</note> <sup>a</sup> *does not perceive the things of the Spirit, nor the mystery of the Kingdom of God; they are foolishness to him, neither can he know them. Therefore, I admonish and exhort the Christian lover of mysteries, if he will study these high writings—and read, search, and understand them—that he does not read them outwardly only, with sharp speculation and meditation. For in doing so, he shall remain in the outward, imaginary ground only, and obtain no more than a* <sup>b</sup> *counterfeited color of them.*
<margin>
b Or feigned shadow of them.
</margin>

2. *For a man’s own reason, without the light of God, cannot come into the ground <note>The "ground" (German: Grund) refers to the fundamental essence or underlying reality of a thing.</note> [of them]; it is impossible. Let his wit be ever so high and subtle, it apprehends only the shadow of it, as if in a glass <note>original: "Glasse"; a mirror.</note>.*

3. *For, Christ says, "Without me you can do nothing"; and he is the light of the world and the life of men.*

<margin>
c Or manifestation.
</margin>
4. *Now, if any would search the divine ground—that is,* <sup>c</sup> *the divine revelation—he must first consider within himself for what end he desires to know such things: whether he desires to practice that which he might obtain, and bestow it to the glory of God and the welfare of his neighbor; also whether he desires to die to earthliness <note>The state of being attached to worldly, material concerns.</note> and to his own will, and to live in that which he seeks and desires, and to be one spirit with it.*

<summary>The author warns that spiritual mysteries cannot be understood through human reason alone, but require divine enlightenment and a sincere, selfless motive. He emphasizes that the reader must seek to live out these truths rather than just studying them intellectually.</summary>
<keywords>natural man, divine revelation, spiritual enlightenment, reason vs. faith, mysticism, self-denial</keywords>

--- Page 182 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's introductory instructions on the spiritual disposition required to understand divine mysteries, emphasizing the necessity of self-surrender.</meta>

# The Preface.

5. *If he does not have a purpose—that if God should reveal himself and his mysteries to him, he would be one Spirit and have one will with him, and wholly resign and yield himself up to him; so that God’s Spirit might do what he pleases with him and through him, and that God might be his knowledge, will, and* <margin>Or working.</margin> *deed—then he is not yet fit for such knowledge and understanding.*

6. *For there are many who seek mysteries and hidden knowledge merely so that they might be respected and highly esteemed by the world, and for their own gain and profit. But they do not reach this foundation, where the Spirit searches all things, even the deep things of God, as it is written.* <note>Reference to 1 Corinthians 2:10.</note>

7. *It must be a totally resigned and yielded will, in which God himself searches and works, and which continually pierces into God in yielding and resigned humility, seeking nothing but his eternal native country <note>The spiritual realm or Heaven.</note> and to do his neighbor service with it. Then it may be attained. He must begin with effective repentance and amendment of life, and with prayer that his understanding might be opened from within; for then the inward will will bring itself into the outward.*

8. *But when he reads such writings and yet cannot understand them, he must not immediately throw them away and think it is impossible to understand them. No, instead he must turn his mind to God, beseeching him for grace and understanding, and read again. Then he shall see more and more in them, until at length he is drawn by the power of God into the very depth itself, and so comes into the supernatural and supersensual <note>term: beyond the reach of the physical senses</note> ground—namely, into the eternal unity of God. There he shall hear unspeakable and effective words of God which shall bring him back and outward again (by the divine effluence <note>term: a flowing out of divine energy or light</note>) to the very grossest and meanest matter of the earth, and then back and inwards to God again. Then the Spirit of God searches all things with him and through him, and so he is rightly taught and driven by God.*

9. *But since those who love these things desire a Clavis <note>original: "Clavis"; a key or explanatory guide</note>, or key to my writings,*

Some of these are taken from nature and <gloss>e</gloss> sensory experience <margin><gloss>e</gloss> From the senses <note>original: "Ex censu"</note>.</margin>; and <margin><gloss>f</gloss> Artists, or mystical authors.</margin> some are the words of strange <gloss>f</gloss> masters, which I have tested according to common sense and found them to be good and fitting.

10. *Reason will stumble when it sees heathen terms and words used in the explanation of natural things, supposing we should use nothing but Scripture phrases (or words borrowed from the Bible). But such biblical words will not always bend or adapt themselves to the fundamental exposition of the properties of nature; a man cannot express the underlying basis with them alone. Also, the wise heathens and Jews have hidden the deep basis of nature under such words, having well understood that the knowledge of nature is not for everyone, but belongs only to those whom God has chosen for it by <gloss>g</gloss> nature.* <margin><gloss>g</gloss> Naturally inclined to it.</margin>

Men must not think that this happens by chance or is done by human reason.*

I will set down here the sum of those writings, or a model or epitome of them, for the consideration and help of beginners. The further explanation of <gloss>l</gloss> it is to be found in the other books.* <margin><gloss>k</gloss> Or revelation.</margin> <margin><gloss>l</gloss> The divine manifestation, or revelation.</margin>

# The Preface.

<warning>The text on this page is heavily bled-through from the reverse side, making the primary text on this leaf nearly illegible. The transcription reflects the visible fragments of the intended text on this page.</warning>

<margin>Exodus 2:10; Acts 7:22; Hebrews 11:24.</margin>
9. *Moses* was skilled in all the learning of the *Egyptians* and was a prince in *Pharaoh's* court; yet when he became a prophet of the Lord, he was a man of a <unclear>meek</unclear> and humble spirit.

10. *Jacob Behmen* <note>original: "Jacob Behmen"; also known as Jakob Böhme (1575–1624), a German Christian mystic and theologian.</note> was a man of very humble education and lived his life working in a mechanical trade <note>original: "Mechanick Employment"; Böhme was a shoemaker by trade.</note>; yet when he became a prophet of the Lord (as he was in the highest degree), he wrote such things that the greatest scholars in the world—unless they are also disciples of Christ—are neither able to understand nor disprove. For he has laid open the very hidden things of God and the mysteries of the entire creation in a manner that has never been done by any man since the world began. And yet, he was a man of a very humble and lowly spirit, as all the children of God are and ever were.

<margin>Concerning the knowledge of God.</margin>
11. But now the world is like this: if any man wishes to speak of the knowledge of God, he must speak like the academic schools and must say exactly what they say, or else they will not accept him. And yet, for the most part, their knowledge is merely the use of the tongue and personal opinion; the true knowledge of God is as much a stranger to them as it is to the most uneducated person.

<margin>Concerning the revelation of the Spirit.</margin>
12. The revelation of divine things is opened by the immediate power of the Spirit, and not by the help of human learning, as the reader will easily discover.

13. I shall say no more of the divine author, except this: that he was a man of a very holy and blameless way of life <note>original: "Conversation"</note>. He spent the greatest part of his time in the practice of prayer and meditation, and in the writing of these books, which he did by the help of the Spirit of God, as he himself often declared.

<margin>The divine manifestation or revelation.</margin>

->JACOB BEHMEN.<-

# THE

## An Exposition of some principal Words and Matters.
## *How God is to be considered, Without Nature and Creature.*

---

->14.<-

Moses says: the Lord our God is but one only God. In another place it is said: of him, through him, and in him are all things <note>original: "ex ipso, per ipsum, et in ipso sunt omnia" (Romans 11:36)</note>. In another: do I not fill all things? And in another: through his Word are all things made, that are made. Therefore, we may say that he is the Original <note>The source or beginning</note> of all things. He is the Eternal, immeasurable unity.

15. For example: when I think what would be in the place of this world if the four Elements and the starry Firmament <note>The sky or heavens</note>, and also Nature itself should perish and cease to be—so that no Nature or Creature were to be found any more—I find there would remain this Eternal Unity from which Nature and Creature have received their origin.

16. So likewise, when I think to myself what is many hundred thousand miles above the starry Firmament, or what is in that place where

<summary>This page introduces the concept of God as the singular, eternal Unity and the source of all existence, existing independently of the physical universe and nature.</summary>
<keywords>God, Unity, Creation, Nature, Elements, Firmament, Moses, Origins of Being</keywords>

--- Page 186 ---
<meta>This page continues the exposition of the "Clavis" or Key, moving from the definition of the Eternal Unity to the manifestation of the Trinity within that Unity.</meta>

# The KEY, or Exposition of Principal Words

Where no creature exists, I find the eternal, unchangeable Unity is there. This is the only Good, which has nothing either before or after it that can add anything to it or take anything away from it, and from which this Unity could have its origin. There is neither ground, time, nor place, but there is the only eternal God—that only Good which a man cannot express.

### A Further Consideration:
### How This One God is Threefold

<margin>a. Or Triune.</margin>
17. The Holy Scripture shows us that this only God is ᵃ threefold: namely, one only threefold essence, having three manners of working, and yet is but one only essence. This may be seen in the outflowing power and virtue <note>original: "vertue"; here meaning inherent power or life-force</note> which is in all things, if anyone would but observe it. But it is especially represented to us in fire, light, and <margin>b. Subsistent forms.</margin> air, which are three several ᵇ sorts of workings, and yet exist in only one ground and substance.

18. And as we see that fire, light, and air arise from a candle (though the candle is none of the three, but the cause of them), so likewise the eternal Unity is the cause and ground of the eternal Trinity. This Trinity manifests itself from the Unity and brings itself forth in: first, Desire or Will; second, Pleasure or Delight; and third, Proceeding or Outgoing.
<margin>1. Father.
2. Son.
3. Holy Ghost.</margin>

19. The Desire or Will is the Father; that is, the stirring or manifestation of the Unity, whereby the Unity wills or desires itself.

20. The Pleasure or Delight is the Son; and is that which the Will wills and desires: namely, his love and pleasure. This may be seen at the baptism of our Lord Jesus Christ, when the Father witnessed, <margin>c. Or Love.
d. Have pleasure.</margin> saying: *"This is my ᶜ beloved Son in whom I ᵈ am well pleased; hear him."*

<margin>e. Or the impression of the will.
f. Or perception.</margin>
21. The Delight is the ᵉ impression in the Will, whereby the Will in the Unity brings itself into a place and working, with which the Will wills and works; and it is the ᶠ feeling and virtue of the Will.

22. The Will is the Father, which is the stirring desire; and the Delight is the Son, which is the virtue and the working in the Will, with which the Will works; and the Holy Ghost is the proceeding Will through the Delight of the virtue—that is, a life of the Will, and of the virtue and delight.

23. Thus there are three sorts of workings in the eternal Unity: namely, the Unity is the Will and Desire of itself; the Delight is the working substance of the Will and an eternal joy of feeling in the Will; and the Holy Ghost is the proceeding of the power. The <margin>g. Or herb.</margin> likeness of this may be seen in a ᵍ plant.

<summary>The author explains the nature of the Trinity as a threefold manifestation of the Eternal Unity, using the analogies of a candle (fire, light, air) and the psychological movements of Will, Delight, and Proceeding Power.</summary>
<keywords>Trinity, Eternal Unity, Father, Son, Holy Ghost, Will, Delight, manifestation, analogy of the candle</keywords>

--- Page 187 ---
<meta>This page continues the exposition of principal terms used in Jacob Boehme’s mystical philosophy, specifically focusing on the "Magnet" of nature and the nature of the "Eternal Word."</meta>

24. The <margin>h Or Loadstone.</margin> **h Magnet**; <note>original: "Magnet"; here referring to the attractive force of desire that draws spirit into matter.</note> namely, the Essential Desire of Nature (that is, the will of the Desire of Nature) <margin>i Or forms.</margin> **i** compresses itself into an *Ens* <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for a "being" or "entity."</note>, stone, or substance, to become a plant. In this compression of the Desire, it becomes feeling—that is, active—and in that activity, the power and virtue arise, wherein the Magnetic Desire of Nature (namely, the outflowing will of God) works in a natural way.

25. In this active feeling, the Magnetic desiring will is elevated and made joyful, and it goes forth from the working power and virtue; from this comes the growth and the smell of the plant. Thus, we see a representation of the Trinity of God in <margin>k Vegetables and animate things.</margin> all **k** growing and living things.

26. If there were not such a desiring feeling and outgoing activity of the Trinity in the Eternal Unity, the Unity would be nothing but an eternal stillness—a Nothing. There would be no Nature, nor any color, shape, or figure; likewise, there would be nothing in this world without this threefold activity—no, there could be no world at all.

### Of the Eternal Word of God.

27. The Holy Scripture says: *God has made all things by his Eternal Word*; it also says: *That Word is God, John 1*, which we understand in this way:

28. The Word is nothing else but the <margin>l Or out-speaking.</margin> **l** out-breathing will from the power and virtue: a various dividing of the Power into a multitude of powers; a distributing and outflowing of the Unity, from which knowledge arises.

29. For in only one Substance, where there is no variation or division, but is only one, there can be no knowledge; and if there were knowledge, it could know only one thing: namely, itself. But if it divides itself, then the dividing will goes into multitude and variety, and each part works within itself.

30. Yet because Unity cannot be divided and pulled apart, the separation instead exists and remains in the out-breathing will within the Unity. The separation of the breathing produces the different variety, whereby the Eternal <margin>m Father.</margin> **m** Will, together with the <margin>n Son.</margin> **n** Delight and <margin>o Holy Spirit.</margin> **o** Proceeding, enters into the <margin>p Or Science.</margin> **p** knowledge or understanding of infinite forms. This is an eternal, perceptible, active, and sensory knowledge of the Powers, where—always in the division of the will during separation—one sense or form of the will sees, feels, tastes, smells, and hears the other. Yet it is but one sensory operation: namely, the great joyous bond of Love, and the most pleasant, only Eternal <margin>q Essence or substance.</margin> **q** Being.

<summary>This page explains the "Magnet" as the attractive force of desire that gives form to nature and describes the Eternal Word as the "out-breathing" of God's unity into a variety of powers and senses.</summary>
<keywords>Magnet, Loadstone, Ens, Trinity, Eternal Word, Out-breathing, Unity, Father, Son, Holy Spirit, Science, Essence, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

<header>The KEY, or Exposition of principal words.</header>

### Of the Holy Name JEHOVAH.

<margin>Jehovah is the sensory Name of the active Deity.</margin>
<margin>Finding, Knowledge.</margin>
31. The Ancient Rabbis among the *Jews* have partly understood it: for they have said, that this Name is the Highest, and most Holy Name of God. By this, they understand the active Deity as perceived through the senses. This is true, for within this active sense lies the true life of all things in time and Eternity, in both the foundation and the Abyss <note>original: "Abysse"; referring to the bottomless void or the infinite nature of God</note>. It is God himself: namely, the Divine active perception, feeling, discovery, science, and Love. That is the true understanding within the active unity, from which the five senses of the true Life spring.

<margin>Difference, or distinction.</margin>
32. Each Letter in this Name indicates to us a peculiar virtue and operation: that is, a form within the active Power.

# J.

<margin>I, I-hood, self, or selfhood.</margin>
33. For **J.** <note>The letter J was often interchangeable with I in this period</note> is the flowing out of the Eternal indivisible Unity; or, the sweet gracefulness of the foundation of the Divine "somethingness" <note>original: "somethingnesse"; the state of having being or essence</note> or selfhood.

# E.

34. **E.** is a threefold **I.** where the Trinity encloses itself within the Unity. For the **I.** goes into **E.** and joins as **IE,** which is an out-breathing of the Unity within itself.

# H.

<margin>Or speaking.</margin>
35. **H.** is the Word, or breathing of the Trinity of God.

# O.

36. **O.** is the Circumference, or the Son of God, through whom the **IE** and the **H** (the breathing) speak outward from the concentrated Delight of the Power and virtue.

# V.

<margin>Or speaking.</margin>
37. **V.** is the joyful flowing out from the breathing; that is, the proceeding Spirit of God.

# A.

<summary>This page provides a mystical, kabbalistic breakdown of the holy name 'Jehovah,' explaining how each letter represents a specific aspect of the Trinity and the divine creative process. It portrays God as an active, sensory being whose essence flows out into the world through these linguistic forms.</summary>

<keywords>Jehovah, Rabbis, Trinity, Divine Name, Mysticism, I-hood, Holy Spirit, Son of God, Unity</keywords>

--- Page 189 ---
<meta>This page continues the exposition of principal terms used in the author’s mystical theology, specifically defining the nature of Wisdom and the Great Mystery.</meta>

# The KEY, or Exposition of principal words.

38. **A** <note>The author uses the letter 'A' here as a symbolic representation of the first manifestation.</note> is that which has proceeded from the power and virtue—namely, the Wisdom. It is a Subject of the Trinity, in which the Trinity works, and in which the Trinity is also made manifest.

39. This Name is nothing else but an out-speaking, or expression, of the threefold working of the Holy Trinity in the unity of God. Read further regarding this in the exposition of the Table of the Three Principles of the Divine Manifestation.

### *Of the Divine Wisdom.*

40. The Holy Scripture says: Wisdom is the breathing of the Divine Power, a ray and breath of the Almighty. It also says: God has made all things by his Wisdom; which we understand as follows.

41. Wisdom is the out-flowing word of the Divine power, virtue, knowledge, and holiness. It is a Subject and resemblance of the infinite and unsearchable Unity; a substance in which the Holy Spirit works, forms, and models. I mean, he forms and models the Divine understanding within Wisdom; for Wisdom is the passive element, and the Spirit of God is the active element, or the Life within her, just as the soul is in the body.

42. Wisdom is the Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"</note> of the Divine Nature, for in her the powers, colors, and virtues are made manifest. In her is the variation of power and virtue—namely, the understanding. She is the Divine understanding; that is the Divine <margin>Or Contemplation.</margin> vision, in which the Unity is manifest.

43. She is the true Divine Chaos <note>In this context, "Chaos" refers to an undifferentiated state containing all potentiality, not disorder.</note> in which all things lie; namely, a Divine Imagination, in which the <margin>Forms, or Images.</margin> *Ideas* of Angels and souls have been seen from eternity, in a Divine type and resemblance. Yet they were not then as creatures, but in a resemblance, as when a man beholds his face in a mirror. Therefore, the Angelic and human *Idea* flowed forth from Wisdom and was formed into an image, as Moses says: "God created Man in his Image." That is, he created the body and breathed into it the breath of the Divine Out-flowing <note>original: "Effluence"</note> of Divine Knowledge, from all the Three Principles of the Divine Manifestation.

### *Of the* <margin>Or Great Mystery.</margin> *Mysterium Magnum.*

44. The *Mysterium Magnum* <note>Latin: The Great Mystery</note> is a subject of Wisdom, where the breathing word, or the working, willing power of the Divine understanding, flows forth through Wisdom. In this, the unity of God also flows out together toward its manifestation.

45. For in the *Mysterium Magnum* the Eternal Nature arises; and two <margin>Essences, or Beings.</margin> substances and wills are always understood to be in the *Mysterium*.

<summary>This page defines Divine Wisdom as the passive "mirror" or substance through which the Holy Spirit works, and introduces the Mysterium Magnum as the origin of Eternal Nature.</summary>
<keywords>Divine Wisdom, Holy Trinity, Mysterium Magnum, Divine Imagination, Creation of Man, Eternal Nature, Holy Spirit</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the exposition of principal terms used in the author's mystical theology, specifically focusing on the "Mysterium Magnum."</meta>

# The KEY, or Exposition of Principal Words.

*Mysterium Magnum* <note>original: "Great Mystery"</note>: the first <gloss>c</gloss> substance is the unity of God, that is, the Divine Power and virtue, the outflowing wisdom.

46. The second substance is the separable will, which arises through the breathing and out-speaking word. This will does not have its foundation in the unity, but in the mobility of the effluence <note>The "effluence" refers to the flowing out of God's power into creation.</note> and out-breathing. This brings itself into one will and into a desire for nature: namely, into the properties as far as Fire and Light. In the Fire, the Natural Life is understood, and in the Light, the Holy Life—that is, a manifestation of the unity, by which the unity becomes a Love-Fire or Light.

47. And in this place or working, God calls himself a loving, merciful God, according to the sharpened, fiery, burning love of the <margin>Or *zealous*.</margin> unity; and an Angry <gloss>d</gloss> Jealous God, according to the fiery ground, according to the Eternal Nature.

48. The *Mysterium Magnum* <note>Great Mystery</note> is that *Chaos* <note>In this context, "Chaos" refers to a divine state of infinite potential, not mere disorder.</note> out of which Light and Darkness—that is, the foundation of Heaven and Hell—has flown from eternity and been made manifest. For that foundation which we now call Hell (being a principle of itself) is the ground and cause of the fire in the Eternal Nature. This fire in God is only a burning love; but where God is not manifested in a thing according to the unity, there is an anguishing, painful, burning fire.

49. This burning fire is but a manifestation of the Life and of <margin><gloss>e</gloss> Or *over-inflames*.</margin> the Divine Love, by which the Divine Love (namely, the unity) <gloss>e</gloss> kindles and sharpens itself for the fiery working of the Power of God.

50. This ground is called *Mysterium Magnum*, or a *Chaos*, because good and evil arise out of it: namely, Light and Darkness, Life and Death, Joy and Grief, Salvation and Damnation.

51. For it is the ground of souls and angels, and of all eternal creatures, both evil and good. It is a ground of Heaven and Hell, and also of the visible world and all that is within it. Within it, all things have lain in one single ground, just as an image lies hidden in a piece of wood before the craftsman carves it out and fashions it.

52. Yet we cannot say that the spiritual world had any beginning; rather, it has been manifested from eternity out of that *Chaos*. For the Light has shone from eternity in the Darkness, and the Darkness has not understood <note>original: "comprehended"</note> it; just as Day and Night are within one another and are two, though they exist in one.

53. I must write distinctly, as if it had a beginning, for the better consideration and understanding of the Divine ground of the Divine Manifestation. I do this to better distinguish Nature from the <margin><gloss>f</gloss> Or *Divinity*. <gloss>g</gloss> Or *Essence of all Essences is*.</margin> <gloss>f</gloss> Deity, and for the better understanding of where evil and good come from, and what the <gloss>g</gloss> Being of all Beings is.

<summary>The author defines the "Mysterium Magnum" as the eternal chaos or divine potential from which all dualities—light and dark, heaven and hell—originate. He explains that while these principles are eternal, he describes them as having a beginning to help the reader understand the distinction between God's nature and the created world.</summary>
<keywords>Mysterium Magnum, Chaos, Divine Manifestation, Dualism, Eternal Nature, Fire and Light, Unity of God</keywords>

### Of the <sup>h</sup> Center of the Eternal Nature.

<margin><sup>h</sup> Center.</margin>
54. By the word <sup>h</sup> Center, we understand the first beginning of Nature—that is, the most inward ground, wherein the <sup>i</sup> self-arisen will brings itself, through a process of reception, into <sup>k</sup> "something-ness" <note>original: "somethingnesse"; the state of becoming a distinct, objective entity</note>: namely, into a natural working. For Nature is merely a tool and instrument of God, which God’s power and virtue work with; and yet it has its own <sup>l</sup> motion, derived from the out-flowing will of God. Thus, the Center is the point or ground of its own receptivity toward becoming something; it is the source from which everything comes to be, and from which the seven Properties proceed.

<margin>
<sup>i</sup> Or self-arisen.
<sup>k</sup> I-hood, or I-ness, or self-centeredness <note>original: "owne-nesse"</note>.
<sup>l</sup> Or mobility.
</margin>

### Of the Eternal Nature and its Seven Properties.

<margin><sup>m</sup> Or separation.</margin>
55. Nature is nothing other than the properties of the receptivity of the self-arisen Desire. This Desire arises in the <sup>m</sup> variation <note>original: "diversification" or "differentiation"</note> of the Breathing Word (that is, of the Breathing Power and virtue), wherein the Properties manifest themselves into substance. This substance is called a "Natural substance," and it is not God himself.

<margin><sup>n</sup> Or thoroughly inhabits, or *totaliter* <note>Latin: totally/completely</note>.</margin>
56. For, though God dwells <sup>n</sup> through and through Nature, Nature only comprehends him as far as the unity of God yields itself into, and communicates itself with, a Natural Substance. In doing so, God makes himself substantial—namely, a substance of Light—which works by itself within Nature and pierces and penetrates Nature. Otherwise, the unity of God is incomprehensible to Nature (that is, to the desiring Receptivity).

<margin><sup>o</sup> Or consists.</margin>
57. Nature <sup>o</sup> arises in the out-flowing word of the Divine perception and knowledge; it is a continual framing and forming of "Sciences" <note>original: "Scientia"; here meaning types of knowledge or distinct forms of consciousness</note> and perception. Whatever the Word works through Wisdom, Nature frames and forms into Properties. Nature is like a carpenter who builds a house that the mind had already figured and contrived within itself; it is to be understood in this way.

<margin><sup>p</sup> Or models.</margin>
58. Whatever the Eternal mind <sup>p</sup> figures in the Eternal wisdom of God within the Divine Power, and brings into an Idea, Nature frames that into a Property.

59. Nature, in its first ground, consists of seven Properties; and these seven divide themselves into the infinite.

### The First Property.

<margin><sup>q</sup> Or Astringency.</margin>
60. The first Property is the Desire, which causes and creates <sup>q</sup> harshness, sharpness, hardness, cold, and substance.

<summary>This page defines the "Center" as the starting point of Nature and explains how the Divine Word manifests as "Natural substance" through seven distinct properties. It establishes that while God permeates Nature, Nature itself is a secondary instrument or "carpenter" that gives form to the Divine Idea.</summary>

### ->The Second Property<-

<margin>Or pricking.</margin>
61. The second property is the stirring or attraction of the desire. It creates stinging, breaking, and dividing of the hardness; it cuts asunder the attracted desire and brings it into multiplicity and variety. It is the ground of bitter pain and also the true 
<margin>Faber, or Smith.</margin>
root of life: it is the *Vulcan* <note>original: "Vulcan"; the Roman god of fire and metalworking, used here to represent the transformative power of friction and heat</note> that strikes fire.

### ->The Third Property<-

62. The third property is perceptiveness and feeling in the breaking of the harsh hardness. It is the ground of anguish and of the natural will, wherein the eternal will desires to be manifested. That is, it wills to be a fire or light (namely, a flash or shining) wherein the powers, colors, and virtues of wisdom may appear. In these first three properties consists the 
<margin>Grim, fierce, cruel, odious, or evil.</margin>
foundation of anger, of hell, and of all that is wrathful <note>original: "wrathfull"</note>.

### ->The Fourth Property<-

63. The fourth property is the fire, in which the unity appears and is seen in the light—that is, in a burning love—and the 
<margin>Operation, or property.</margin>
wrath in the essence <note>original: "Essence"</note> of fire.

### ->The Fifth Property<-

64. The fifth property is the light, with its virtue of love, in and with which the unity works in a natural substance.

### ->The Sixth Property<-

65. The sixth property is the sound, voice, or natural understanding, wherein the five senses work spiritually—that is, in an understanding natural life.

### ->The Seventh Property<-

<margin>Compass, conclusion, comprising, or container.</margin>
66. The seventh property is the subject, or the content <note>original: "Contence"</note> of the other six properties, in which they work just as the life does in the flesh. This seventh property is rightly and truly called the ground or place of nature, wherein the properties stand together in one single ground.

## ->The First SUBSTANCE in the Seven Properties<-

67. We must always understand two substances in the seven properties. We understand the first, according to the abyss <note>original: "Abysse"; the bottomless mystery of God's pre-existence</note> of these 
<margin>Essence, or substance.</margin>
properties, to be the divine being <note>original: "Being"</note>. That is, the divine will with the outflowing unity of God, which flows forth together through nature and brings itself into receptivity and sharpness, so that the eternal love may become active and sensible thereby, and that it may have something which is passive wherein it may manifest

<summary>This page defines the seven properties of nature, ranging from the initial "stinging" of desire to the "ground of nature" where all properties unite, explaining how divine love manifests through these natural forces.</summary>

When the Love manifests itself in the Light within the Fire, it then overwhelms Nature, just as the sun overwhelms a plant, or as fire overwhelms <margin>A red-hot iron.</margin> iron.

### The second SUBSTANCE.

68. The second Substance is Nature’s own Substance, which is <margin>painful.</margin> aching and passive, and is the tool and instrument of the Agent <note>The "Agent" refers to the active, divine will or the "Eternal Unity" mentioned in the next paragraph.</note>. For where there is no passivity, there is also no desire for deliverance or for something better; and where there is no desire for something better, a thing simply rests within itself.

69. And therefore the Eternal Unity brings itself, through its own flowing out and separation, into Nature, so that it may have an object in which it may manifest itself. It does this so that it may love something and be loved by something in return, so that there may be a perceiving, or a sensible working and will.

## An Explanation of the seven Properties of Nature.

### <note>The astrological symbol for Saturn (♄).</note> The first Property.

70. The first Property is a desire; like that of a <margin>Or Loadstone.</margin> magnet; namely, the compression of the will. The will desires to be something, and yet it has nothing out of which it may make something for itself. Therefore, it brings itself into a state of receptivity toward itself and compresses itself into "something." That "something" is nothing but a magnetic hunger—a harshness, like a hardness—from which hardness, cold, and physical substance arise.

71. This compression or attraction casts a shadow over itself and makes itself a darkness. This is indeed the ground <note>original: "Ground" - the foundational basis or underlying cause.</note> of the eternal and temporary darkness. At the beginning of the world, salt, stones, bones, and all such things were produced by this sharpness.

<summary>This page explains how the Eternal Unity creates a passive "Nature" to act as its mirror and object of love, and introduces the first property of nature (Saturn) as a magnetic, contractive force that creates darkness and physical matter.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Seven Properties of Nature, Saturn, Magnetism, Divine Unity, Substance, Alchemy, Metaphysics</keywords>

### The Second Property

72. The second property of the Eternal Nature arises from the first, and it is the drawing or motion within the sharpness. For while the "Magnet" <note>The first property of contraction or attraction</note> creates hardness, the motion breaks that hardness apart again; it is a continual internal struggle.

73. For that which the Desire compresses and makes into "something," the motion cuts asunder and divides, so that it takes on forms and images. Between these two properties arises the <margin>Or pain.</margin> bitter woe—that is, the sting of perception and feeling.

74. For when there is a motion within the sharpness, then the <margin>Or painful.</margin> property is aching, and this is also the cause of feeling and pain. If there were no sharpness and motion, there would be no feeling. This motion is also the foundation of the Air in the visible world, which is manifested by the Fire, as shall be mentioned hereafter.

75. Thus we understand that the Desire is the foundation of "somethingness," so that something may come out of Nothing. In this way, we may also conceive that the Desire was the beginning of this world, by which God brought all things into substance and <margin>Or Fiat.</margin> being. For the Desire is that by which God said, "*Let there be*" <note>original: "Fiat"</note>. The Desire is that "Be it" which made something where there was nothing but a Spirit. It has made the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"; the chaos or potentiality from which all things flow</note> (which is spiritual) visible and substantial, as we may see by the elements, stars, and other creatures.

76. <margin>Or stirring.</margin> The second property (that is, the motion) was in the beginning of this world the Separator or Divider of powers and virtues. By this, the Creator—namely, the will of God—brought all things out of the *Great Mystery* into form. For it is the <margin>Or images.</margin> out-flowing, movable Word by which the supernatural God made all things and brought them into form, figure, and shape.

---

<image-desc>An alchemical or astrological symbol consisting of a circle with a cross on top and a horizontal line through the cross, representing the Third Property.</image-desc>

### The Third Property

77. <margin>Or Will.</margin> The third property of the Eternal Nature is the Anguish—namely, that <term>Will</term> <note>original: "Velle"</note> which has brought itself into a state of receptivity toward Nature and "somethingness." When the individual will stands in that <margin>Or feeling.</margin> sharp motion, it enters into Anguish, which is to say, into <gloss>feeling</gloss>. Without Nature, the will is not <note>original: "feelable"</note> perceptible, but in the movable sharpness, it becomes feeling.

78. And this feeling is the cause of the Fire, and also of the mind and senses; for the individual's natural will is made volatile by it,

<summary>Boehme describes the second and third properties of nature, explaining how the tension between contraction and motion creates perception, feeling, and the physical elements of the universe.</summary>

and seeks Rest: and thus the separation of the will goes out from itself; and pierces through the Properties, from which the taste arises, so that one Property tastes and feels the other.

79. It is also the ground and cause of the Senses, in that one property penetrates into the other, and kindles the other, so that the will knows where the passivity <note>original: "passivenesse"; here meaning the capacity to be acted upon or to feel sensation</note> comes from: for, if feeling were not present, the will could know nothing of the Properties, for it would be merely alone: and thus the Will receives Nature into itself by feeling the sharp motion within itself.

80. This motion is in itself like a turning wheel; not that there is literally such a turning and winding; but it is so within the Properties; for, the Desire attracts into itself, and the Motion thrusts forwards out of itself; and so the will being in this anguish, can neither get inwards nor outwards, and yet is drawn both out of itself, and <margin>form, manner, or condition.</margin> into itself; and so it remains in such a posture, as a will moving into itself and out of itself (that is, over itself and under itself); and yet can go nowhere, but is an Anguish: and the true foundation of Hell, and of God's Anger; for this Anguish stands in the dark, sharp Motion.

81. In the Creation of the world, the Sulphur-Spirit, with the matter of the Sulphurous <margin>Or property.</margin> Nature, was produced out of this ground: which Sulphur-Spirit is the Natural Life of the earthly and Elementary Creatures.

82. The wise Heathen <note>Referring to ancient philosophers and alchemists like Hermes Trismegistus or Paracelsus.</note> have in some measure understood this ground, for they say, that in *Sulphur*, *Mercury*, and *Salt* <note>original: "Sal"</note>, all things in this world consist: wherein they have not looked upon the Matter only but upon the Spirit, from which such matter proceeds: for the ground of it consists not in literal Salt, Quicksilver, and Brimstone—they do not mean it so—but they mean the Spirit of such Properties, in which everything indeed consists, whatever lives, grows, and has being in this world, whether it be spiritual or material.

83. 84. For in the Sulphur-Spirit, wherein the fiery Life burns, the Oil lies, and the Quintessence <term>Quintessence</term><note>The "fifth essence" or purest, most concentrated essence of a substance.</note> lies in the Oil—namely, the fiery *Mercury*, which is the true Life of Nature; and which is an Effluence <note>A flowing out</note> from the word of the Divine Power and motion, wherein the ground of Heaven is understood: and in the Quintessence there lies the Tincture <term>Tincture</term><note>In alchemy, a spiritualized substance capable of transforming or "coloring" other things with its own properties.</note>, namely, the Paradisiacal ground, the out-flown word of the Divine Power and virtue, wherein the Properties lie in <margin>Temperature, or Harmony.</margin> equality.

85. Thus, by the third Property of Nature, which is the **Anguish**, we mean the sharpness and painfulness of the fire—namely, the burning and consuming. For when the will is placed into such sharpness, it will always consume the cause of that sharpness; for it always <margin>Or thongs/presses after.</margin> striveth to return to the unity of God, which is the Rest. The unity thrusts itself with its own outward flow <note>original: "Effluence"</note> toward this motion and sharpness; thus, there is a continual joining together for the manifestation of the Divine will. We always find this in these three—namely, in **Salt, Brimstone, and Oil**—which represent a Heavenly element within the Earthly. Whoever truly understands this and considers the Spirit shall find it to be so.

86. For the soul of a thing lies in the sharpness; the true life of the sensory nature and property lies in the Motion; and the powerful Spirit, which arises from the **Tincture** <note>In alchemy, the "Tincture" is the spiritual essence or soul-force that enlivens matter.</note>, lies in the **Oil** of the **Sulphur**. Thus, a Heavenly element always lies hidden in the Earthly; for the invisible <margin>Namely, the Light and dark world: God's Love and wrath.</margin> spiritual world came forth with and within the Creation.

### The fourth Property

87. The fourth Property of the Eternal Nature is the **Spiritual Fire**, wherein the Light—that is, the unity—is made manifest. For <margin>Shining, luster, or brightness.</margin> the brilliance of the fire arises and proceeds from the "out-flown" unity, which has incorporated and united itself with the Natural Desire. The burning property of fire—namely, the Heat—proceeds from the sharp, devouring nature of the first three Properties, which occurs as follows:

88. The Eternal unity (which I also call the **Liberty** in some of my writings) is the soft and still tranquility. It is lovely and like a soft, comfortable ease; it cannot be expressed how soft a tranquility exists outside of Nature within the unity of God. But the three Properties that lead to Nature are sharp, painful, and horrible.

89. The "out-flown" will consists of these three painful Properties and is produced by the Word or Divine Breathing. Since the unity is also present within them, the Will longs earnestly for the unity, and the unity longs for the "feelingness"—namely, for the fiery ground. Thus, the one longs to enter the other. When this <margin>Crashing or screeching.</margin> longing occurs, there is a "cracking" noise or a flash of lightning, similar to when we strike steel and stone together, or pour water into fire; we speak here by way of a metaphor.

90. In that Flash, the unity feels the "feelingness," and the will receives the soft, tranquil unity. Thus, the unity becomes a shining brilliance of Fire, and the fire becomes a burning Love; for <margin>Or Entity/Essence.</margin> it receives the **Ens** <note>Latin: *Ens* (being or essence). The fundamental substance of a thing.</note> and power from the soft unity. In this kindling, the darkness of the **Magnetical compressure** <note>The "magnetic" force of the first property that pulls spirit into dense matter.</note> is pierced through

He uses the alchemical trinity of Salt, Sulphur, and Oil to illustrate how the spiritual world remains hidden within the physical creation.</summary>

91. Now two Eternal Principles arise here: namely, the Darkness—which is harshness, sharpness, and pain dwelling in itself—and the feeling power and virtue of the unity in the Light. Regarding this, the Scripture says that God (that is, the Eternal unity) dwells in a Light to which no one can approach.
<margin>Or approach.</margin>

92. For in this way, the Eternal unity of God manifests itself through the Spiritual Fire, in the Light; and this Light is called Majesty. God (that is, the Supernatural unity) is the Power and virtue of it.

93. For the Spirit of this Fire receives *essence* <note>original: "Ens"; the fundamental being or spiritual substance</note> [or virtue] to shine from the unity. Otherwise, this fiery ground would be nothing but a painful, horrible hunger and a pricking desire. And it is indeed so when the will breaks itself off from the unity and chooses to live according to its own desire, as the Devils have done, and as the false soul still does.
<margin>Or Spirit.</margin>

94. And thus you may perceive two Principles here: the first is the ground of the burning of the Fire—namely, the sharp, moving, perceivable, and painful darkness in itself. The second is the Light of the Fire, in which the unity enters into mobility and Joy; for the Fire is an object of the great Love of God’s unity.

95. For in this way, the Eternal Delight becomes perceivable, and this perceiving of the unity is called Love. It is a Burning or Life within the unity of God. According to this burning of Love, God calls himself a merciful, loving God; for the unity of God loves and pierces through the aching <note>original: "painfull"</note> will of the Fire (which in the beginning arose in the breathing of the word, or the outgoing of the Divine Delight) and changes it into great Joy.
<margin>Aching.</margin>

96. And the soul of Man, as well as the Angels, stands in this fiery will of the Eternal Nature; this is their ground and Center. Therefore, if any soul breaks itself off from the Light and Love of God and enters into its own Natural Desire, then the ground of this darkness and painful property <note>original: "source"</note> will be manifested within it. This is the hellish Fire and the Anger of God when it is made manifest, as can be seen in *Lucifer*. Whatever can be thought to have a Being anywhere in the Creature, the same is likewise outside the Creature everywhere; for the Creature is nothing else but an Image and Figure of the separable and various power and virtue of the universal Being.
<margin>Or source.</margin>
<margin>Or everywhere.</margin>

97. Now understand correctly what the ground of Fire is: namely, Cold (from the compression) and heat (from the Anguish), and the Motion is the *Vulcan* <note>original: "Vulcan"; the Roman god of fire, used here to represent the active force or "striker" that ignites the fire</note>. The Fire consists of these three; but the shining of the Light arises and proceeds from the joining of the unity in the ground of Fire. And yet, the whole ground is nothing but the out-flowing will.
<margin>Or striker of fire.</margin>

98. Therefore, the life of all things consists in Fire and Light (namely, in the will of that fire), whether they are g inanimate, vegetable, or rational things. Every thing has its foundation as the Fire does, either from the Eternal—such as the soul—or from the temporary—such as astral and elementary things. ### The Fifth Property.

<margin>h The Power and Light-world.</margin>
99. Now the fifth Property is the Fire of Love, or the h world of Power and Light. This dwells within itself in the darkness, and the darkness does not comprehend it, as it is written in John, chapter 1: *The Light shines in the darkness, and the darkness did not comprehend it* <note>original: "Et lux in tenebris lucet, et tenebrae eam non comprehenderunt"</note>. Also, the Word is in the Light, and in the Word is the true understanding Life of Man, namely, the true Spirit.

100. This Fire is the true soul of Man; that is, the true Spirit which God breathed into Man for a creaturely Life.

101. You must understand that the true, desiring soul comes out of the Eternal foundation in the spiritual fire of the will. In the power and virtue of the Light is the true understanding Spirit, in which the unity of God dwells and is made manifest. As our Lord Christ says, "The Kingdom of God is within you," and Paul says, "You are the Temple of the Holy Ghost, who dwells in you." This is the place of Divine habitation and Revelation.

102. Furthermore, the soul comes to be damned in this way: when the fiery will breaks itself off from the Love and unity of God and enters into its own Natural Self-interest <note>original: "Propriety"</note>, that is, into its evil properties. This ought to be considered further.

103. O Zion <note>A reference to the true spiritual church or the state of enlightenment</note>, observe this foundation, and you are freed from Babel <note>original: "Babell"; representing spiritual confusion and the false, divided church</note>.

104. The second Principle (namely, the Angelic world and the Thrones) is meant by the fifth Property; for it is the motion of the unity, wherein all the properties of the fiery Nature burn in Love.

<margin>i Or thing.</margin>
105. An example or likeness of this i foundation may be seen in a lighted candle. The properties lie within one another in the candle, and none of them is more manifest than another until the candle is lighted. Then we find Fire, Oil, Light, Air, and water (from the Air). All the four Elements become manifest in it, which lay hidden before in only one Foundation.

106. And so likewise it must be understood to be in the Eternal Foundation; for temporary substance has flowed forth from the Eternal. Therefore, they are both of the same quality, but with this difference: one is Eternal and the other transitory; one is Spiritual and the other corporeal.

109. The first is the upward movement of the fiery will; the second is the downward movement, or sinking, of the watery spirit, namely, the meekness; and the third is the forward movement of the oily spirit, in the midst, in the center of the fiery spirit of the will. This oily spirit is the *Ens* <note>The essential substance or "being" of a thing.</note> of the unity of God, which has become a substance in the desire of Nature. Yet all is but spirit and power; it only appears this way in the form of the manifestation—not as if there were any actual severing or division, but it appears so in the manifestation.

110. This threefold manifestation is according to the Trinity; for the center wherein it exists is the only God according to his manifestation. The fiery flaming spirit of love is that which goes upwards, and the meekness which proceeds from the love is that <margin>l Or</margin> which goes downwards; and in the midst there is the center [ ^l of ] the circumference, which is the Father, or the whole God according to his manifestation.

111. And as this is to be known in the Divine manifestation, so it is also in the Eternal Nature, according to Nature’s property; for Nature is but a ^m resemblance of the Deity.

### -> ♃ The sixth Property <-

118. The sixth Property of the Eternal Nature is the sound, noise, voice, or understanding: for when the Fire flashes, all the Properties sound together. The Fire is the mouth of the Essence, the Light is the Spirit, and the sound is the understanding, wherein all the Properties understand one another.

119. According to the Manifestation of the holy Trinity, by the flowing out of the unity, this sound or voice is the Divine working word—namely, the understanding in the Eternal Nature, by which the supernatural knowledge manifests itself. But according to Nature and Creature, this sound or voice is the knowledge of God, wherein the Natural understanding knows God; for the Natural understanding is a platform, resemblance, and flowing out from the Divine understanding.

120. The five Senses lie in the Natural understanding in a Spiritual manner; and in the second Property (namely, in the motion in the fiery *Mercury*), they lie in a Natural manner.

121. The sixth Property gives understanding in the voice or <margin>Articulation <note>The distinct forming of speech or sounds.</note></margin> sound—namely, in the speaking of the word. The second property of Nature is the producer, and also the House, Tool, or Instrument of the speech or voice. In the second Property, the power and virtue is painful, but in the sixth Property, it is joyful and pleasant. The difference between the second and sixth Property is as between Light and darkness, which are in one another as fire and Light; there is no more difference between them than that.

### -> ☽ The seventh Property <-

122. The seventh Property is the substance, that is, the *subject* <note>original: "subjectum." The underlying material or foundation in which the other properties dwell.</note>

...or house of the other six, in which they all exist substantially, just as the soul exists in the body. By this we understand especially—regarding the Light-world—the Paradise or the budding of the working Power.

<margin>r Or resemblance.</margin>
123. For every Property creates for itself a Subject, or <margin>r</margin> Object, by its own effluence <note>original: "Effluence"; the flowing out of energy or substance</note>. In the seventh property, all the properties exist in a balanced temperature, as if in one single Substance. Just as they all proceeded from the unity, they all return again into one ground.

124. And though they work in different kinds and manners, yet here there is but one single substance, whose power and virtue is called Tincture <note>original: "Tincture"; in Boehme's thought, the spiritual essence or "dye" that transforms and enlivens matter</note>. That is, a holy, penetrating, growing, or springing Bud.

<margin>s Body, or Substance. <note>original: "Corpus, aut Substantia"</note>

We mean that it is so according to the power and virtue of the Divine manifestation; but as it is a Property of Nature, it is the substance of the attracted desire of all properties.

u Or Limit.</margin>
126. It is especially to be observed that the First and the seventh Property are always accounted as one; as are the second and sixth, and the third and fifth. The fourth property is only the dividing mark or <margin>u</margin> bound.

127. For, according to the manifestation of the Trinity of God, there are but three Properties of Nature. The first is the Desire, which belongs to God the Father; yet it is only a Spirit, whereas in the seventh Property the Desire is substantial.

128. The second is the Divine power and virtue, and belongs to God the Son. In the second Number it is only a Spirit, but in the sixth it is the substantial Power and virtue.

129. The third belongs to the Holy Ghost. In the beginning of the third Property it is only a fiery Spirit, but in the fifth Property the great Love is manifested within it.

<margin>x Appears.</margin>
130. Thus the effluence of the Divine Manifestation—regarding the three Properties in the first Principle before the Light <margin>x</margin>—is Natural; but in the second Principle in the Light, it is spiritual.

131. Now these are the seven Properties in one single Ground. All seven are equally Eternal without beginning. None of them can be truly called the first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, or last; for they are equally Eternal without beginning, and they also have one Eternal beginning from the unity of God.

132. We must represent this in a symbolic way so that it may be understood how one is born out of the other, the better to conceive what the Creator is, and what the Life and Substance of this world is.
