### Of the third Principle: namely, the visible world, where it came from, and what the Creator is.

->33.<-
This visible world has sprung from the spiritual world mentioned before: namely, from the out-flowing Divine power and virtue. It is a subject or object that resembles the spiritual world; the spiritual world is the inward foundation of the visible world, and the visible world exists within the spiritual.

134. The visible world is only an outflowing of the seven properties <note>The seven properties of nature according to Boehme's cosmology</note>, for it proceeded out of the six active properties; but in the seventh (which is Paradise), it is at rest. That is the eternal Sabbath of rest, in which the Divine power and virtue rests.

135. Moses says that God created Heaven and Earth, and all creatures, in six days, and rested on the seventh day, and also commanded <margin>Or, to rest on it.</margin> it to be kept as a day of rest.

136. The true meaning lies hidden and secret in those words: could He not have made all His works in one day? Furthermore, we cannot properly say there was any "day" before the Sun existed, for in the <margin>Or, the depth or vast firmament above the Moon.</margin> deep <note>original: "Deep," referring to the vast expanse of space or the abyss</note> there is only one continuous day.

137. But the meaning lies hidden in those words; by each day’s work, he understands the creation or manifestation of the seven properties. For he says, "In the beginning, God created the Heaven and the Earth."

<margin>The first day.</margin>
138. In the FIRST motion, the magnetic desire compressed and compacted the fiery and watery *Mercury* <note>In alchemy, Mercury represents the fluid, transformative principle of life and consciousness</note> along with the other properties. Then, the coarseness separated itself from the spiritual nature: the fiery part became metals, stones, and partly saltpeter (which is Earth), and the watery part became water. Then the fiery *Mercury* of this process became pure, and Moses calls it "Heaven." The Scripture says, "God dwells in Heaven," for this fiery *Mercury* is the power and virtue of the firmament—namely, an image and resemblance of the spiritual world in which God is manifested.

139. When this was done, God said, "Let there be Light." Then the inward world pushed itself out through the fiery heaven, from which a shining power and virtue arose in the fiery *Mercury*. That was the light of the outward nature within the properties, in which the <margin>Or, growing.</margin> vegetable life <note>The "vegetable life" refers to the basic biological power of growth found in plants and living things</note> consists.

### The second Day.

140. In the SECOND day’s work, God separated the watery and fiery *Mercury* from one another, and called the fiery the Firmament.

### The Third Principle

This visible world, which is produced out of the two Inward Worlds, and is Outward, in Light and Darkness, Good and Evil, mixed; and it shall have a beginning and an end.

# The KEY, or Exposition of Principal Words.

ment of Heaven, which came out of the midst of the waters, namely of *Mercury* <note>original: "viz. of Mercury"</note>, from which arose the male and female <margin>b Sex.</margin> kinds in the spirit of the outward world: that is, the male in the fiery *Mercury*, and the female in the watery.

141. This separation was made everywhere in everything, so that the fiery *Mercury* should desire and long for the watery, and the watery for the fiery. In this way, there might be a desire of love between them in the Light of Nature, from which their union arises. Therefore the fiery *Mercury*—that is, the out-flowing Word—separated itself according to both the fiery and the watery nature of the Light; from this comes both the male and female kind in all things, both animals and vegetables.

### The Third Day.

142. In the THIRD day’s work, the fiery and watery *Mercury* entered again into union or mixture and embraced one another. In this state, the saltpeter <note>original: "Salnitre"; here referring to a creative, explosive mineral force or the "Separator" in the Earth</note> brought forth grass, plants, and trees; and that was the first generation or production between male and female.

### The Fourth Day.

143. In the FOURTH day’s work, the fiery *Mercury* brought forth its fruit, namely the fifth essence <note>original: "fift Essence" or quintessence; the purest substance that permeates the four elements</note>, a higher power or virtue of life than the four elements; and yet it exists within the elements, and the stars are made of it.

144. ### The Fifth Day.

145. In the FIFTH day’s work, the <margin>e The Spirit of the world.</margin> Spirit of the World <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi"</note>, which is <margin>f Soul of the Great World.</margin> the Soul of the Great World <note>original: "Anima Macrocosmi"</note>, opened itself in the fifth essence (by which we mean the life of the fiery and watery *Mercury*). In this, God created all beasts, fishes, fowls, and worms, every one from its own specific property of the divided *Mercury*.

146. Here we see how the eternal principles have moved themselves according to evil and good, regarding all the seven properties <note>The seven qualities of nature in Boehme's theosophy</note> and their flowing out and mixture. For there are evil and good creatures created, everything according to how the *Mercury* (that is, the Separator) has figured and <margin>g Or Imaged.</margin> framed himself into a being <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for an existing thing or essence</note>, as may be seen in the evil and good creatures. And yet every kind of life has its origin in the Light of Nature—that is, in the love of nature. It is because of this that all creatures, within their own kind or property, love one another according to this out-flowing love.

### The Sixth Day

147. In the work of the SIXTH Day, God created Man; for on the sixth day, the understanding of Life opened itself out of the fiery *Mercury* <note>original: "Mercury"; here referring to the active, transformative life-force or "spirit of motion" in Behmenist alchemy</note>, that is, out of the Inward Ground.

148. God created him in his likeness out of all the three Principles <note>The three realms: the dark fire-world, the light love-world, and the outer material world</note>, and made him an Image. He breathed into him the understanding fiery *Mercury*, according to both the Inward and outward Ground (that is, according to both time and Eternity), and so he became a living, understanding soul. In this Ground of the soul, the Manifestation of the Divine Holiness moved—namely, the living, outflowing word of God—together with the Eternal knowing *Idea* <note>original: "Idea"; the divine archetype or pattern</note>, which was known from Eternity in the Divine Wisdom as a Subject or form of the Divine Imagination.

<margin>
h Or Image.
i Endowed, or invested.
k Or Contemplation.
</margin>
149. This $^h$ *Idea* becomes $^i$ clothed with the Substance of the heavenly world, and so it becomes an understanding Spirit and Temple of God; an Image of the Divine $^k$ vision. This Spirit is given to the soul as a Spouse: just as fire and Light are wedded together, so it is to be understood here.

150. This Divine Ground budded and pierced through soul and body; and this was the true Paradise in Man, which he lost by sin when the ground of the dark world, with its false Desire, gained the upper hand and dominion in him.

### The Seventh Day

151. On the SEVENTH Day, God rested from all his works which he had made, says *Moses*. Yet God needs no Rest; for he has worked from Eternity, and he is pure working Power and virtue. Therefore, the meaning and understanding here lies hidden in the Word, for *Moses* says he has commanded [us] to Rest on the seventh day.

152. The seventh day was the true Paradise (understand it spiritually); that is, the Tincture <note>original: "Tincture"; in alchemy, a spiritual essence that transforms and perfects matter</note> of the Divine Power and virtue, which is a temperament <note>A perfect balance or harmony of qualities</note>. This pierced through all the Properties and worked in the seventh—that is, in the substance of all the others.

153. The Tincture pierced through the Earth and through all Elements, and infused them all; and then Paradise was on Earth and in Man. For evil was hidden; just as the Night is hidden in the Day, so <margin>l Or grim fierceness. m Or retired.</margin> the $^l$ wrath of Nature was also hidden in the first Principle until the fall of Man. Then the Divine working, along with the Tincture, $^m$ fled into its own Principle—namely, into the Inward Ground of the Light-world.

154. For the $^l$ wrath rose up and gained predominance, and that is the Curse (where it is said) God cursed the Earth; for his cursing is to cease his influence and fly from his working. As when God's...

Power and virtue in a thing work with the Life and Spirit of that thing, and afterwards withdraw themselves along with their working; then the thing is cursed, for it works in its own will and not in God’s will.

### Of the Spirit of the World, and of the four Elements.

<margin>n Or Water.</margin>
155. We may very well observe and consider the hidden spiritual world by looking at the visible world. For we see that Fire, Light <note>original: "n Light"</note>, and Air are continually begotten in the depths of this world, and that there is no rest or stopping of this begetting. It has been so from the beginning of the world, and yet men can find no cause for it in the outward world, or tell what the basis of it should be. Reason says, "God has created it so, and therefore it continues so." This is indeed true in itself; but Reason does not know the Creator who creates in this way without ceasing. That Creator is the true <term>Archeus</term> <note>The "Archeus" is the internal "master-builder" or "separator" that directs the growth and function of a living thing.</note> or Separator <margin>o Distinguisher, or divider.</margin>, which is an outflowing from the invisible world—namely, the out-spoken Word of God. This is what I mean and understand by the term "fiery *Mercury*."

156. For whatever the invisible world is in its spiritual working—where Light and darkness exist within one another, yet one does not overcome the other—that is what the visible world is in its physical working. Whatever powers and virtues in the outflowing Word are to be understood <margin>p Or conceived.</margin> in the inward spiritual world, we understand those same things to exist in the visible world, in the stars and elements; yet they exist there in another principle of a more holy nature <margin>q Kind, quality, or condition.</margin>.

157. The four elements flow from the <term>Archeus</term> of the inward ground; that is, they come from the four properties of the Eternal Nature. In the beginning of time, they were breathed out from the inward ground and compressed and formed into a working substance and life. Therefore, the outward world is called a "Principle" and is a subject of the inward world. It is a tool and instrument of the inward Master <margin>r Artificer or workman.</margin>, which Master is the Word and power <margin>s Or virtue.</margin> of God.

158. And just as the inward divine world has within it an intellectual <margin>t Or Intellectual.</margin> life from the outflowing of divine knowledge (by which the Angels and souls are meant), so likewise the outward world has a rational life within it. This consists of the outflowing powers and virtues of the inward world. This outward rational life has no higher understanding and can reach no further than the thing in which it dwells—namely, the stars and the four elements.

159. The *Spirit of the World* <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi"</note> is hidden in the four elements, just as the soul is in the body. It is nothing else but an outflowing and working power proceeding from the Sun and stars. Its dwelling place, in which it works, is spiritual and is encompassed by the four elements.

<summary>Boehme explains that the visible world is a physical manifestation of the invisible spiritual world, driven by the 'Archeus' or the out-spoken Word of God. He defines the Spirit of the World (Spiritus Mundi) as the animating force derived from the stars that resides within the four elements.</summary>
<keywords>Archeus, Spiritus Mundi, four elements, Mercury, macrocosm, spiritual vs visible world, Jacob Boehme, divine word</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the exposition of principal terms, specifically defining the "Spiritual House" and the origins of the material world from the eternal nature.</meta>

### I.

160. The Spiritual house is, first, a sharp magnetic power and virtue, resulting from the outflowing of the Inward world, from the first property of the Eternal Nature. This is the foundation of all salt and powerful virtue, and also of all forming and physical substance.

### II.

<margin>Species, kind, or property.</margin>
161. Secondly, it is the outflowing of the Inward Motion, which has flowed out from the second form of the Eternal Nature. It consists of a fiery Nature, like a dry kind of water source, which is understood to be the foundation of all metals and stones, for they were created from that.

162. I call it the fiery *Mercury* <note>In alchemy, Mercury represents the principle of volatility, motion, and the "spirit" of a substance.</note> in the Spirit of this world, for it is the mover of all things and the separator of powers and virtues. It is the shaper of all forms and the foundation of outward life, regarding motion and physical sensation.

### III.

163. The third foundation is perception within motion and sharpness. This is a spiritual source of Sulphur <note>In alchemy, Sulphur represents the principle of combustion, soul, and growth.</note>, proceeding from the foundation of the "painful will" <note>original: "painfull will"; referring to the tension or longing within the divine nature that drives manifestation.</note> in the Inward Ground. From this, the Spirit with the five senses arises—namely, seeing, hearing, feeling, tasting, and smelling. This is the true Essential Life, through which the fire (which is the fourth form) is made manifest.

164. The ancient wise men called these three properties *Sulphur*, *Mercury*, and *Salt* <note>The *Tria Prima*, or three primary substances of Paracelsian alchemy.</note>, regarding the materials produced by them in the four Elements, into which this Spirit coagulates or makes itself substantial.

165. The four Elements also lie within this foundation and are not different or separate from it. They are only the manifestation of this spiritual foundation and serve as a dwelling place for the Spirit in which this Spirit works.

166. The Earth is the coarsest outflowing from this subtle Spirit; after the Earth, Water is the second; after Water, Air is the third; and after Air, Fire is the fourth. All these proceed from only one foundation: namely, from the *Spirit of the World* <note>original: "Spiritus mundi"</note>, which has its root in the Inward world.

167. But Reason will ask: for what purpose has the Creator made this manifestation? I answer, there is no other cause but that the spiritual world might thereby bring itself into a visible form or Image, so that the Inward powers and virtues might have a form and Image. Now, for this to happen, the spiritual substance had to bring itself into a material foundation where it could figure and form itself. There had to be such a separation so that this separated being might continually long for the first foundation again—namely, the Inward for the outward, and the outward for the Inward.

168. So also the four Elements, which are inwardly nothing but one single foundation, must long for one another, desire one another, and seek the inward foundation in one another.

<summary>This page explains the alchemical and spiritual origins of the physical world, describing how the "Spirit of the World" manifests through Salt, Mercury, and Sulphur to create the four elements. The author argues that the material world exists so that the spiritual world can take a visible, interactive form.</summary>

# The KEY, or Exposition of Principal Words

169. For the Inward Element <note>original: "Inward Element"; referring to the pure, undivided spiritual substance from which the four physical elements emerged</note> within them is divided, and the four elements are merely the properties of that divided Element. This division causes the great anxiety and desire between them; they continually strive to return into the first ground again—that is, into that one Element in which they may find rest. As the Scripture says: *Every creature groans with us and earnestly longs to be delivered from the vanity to which it is subject against its will.* <note>Romans 8:22</note>

170. In this anxiety and desire, the outflowing of the Divine power and virtue is also formed and brought into figures through the working of Nature. This occurs for the eternal glory and contemplation of Angels, Men, and all eternal creatures. We may see this clearly in all living things and also in plants: how the Divine power and virtue <margin>fashions</margin> imprints and forms itself.

171. For there is nothing substantial in this world in which the image, resemblance, and form of the inward spiritual world does not exist. This is true whether it follows the <margin>or grimness</margin> wrath of the Inward Ground, or the good virtue. And yet, in the most <margin>or poisonous</margin> venomous virtue or quality in the Inward Ground, the greatest virtue from the Inward World often lies hidden.

172. But where there is a dark life (that is, a dark oil) in a thing, there is little to be expected from it; for it is the foundation of wrath—namely, a false, bad poison—and is to be utterly rejected.

173. Yet where life consists in venom and has a light or <margin>or pain</margin> brightness shining in the oil (or in the <term>fifth essence</term> <note>original: "fift Essence"; the "quinta essentia," a pure, medicinal, or spiritual substance latent in matter</note>), there Heaven is manifested in Hell, and a great virtue lies hidden within it. This is understood by those who are "ours" <note>referring to fellow initiates or spiritual seekers</note>.

174. The whole visible world is simply a <term>spermatic</term> <note>original: "spermaticall"; meaning seminal, generative, or containing the seeds of life</note> working <margin>or substance</margin> ground. Everything has an inclination and longing toward another: the highest toward the lowest, and the lowest toward the highest. Because they are separated from one another, they embrace each other in desire through this hunger.

175. We may recognize this by the Earth, which is so very hungry for the influence and virtue of the stars and the <term>Spirit of the World</term> <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi"; the animating spirit or soul of the physical universe</note>—that is, for the Spirit from which it proceeded in the beginning—that it has no rest because of hunger. This hunger of the Earth consumes bodies so that the Spirit may be separated again from the gross elementary condition and return into its <margin>Archaeus</margin> <margin>or property</margin> <term>Archaeus</term> <note>the "Separator" or internal formative power that directs the growth and life of an organism</note> again.

176. We also see in this hunger the impregnation of the Archaeus (that is, of the Separator): how the lowest Archaeus of the Earth attracts the outermost subtle Archaeus from the constellations above the Earth. In this process, the compacted ground longs for its original source from the uppermost Archaeus and pushes itself toward the highest. In this pushing forth, the growth of metals...

177. For the *Archæus* <note>original: "Archæus"; the vital, formative principle or "master workman" of nature in Paracelsian philosophy</note> of the Earth becomes exceedingly joyful because of this, because it tastes and feels its first ground within itself again. In this joy, all things <margin>or grow</margin> spring out of the Earth. Yes, the growth of animals also consists in this: a continual conjunction of the heavenly and the earthly, in which the Divine power and virtue also work, as may be known by the tincture <note>original: "Tincture"; the essential spiritual quality or "soul" of a substance</note> of the vegetables in their inward ground.

178. Therefore Man, who is such a noble image, having his foundation in both time and eternity, should well consider himself. He should not run headlong in such blindness, seeking his native country far away from himself, when it is actually within himself—though covered by the coarseness of the elements through their strife.

179. Now, when the strife of the elements ceases through the death of the coarse body, then the spiritual man will be made manifest, whether he be born in and to light or darkness. Whichever of these two holds sway and has dominion in him, the spiritual man has his being in it eternally, whether it be in the foundation of God’s anger or in His love.

180. For the outward, visible man is not currently the image of God; he is nothing but an image of the *Archæus*. That is, he is a house (or husk) of the spiritual man, in which the spiritual man grows, just as gold does in the <margin>or drossy stone or ore</margin> coarse stone, and a plant from the wild earth. As the Scripture says: "As we have a natural body, so we have also a spiritual body." Such as the natural is, such also is the spiritual.

181. The outward, coarse body of the four elements shall not inherit the Kingdom of God, but only that which is born out of that one element—namely, out of the Divine manifestation and working.

182. For this body of the flesh and of the will of man is not it; rather, it is that which is wrought by the heavenly *Archæus* within this coarse body, for which this coarse body is a house, tool, and instrument.

183. But when the crust is taken away, then it shall appear why we have been called "men" here; and yet some of us have scarcely been beasts—nay, some far worse than beasts.

184. For we should rightly consider what the spirit of the outward world is: it is a house, husk, and instrument of the inward spiritual world which is hidden within it and works through it, and so brings itself into figures and images.

185. And thus, human reason is but a <margin>or dwelling</margin> house of the true understanding of Divine knowledge. No one should trust so much in his reason and sharp wit, for it is only the constellation <note>original: "Constellation"; here meaning the influence or arrangement of the stars</note> of the outward stars, and it seduces him rather than leading him to the unity of God.

186. Reason must wholly yield itself up to God, so that the inward...

### Now follows a short explanation, or <margin>k Formula, or Model.</margin> ^k description of the Divine Manifestation.

188. God is the eternal, immense, incomprehensible Unity which manifests itself in itself from eternity to eternity by the Trinity; and is Father, Son, and Holy Ghost, in a threefold working, as was mentioned before.

189. The first effluence <note>The flowing out or radiation</note> and manifestation of this Trinity is the Eternal Word, or the out-speaking of the divine power and virtue.

190. The first outspoken substance from that power is the Divine Wisdom; which is a substance wherein the power works.

191. Out of the Wisdom flows the power and virtue of out-breathing, and goes into separability and forming; and therein the divine power is manifest in its virtue.

192. These separable powers and virtues bring themselves into receptivity, to their own perception; and out of that perception arises one's own self-will and desire. This "own-will" is the ground of the Eternal Nature, and it brings itself, with the desire, into the properties as far as Fire.

193. In the desire is the origin of darkness: and in the fire the eternal Unity is made manifest with the light, in the fiery nature.

194. Out of this fiery property and the property of the light, the angels and souls have their origin; which is a divine manifestation.

195. The power and virtue of fire and light is called Tincture <note>The spiritual essence or "dye" that gives quality and life to a thing</note>; and the motion of this virtue is called the holy and pure Element.

196. The darkness becomes substantial in itself: and the light also becomes substantial in the fiery desire. These two make two Principles: namely, God's Anger in the darkness, and God's Love in the light. Each of them works in itself, and there is only such a difference between them as between day and night; and yet both of them have but one single ground, and the one is always a cause of the other, so that the other becomes manifest and known in it, as light is known from fire.

197. The visible world is the third Principle, that is, the third ground,

198. The inward eternal working is hidden in the visible world; it is in everything and through everything, yet it cannot be understood or grasped by any thing through that thing's own power. The outward powers and virtues are merely the passive instruments and the house in which the inward powers work.

<margin>The common creatures.</margin>
199. All other worldly creatures are composed only of the substance of the outward world. But Man is created out of both Time and Eternity, out of the Being of all Beings, and made an image of the Divine Manifestation.

200. The eternal manifestation of the Divine Light is called the Kingdom of Heaven, and the habitation of the holy angels and souls.

201. The fiery darkness is called Hell, or God’s Anger, wherein the devils dwell together with the damned souls.

202. In the space of this world, Heaven and Hell are present everywhere, but according to the Inward Ground <note>The "Inward Ground" refers to the underlying spiritual reality or essence that exists beneath the physical surface.</note>.

203. Inwardly, the Divine working is manifest in God’s children; but in the wicked, the working of the painful darkness is manifest.

204. The place of the Eternal Paradise is hidden in this world, in the Inward Ground; but it is manifest in the inward man, in whom God’s power and virtue work.

205. Of this world, only the four elements shall perish, together with the starry heaven and the earthly creatures—namely, the outward, coarse life of all things.

206. The inward power and virtue of every substance remains eternally.

### Another Exposition of the *Mysterium Magnum* <note>original: *Mysterium Magnum*; translates to "The Great Mystery," referring to the chaos or potentiality from which God created all things.</note>.
<margin>The Great Mystery.</margin>

207. God has manifested the Great Mystery out of the power and virtue of his Word. In this Great Mystery, the whole creation has existed essentially without being formed, in a state of balance <note>original: *in Temperamento*; meaning a state of perfect proportion or neutrality before differentiation.</note>. By this Mystery, He has expressed the spiritual forms in separability <gloss>or variety</gloss>. In these forms have stood the "sciences" <note>original: *Sciences*; here meaning the distinct types of knowledge, ideas, or formative powers.</note> of the powers and virtues within the Desire (that is, in the *Fiat* <note>original: *Fiat*; Latin for "let it be," referring to God's creative command.</note>). Within this state, every "science" in the desire for manifestation has brought itself into a physical substance.

208. Such a Great Mystery also lies within Man (namely, in the Image of God). It is the essential word of the power of God, according to Time and Eternity, by which the living Word of God expresses itself either in Love, or Anger, or in Fancy;

# OF THE WORD
# SCIENCE.

<margin>Science <note>original: "SCIENTZ"</note></margin>

210. The word "Science" is not used by me in the way men understand the word *Scientia* <note>Latin: knowledge</note> in the Latin tongue. I understand it to mean the true foundation according to the senses, which is missed and neglected through ignorance in Latin and all other languages. For every word—in its impression, forming, and expression—gives the true understanding of what the thing is that is so named.

211. You understand "Science" to mean some skill or knowledge; in this you speak truly, but you do not fully express the meaning.

<margin>Thought, consideration, or reasoning.</margin>
<margin>Or forming.</margin>
212. Science is the root of understanding, as it is to sensibility. It is the root of the center of the **forming** <note>original: "Impressure"</note> of "Nothing" into "Something." When the will of the Abyss <note>original: "Abysse"; the infinite, bottomless void or divine source</note> attracts itself into itself toward a center of formation (namely, toward the Word), then the true understanding arises.

213. The will exists within the "separability" <note>the capacity for the unified divine source to divide into distinct qualities</note> of Science, and there it separates itself out from the formed compaction. Men first understand "Essence" in that which is separated, in which this separability forms itself into a substance.

<margin>Essence <note>original: "ESSENTZ"</note></margin>
214. For **Essence** is a substantial power and virtue, but Science is a moving, fleeting one, like the senses; it is indeed the root of the senses.

215. Yet in the understanding, in which it is called Science, it is not the act of sensing itself, but a cause of sensing. In the same way, when the understanding forms itself in the mind, there must first be—

<summary>The author redefines "Science" not as mere academic knowledge, but as the foundational, creative "root" that allows the infinite void to take form and become sensible reality. He distinguishes between "Essence" (substantial power) and "Science" (the moving force behind the senses).</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the discussion on the nature of "Science" as the foundational root of spiritual and natural life, following the previous page's exploration of the divine will.</meta>

# Of the word SCIENCE.

...a cause which must give the Mind, from which the understanding flows forth into its contemplation. Now this Science <note>original: "Science"; here meaning "Scientia" or a foundational, innate knowledge/knowing power</note> is the root of the fiery mind, and it is, in brief, the root of all spiritual beginnings. It is the true root of souls and proceeds through every life, for it is the ground from which life comes.

216. I could not give it any other better name, for this so wholly accords and agrees with the sense: for Science is the cause that the Divine Abyssal <note>original: "Abyssall"; referring to the "Ungrund" or the bottomless, infinite nature of God</note> Will compacts and impresses itself into Nature, into the separable, various, intelligible, and perceivable life of understanding and distinction. For through the impression of Science (whereby the will attracts it into itself), the natural life and the word of every life arise originally.

217. The distinction or separation out of the fire is to be understood as follows: The eternal Science in the will of the Father draws the will (which is called Father) into itself, and shuts itself into a center of the Divine Generation of the Trinity. By this Science, it speaks itself forth into a word of understanding; and in that speaking is the separation within the Science. In every separation there is the desire for the impression of the <margin>Or out-speaking.</margin> expression. This impression is essential and is called Divine Essence.

218. From this essence, the word expresses itself in the second separation (that is, the separation of Nature). In that expression—wherein the natural will separates itself in its center into a sensing—the separation out of the fiery <margin>One copy has "Essence."</margin> Science is understood; for from there comes the soul and all angelic spirits.

219. The third separation is according to the outward nature of the expressed, formed word, wherein the bestial <note>original: "Bestiall"; referring to the animal or physical nature</note> Science lies. This may be seen in the treatise of the *Election of Grace*, which has a <margin>Acute, or sublime.</margin> sharp understanding and is one of the clearest of our writings.

# A CATALOGUE
### OF THE
# BOOKS
### WRITTEN,
## By JAKOB BÖHME. <note>original: "Jacob Behmen"</note>

<margin>1.</margin> In the year 1612, he wrote the first book called *Aurora, the Rising of the Sun*. After being accused as the author of this work, the book was confiscated and stored at the court by the Magistrate at Görlitz. He was commanded that from then on—being a simple layman—he should refrain from writing books, as it did not belong to his profession or social standing. Consequently, he did refrain for seven years. However, he was afterwards stirred up again by the Holy Spirit of God and encouraged by the requests and desires of some God-fearing people. He took up his pen again and proceeded in writing, completing the following works with careful leisure and deliberation:

<margin>2.</margin> In the year 1619, *The Second Book: Of the Three Principles*, together with an appendix concerning the *Threefold Life of Man*.

<margin>3.</margin> In the year 1620, *A Book of the Threefold Life of Man*.

<margin>4.</margin> *An Answer to the 40 Questions of the Soul*, proposed by <term>Doctor Balthasar Walter</term> <note>A physician and chemist who was a significant supporter of Böhme</note>. In the first chapter of this work is an *Exposition of the Turned Eye*, or *Philosophical Globe*; it also includes an addition concerning the Soul, the Image of the Soul, and the **Turba** <note>original Latin: "Turba," referring to a state of wrath, disturbance, or the "destroyer" that breaks the divine harmony of the soul's image</note> or the destroyer of the Image.

<margin>5.</margin> *Three Books: The first, of the Incarnation of Jesus Christ. The second, of the Suffering, Death, and Resurrection of Christ. The third, of the Tree of Faith.*

<margin>6.</margin> *A Book of Six Points.*

<margin>7.</margin> *A Book of the Heavenly and Earthly Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium"</note>.

<margin>8.</margin> *A Book of the Last Times*, addressed to P.K.
